1 
 
Foreword 
 
Welcome adventurer, 
 
Fantasy is an elemental genre: it transports us from the bindings of this 
world to a bygone place that never truly existed. For me, the experience 
of fantasy is less about what we think of now – crude escape – but the 
ability to access and experience feelings and truths that are 
fundamentally human. Fantasy intersects with this kind of game in a so 
truly complementary fashion that it seems almost by design. And it is by 
half-design that fantasy and tabletop roleplaying are so intimately 
entwined, often to the exclusion of other genres.  
 
I hope you find this roleplaying game to be a complete experience, 
allowing you to run and play fantastical campaigns for yourself and 
your friends. It is the product of many years of running games and my 
charged sense of what is fun and what we like in such games. It is my 
hope that you find within the following pages a call to valour, 
undeniable adventure and a smattering of sorcery of the darkest kind. 
 
A good fantasy campaign requires, among other things, the balance of 
action and exploration, the admixture of the genre with a director’s 
perception, and it requires your own unquenchable thirst for adventure. 
It requires, when push comes to shove, a capacity for heroism of the real 
sort. The fact that you are reading this is assurance enough and more 
on that account.  
 
G. A. Mitchell, 2025 
 
 
 
 
 
 
And here, in the dark vaults of the Dreyori, the gem 
pulsed with a dim and lurid light. It could sense the 
approach of those who would rip it from this security 
and expose it to the blinding sun once more. Entombed 
in slumbering bones, the Black Jewel would not allow 
itself to be won so easily… 
 
 
 
Excerpt from The Saga of the Black Jewel – Vol. II  
 
 
 


2 
 
Introduction
 
Welcome to Advanced Fantasy 
Journeyman, Expert, Master or (JEM for short) has been available for 
some time now, allowing game masters to create any sort of 
campaign that they like. Advanced Fantasy is here to help you craft 
a campaign within the classic worlds of fantasy adventure. Within 
this tome you find rules for creating and playing a multitude of 
fantastic characters from enigmatic elves to curmudgeonly dwarfs, 
insane alchemists and brooding barbarians. As much of the leg work 
in creating a campaign that can be done has been done – all what’s 
left now is for you to decide what parts you will use. As ever, the 
design goals of JEM remain the same. They are to: 
 
▶ Provide a classless, skills-based system that is easy to read.  
▶ Ensure a unified mechanic is used for task resolution that involves 
rolling polyhedral dice and aiming to roll high. 
▶ Ensure all character abilities, such as spells and special attacks are 
handled with a single resource pool: Fatigue Points. 
 
Dice 
The game uses several dice. They are mostly your standard 
roleplaying game dice but in case you are unfamiliar with these, 
they are explained below: 
 
▶ D3: A die whose outcome must be 1-3. Achieve this by rolling a 
d6 and halving the number. 
▶ D4: A four-sided die. 
▶ D6: A six-sided die. These are used frequently for ‘armour saves’ 
and it’s recommended each player has several to hand. 
▶ D8: An eight-sided die. 
▶ D10: A ten-sided die. 
▶ D12: A twelve-sided die.  
▶ D20: A twenty-sided die. This is by far the most common die 
used in the game. 
▶ D100: A hundred-sided die. This can be achieved by rolling 
2d10s of a different colour, with one representing the ‘tens’ and the 
other corresponding to the ‘units’. 
 
Game Master and Player Character 
One person playing the game will be the ‘game master’ (sometimes 
abbreviated to the GM). The game master runs the scenario, plays 
all the allies, and monsters and describes the world and the 
outcome of player actions. 
 
On the other hand, most people playing will be referred to as 
‘players’. Players have one ‘player character’ each, that is, a 
creature within the game world that can try and influence the 
outcome of the narrative or moment with their actions.  
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
Player Character Traits 
Core Attributes and Skills 
Each character in the game has five core attributes and 30 skills 
appropriately designed and prepared for use in Advanced Fantasy 
campaigns. 
 
Proficiency Levels 
For each core attribute and skill, a character will be one of six 
proficiency levels, known as: Untrained (0), Trained (1), 
Journeyman (2), Expert (3), Master (4) or Grandmaster (5).  
 
Untrained means the character possesses no formal education or 
even raw talent with the skill or attribute, Trained reflects 
generalised training and most skills or attributes that have any 
training, will likely be of Trained proficiency level. Journeyman is 
reflective of a skill that a character has spent some time working on. 
Expert will be reserved for just a handful of skills that someone at 
the height of their profession might have achieved. A skill of the 
Master proficiency level reflects many years of highly focused 
specialisation. Lastly, few if any skills will reach the level of 
Grandmaster, which might reflect a once in a generation level of 
competency at a craft. 
 
The target number to succeed on a skill check that a character has 
the Journeyman proficiency level for example will be the same for 
all journeyman skills. This target number is defined by the 
character’s current experience level as described later. 
 
Talents 
As well as attributes and skills, characters gain access to talents, 
starting with two at first level and unlocking more as they gain 
experience. Talents reflect specific qualities a character has, and 
some talents have requirements before they can be taken. They may 
improve skills, offer access to supernatural abilities, or reduce the 
cost of certain skill or power usages. 
 
Hit Points (HP) 
A character will have a number of hit points as determined by their 
level (this is usually 1 per level up to 9th level, but certain talents and 
attribute levels may adjust this). When a character falls to 0hp, they 
are unconscious and dying. A character naturally heals 1hp per day. 
 
Fatigue Points (FP) 
Fatigue points represent the characters own internal energy and grit. 
It is spent to perform weapon-shattering blows, accomplish various 
martial feats of arms, and cast magical spells and the like. FP 
increases as characters level up. To recover all FP, a character must 
rest for at least an hour, as part of a short rest or for eight hours as 
part of a long rest. 
 
A character may also ‘burn’ FP to increase a dice roll of a normal test 
on a 1-1 ratio. 
 
 
 


 
3 
 
Core Attributes 
In JEM each character has five core attributes which reflect their 
different central traits. These are as follows: Strength (STR); Agility 
(AGI); Endurance (END); Intellect (INT) and Personality (PER). 
 
Strength (STR) 
This reflects a character’s physical ability to bring brute force to bear. 
It is commonly used for lifting things, moving obstacles out of the 
way and other acts of physique.  
 
A character who has training in Strength gains a bonus to damage 
caused by melee attack tests, and determines the number of carry 
slots a character has, as listed on the table below:

Agility (AGI) 
This reflects a character’s hand-eye co-ordination as well as their fine 
motor skills, flexibility, and general grace. 
 
A character who has training in Agility gains a bonus to damage 
caused by weapons that require a ranged attack test. In addition, 
they gain an innate power save as a result of their lightning reflexes.

Endurance (END) 
Endurance is a character’s capacity to keep going even when things 
are difficult. It is used in hostile environments to test perseverance, 
and it directly affects a character’s ability to stay in a fight. 
 
A character who has higher Endurance benefits from increased HP 
at certain levels. If a character is entitled to bonus HP, then they gain 
these bonus hit points at levels 1, 3, 5, 7 and 9. If a character later 
gains these END bonuses, they may add them to their character 
retroactively.

Intellect (INT) 
Intellect is a character’s mental capacity for learning and articulation. 
It used for recalling information, especially data learned in a formal 
setting. 
 
A character who has higher Intellect benefits from lower XP 
requirements to level up.

Personality (PER) 
Personality is a character’s personal charisma and willpower. It 
reflects an ability to resist charms, both supernatural and mundane, 
and protects a character from many of the more deleterious forms of 
magical attack. 
 
A character who has higher Personality benefits from the ability to 
have more followers in a campaign. Followers are friends and 
retainers that will accompany a character into dangerous situations 
and adventure sites. In addition, characters with a higher PER score 
receives bonuses on Reaction Rolls.

4 
 
Creating Characters 
Creating characters for JEM: Advanced Fantasy is a step-by-step 
process. To complete this it is recommended you print the character 
sheet provided but, in a pinch, you can muddle through with some 
paper and a pencil.  
 
1. Choose a Heritage 
A character’s heritage represents their background. In Advanced 
Fantasy this determines the character’s species and perhaps a 
cultural background, such as dwarfs, elves, graylags and humans. 
 
The heritage will determine what the proficiency level of your Core 
Attributes are at the start of the game, and they may offer other 
abilities such as skill training, certain talents, and access to talent 
trees. 
 
You should also note the character’s starting languages. 
 
2. Choose an Experience Path 
There are two experience paths in Advanced Fantasy. A character’s 
experience path determines their general outlook and broadly helps 
determine how specialised they are in their trained skills. 
 
▶ Jack: The Jack experience path means the target numbers for 
untrained skills will generally be lower, representing a willingness 
to have a go at everything. However, the other proficiency levels will 
generally be higher, meaning they will never be quite as good at any 
one thing as a Specialist.  
▶ Specialist: The Specialist has very high target numbers for 
untrained skills, meaning that if they lack training in a specific area, 
it will be very hard for them to act competently. However, where 
they do have training, they will almost always outclass a Jack, even 
of the same level. 
 
3. Choose Beginning Talents 
Every character starts play with two talents. The player should 
choose these now, being careful to ensure that they don’t violate the 
restrictions of any. Talents are organised into Talent Trees, which 
can be seen later in the book. Usually, the very first talent a character 
takes will give them extra benefits. 
 
4. Note Starting Equipment 
The first talent a character takes will list some starting equipment. 
The character gains these now and should note down what they have 
received. The Equipment, Gear and Services section gives rules for 
each piece of starting equipment. 
 
5. Note Hit Points 
Note the character’s hit points, which are usually 4 at 1st level, 
depending on a character’s END and specific talents they may have 
chosen. 
 
6. Note Fatigue Points 
Note the character’s fatigue points. These are a generic resource used 
to activate special abilities such as particular combat moves or 
supernatural powers. Note that this is usually 6. 
 
7. Choose Alignment and Deity 
All characters have an alignment that broadly determines their 
outlook on life. This should be chosen now.  
 
Additionally, players should choose who their patron deity is. A list 
of generic deities is found in the Deities and Prayers section of this 
book. 
 
8. Name the Character 
All characters need a name as befits their status as adventurers.

6 
 
Creating Characters: Advanced Fantasy
Introduction 
The following information is intended to be used to create characters 
in a classic heroic fantasy game in which brave characters enter 
forgotten dungeons, slay horrid monsters and retrieve loot, saving 
the local peasants in the process. This is very much a medieval 
fantasy of games, inspired by the classic sword and sorceries of the 
likes of Michael Moorcock, Robert E. Howard, J.R.R. Tolkien, Fritz 
Leiber, and Jack Vance.  
 
Over the coming pages you will find rules for creating characters – 
their heritage and species as well as their talents, abilities, and 
possible equipment. 
 
Languages 
These are the following languages available in Advanced Fantasy: 
▶ Common, the language of most intelligent races in the setting. 
▶ Dark Speech, the language of powerful evil creatures such as 
demons and dark lords. 
▶ Draconic, the language of dragons and dragon kin. Closely related 
to the language of magic. 
▶ Dwarfish, the language of the dwarfs. This can often be found 
carved into the stones of their ancient mountain holds. 
▶ Elvish, the most common vernacular spoken by the elves. It is 
considered much more beautiful than common. 
▶ Giant, the language of giants, ogres and other large cannibalistic 
humanoids. 
▶ Gnomish, the language of gnomes. This has a more than passing 
resemblance to the secret language shared by all burrowing 
creatures like moles and badgers. 
▶ Goblinoid, the language of goblins, hobgoblins and their kin. 
▶ Grayhonk, the language of graylags. This is a loud, monosyllabic 
series of honks that graylag use to communicate. 
▶ Orcish, a similar vocabulary to goblinoid, orcish is the language 
of orcs and similar, often evil, creatures. 
▶ Undercommon, a strange dialect of common and almost an 
entirely separate language. Undercommon is the language used by 
those creatures that dwell in the deep underworld. 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
Alignment 
Alignment is a classic element in the fantasy roleplaying genre. 
Alignment reflects the moral universe in which a creature exists. All 
creatures have an alignment, and they broadly guide the players and 
game master as to the behaviour of the creature. The alignments are 
as follows: 
▶ Pure Good (PG): This is a creature that is always trying to do the 
morally correct thing. It will always protect the weak and push back 
the destructive forces of evil. Pure good means that the creature is 
inherently good, and it is generally only used in the case of things 
like angels, unicorns and other naturally saintly creatures. 
▶ Wilfully Good (WG): A creature that is wilfully good has a soul 
and can make a choice as to right and wrong. This creature generally 
chooses the morally right path. Many adventurers will be wilfully 
good, hoping to take the fight to chaotic monsters and evil overlords. 
▶ True Neutral (TN): True Neutral creatures are those that are 
incapable of moral debate or consideration. They are driven by other 
instincts such as food or survival. True neutral creatures are not 
capable of having a developed ethical sense. This will generally 
include dangerous creatures of animal intelligence, such as man-
eating sharks. It will also include creatures magically aligned to 
neutrality, such as clockwork automata. 
▶ Wilful Neutral (WN): Wilfully Neutral characters are sentient 
creatures capable of moral discussion and debate – they just choose 
to ignore it! It is the domain of thieves, petty nobles and generally 
other self-serving (but not evil) individuals. 
▶ Pure Evil (PE): Some creatures are inherently evil. They seek the 
destruction of civilisation or its subjugation on their heels. Pure evil 
creatures include vampires, zombies and other undead, as well as 
demons and devils. 
▶ Wilfully Evil (WE): Those creatures who have agency and power 
and choose to use it for their own ends. They delight in the downfall 
of others and aim to control, dominate and even kill others. Player 
characters of this ilk should not be trusted. 
 
Player Character Alignment 
When creating a character, a player will be directed to choose an 
alignment. All player characters can choose freely between three of 
the available alignments. They may be: Wilful Good, Wilful 
Neutral or Wilful Evil. 
 
A player should use their alignment as a guide as to how they play 
their characters. However, if they continuously make decisions that 
are out of alignment with their character, then the game master 
may choose to change their character’s alignment. In this way, 
alignment becomes both a guide as to how to play the character 
and a descriptor of their behaviour enacted in game. Remember, 
some character abilities like spells and talents may have limitations 
placed on them regarding a characters’ alignment. 
 


Heritages 
7 
 
Heritages 
The following heritages are available for characters within the game. 
Broadly speaking they are a character’s species and give the 
character access to certain abilities and skills. 
 
Dwarf 
A dwarf is a stout humanoid with a long beard, short temper, and a 
propensity for working with stone. They are generally stronger than 
other species and capable of resisting the inimical nature of magic. 
 
MOVEMENT AND SIZE 
▶ A dwarf has a movement of 25’ per round, 125’ per turn and 20 
miles per day. 
▶ A dwarf is of small size. 
 
STARTING CORE ATTRIBUTES: 
Dwarfs start with the following proficiency levels in their core 
attributes: 
▶ +1 Proficiency Level (Trained) in STR and PER. 
▶ +2 Proficiency Levels (Journeyman) in END. 
 
STARTING SKILLS: 
Dwarfs start with the following proficiency levels in their skills: 
▶ +1 Proficiency Level (Trained) in the Consume Alcohol skill. 
▶ +1 Proficiency Level (Trained) in the Listen skill. 
▶ +1 Proficiency Level (Trained) in the Search skill. 
▶ +1 Proficiency Level (Trained) in the Set/Remove Traps skill. 
 
STARTING LANGUAGES: 
Dwarfs start with the following languages: 
▶ Common 
▶ Dwarfish 
 
HERITAGE TALENT: INFRAVISION 
A dwarf has infravision and therefore can see in the dark up to 60’. 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
Elf 
Elves are mercurial creatures that have a propensity for magic. They 
make their homes in the depths of the woods and seldom leave to 
interfere with human matters. 
 
MOVEMENT AND SIZE 
▶ An elf has a movement of 30’ per round, 150’ per turn and 24 
miles per day. 
▶ An elf is of medium size. 
 
STARTING CORE ATTRIBUTES: 
Elves start with the following proficiency levels in their core 
attributes: 
▶ +1 Proficiency Level (Trained) in INT and PER. 
▶ +2 Proficiency Levels (Journeyman) in AGI. 
 
STARTING SKILLS: 
Elves start with the following proficiency levels in their skills: 
▶ +1 Proficiency Level (Trained) in the Listen skill. 
▶ +2 Proficiency Levels (Journeyman) in the Search skill. 
 
STARTING LANGUAGES: 
Elves start with the following languages: 
▶ Common 
▶ Elvish 
 
HERITAGE TALENT: INFRAVISION 
An elf has infravision and therefore can see in the dark up to 60’. 
 
HERITAGE TALENT: PARALYSIS IMMUNITY 
An elf is immune to magic paralysis, such as that caused by ghouls 
as well as all forms of magically induced sleep. 
 
 


Heritages 
8 
 
 
Halfling 
Halflings stand about three feet tall and generally eschew footwear. 
They are a gregarious bunch who have a propensity for large meals. 
They generally live in large, luxurious burrows beneath the surface 
of green fields but are also often found amongst humans, particularly 
as cooks and merchants. 
 
MOVEMENT AND SIZE 
▶ A halfling has a movement of 25’ per round, 125’ per turn and 20 
miles per day. 
▶ A halfling is of small size. 
 
STARTING CORE ATTRIBUTES: 
Halflings start with the following proficiency levels in their core 
attributes: 
▶ +1 Proficiency Level (Trained) in AGI and INT. 
▶ +2 Proficiency Levels (Journeyman) in PER. 
 
STARTING SKILLS: 
Halflings start with the following proficiency levels in their skills: 
 
▶ +1 Proficiency Level (Trained) in the Climb skill. 
▶ +1 Proficiency Level (Trained) in the Gossip skill. 
▶ +1 Proficiency Level (Trained) in the Haggle skill. 
▶ +1 Proficiency Level (Trained) in the Stealth skill. 
 
STARTING LANGUAGES: 
Halflings start with the following languages: 
▶ Common 
 
HERITAGE TALENT: BENEATH NOTICE 
A halfling may reroll failed Stealth tests. They must accept the 
second result. 
 
Human 
Humans are the most populous intelligent creatures in the world. 
They are versatile both in skill and demeanour. Humans are capable 
of violence, callousness, and cowardice, but also mercy, kindness, 
and altruism. 
 
MOVEMENT AND SIZE 
▶ A human has a movement of 30’ per round, 150’ per turn and 24 
miles per day. 
▶ An human is of medium size. 
 
STARTING CORE ATTRIBUTES: 
Humans start with the following proficiency levels in their core 
attributes: 
▶ +1 Proficiency Level (Trained) in PER. 
▶ +1 Proficiency Level (Trained) in any one core attribute of their 
choice. 
▶ +2 Proficiency Levels (Journeyman) in any one core attribute of 
their choice. 
 
STARTING SKILLS: 
Humans start with the following proficiency levels in their skills: 
▶ +1 Proficiency Level (Trained) in any skill of their choice from 
the Human Skill Pool below. 
▶ +1 Proficiency Level (Trained) in any skill of their choice from 
the Human Skill Pool below. 
▶ +2 Proficiency Levels (Journeyman) in any skill of their choice 
from the Human Skill Pool below. 
 
HUMAN SKILL POOL 
▶ Climb 
▶ Consume Alcohol 
▶ Gambling 
▶ Gossip 
▶ Haggle 
▶ Healing 
▶ Knowledge (any one of the following specialisms: Arcana, 
Dungeoneering, History, Nature, Religion, or a region within 
the campaign world) 
▶ Listen 
▶ Perform 
▶ Persuasion 
▶ Pray 
▶ Search 
▶ Streetwise 
 
STARTING LANGUAGES: 
Humans start with the following languages: 
▶ Common 
 
HERITAGE TALENT: RESOLUTE 
When a tie is rolled on the initiative die in combat, a character with 
this talent acts first. 
 


Heritages 
9 
 
Optional Rule: Random Heritage
It may be that a game master wishes for their players to have 
randomly assigned heritages, to encourage greater diversity of 
character types. If a player chooses to have their heritage 
randomised, they roll on the appropriate table below. If this is 
done, then the XP required to increase the character’s level is 
reduced by 10%. If they are unsatisfied with what they have rolled, 
they may roll twice more and choose from one of the three. If they 
do this, then the experience point award is reduced to 5% instead 
of 10%. Any experience point reduction stacks with experience 
point reduction awarded from INT. 
 
Random Heritage Table 
In the free edition of Advanced Fantasy there is one table used to 
determine a random character heritage – the classic table. In the 
premium edition, which contains entries for all the additional 
heritages, there is also the modern and weird heritage table. 
 
Classic Random Table 
This table is for game masters who wish to evoke the feeling of 
classic fantasy adventure games. It is a tradition that hearkens back 
to the 1970’s and 1980’s and helps enforce those sensibilities – it is 
humanocentric and involves few fantasy creatures outside of the 
genre’s foundational texts. 
 
RANDOM HERITAGE TABLE 
Heritage 
Classic 
d20 
Dwarf 
1-3 
Elf 
4-6 
Halfling 
7-10 
Human 
11-20 
 
 
Optional Rule: Choose Heritage Talents
In some cases, a game master may wish to give their players even 
more choice and customisation of their characters. Below are a 
series of talents that a character may take in exchange for one of 
their Trained starting skills: 
 
Noble Blood 
The character is of knightly, noble or even royal blood. This is 
something they make evident about themselves in either their 
utterances or demeanour. Their character may expect basic 
hospitality from peasants and other ordinary folk. This may take 
the form of being allowed to stay at an inn for free or asking a 
peasant to provide a meal for the evening.  
 
Zealot 
The character grew up within a monastery or other institution of 
organised religion. While they may not be a cleric or other form of 
ordained priest, their time in such a place has left its impact on 
them. They may expect to find succour in such places and 
generally priests of the same alignment as the character will allow 
them to sleep within their temples and will furnish them with 
simple hospitality such as a meal for the evening. 
 
Criminal Network 
The character spent much of their youth in the company and 
employ of the criminal underclass, perhaps as a member of a 
thieves’ (or even assassins’) guild. The character should be able to 
make contact with this organisation if they find themselves in a 
large city. 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
Fame 
Before taking up the adventuring life the character was already 
famous for something else. This should generally be something 
positive, such as being a well-known a tavern performer or 
sportsman within a particular kingdom. Whatever the reason, 
people generally recognise the character, especially in the more 
metropolitan places of the campaign world.  
 
Rank 
The character begins the game with a significant rank in another 
organisation. This may be something like a guild or military 
company. The exact nature of this particular heritage talent should 
be negotiated with the game master. 
 
Rich 
The character is wealthy. They start the game with an additional 
3d6 x 100gp. The source of this wealth it up to the player: it could 
be stolen; the result of prudent business; or even the result of a 
distant death and inheritance. Whatever the reason, the character is 
moneyed. 
 


Skills 
10 
 
Skill Descriptions 
Below is a detailed list of all the skill descriptions used in an 
Advanced Fantasy campaign. 
 
Animal Handling 
This skill is used to deal with animals. It can be used when driving 
horses, on precarious roads, or to calm wild beasts that might 
otherwise attack. Using this skill should offer a bonus to successive 
Reaction Rolls made for animals encountered. 
 
Armour 
This skill is used for wearing different types of armour. Different 
armours require different levels of skill proficiency and as a rule, 
heavy armour with more stopping power generally requires a greater 
level of proficiency with this skill. 
 
Climb 
Climb is used to climb up surfaces, this may be walls, rock faces, 
trees or anything else the character might think of. In combat a 
character should test their climb skill for roughly every 10’ they are 
trying to climb. Outside of combat, where the conditions are more 
optimal, a game master may choose to increase this significantly. 
 
Consume Alcohol 
Consume Alcohol is used to resist the effects of alcoholic beverages. 
It may also be used to measure a character’s ability to imbibe more 
potent brews and narcotics at the game master’s discretion. When 
drinking alcoholic beverages, characters should use the following 
rules: 
 
Each time a character drinks and alcoholic beverage, test consume 
alcohol. Once they have failed more times than their drunk limit, 
they must save vs END. On a success, they are fine but, on a failure, 
they immediately roll on the Past Your Limit table below, adding 
the number of drinks you are over your drunk limit by. 
 
Drunk Limit 
To calculate a character’s drunk limit, use the following. A 
character starts with a base drunk limit of 3 drinks, which may be 
modified by the table below: 
 
DRUNK LIMIT MODIFIERS 
Descriptor 
Modifier 
Each level of Consume Alcohol skill 
+1/ prof level 
Journeyman or better in END 
+1 
Master or better in END 
+2 
 
PAST YOUR LIMIT 
1d8 
Result 
1 
You immediately fall asleep, awake with 1d4 drained 
conditions. 
2 
You immediately buy 1d10 nearby patrons a drink. 
3 
Collapse onto the floor. You fall over, suffering 1 
bludgeoning damage. 
4 
You become angry and lash out at random nearby 
person for 1 turn, attempting to hurt them. 
5 
You vomit over yourself. Tomorrow you will awake 
with 1d4 drained conditions. 
6 
You are going to be quite badly hungover. Tomorrow 
you will awake with 1d4+1 drained conditions. 
7 
You are going to be exceptionally hungover. Tomorrow 
you will awake with 2d4 drained conditions and find 
1d20% of your carried wealth missing. 
8+ 
Oh dear. The character engages in a series of escapades. 
Every player in the group may add a detail to the story 
as to what this player gets up to while drunk for the rest 
of the night. The character awakes with 3d4 drained 
conditions and loses 1d100% of their carried wealth. 
 
 
 
 
 
 
Craft (Choose Type) 
Craft is the ability to create something material. It includes the 
training and understanding of an item’s production and its general 
quality once created. Specialisms include Alchemy, Armour, 
Blacksmithing, Brewing, Carpentry, Cookery, Jewellery, Magic 
Items, Potions, etc. 
 
Alchemy, Runes and Enchanting 
Additional rules are provided in their own section for the crafting of 
alchemical potions, dwarfish runes and other forms of magical 
weapons, armour and equipment. 
 
Disguise 
Disguise is the ability for a character to hide themselves in a false 
persona through the use of clothing, wigs and makeup. It can be 
used to fashion a completely new identity. Unless a character is 
under active scrutiny a Disguise test is only required once per week 
by a character currently in disguise. 
 
 
 
Dodge 
Dodge is a martially trained ability to avoid incoming blows. It is 
particularly useful against attacks that deal incredibly high damage 
and would otherwise be best avoided entirely, regardless of the 
armour being worn. 
 
Forage/Hunting 
This skill is used to furnish characters with food while they travel. 
Tested once per day, on a success it can provide adequate provisions 
for up to six party members. A game master should use the following 
modifiers when determining success: 
▶ Easy (+3) if the area is naturally full of game, such as farmland 
and temperate woodland. 
▶ Hard (-3) if the area is sparse such as barren hills and scrubland. 
▶ Very Hard (-6) the area is largely empty, such as high, snow-
capped mountains and deserts. 
▶ Formidable (-9) if the area is unnaturally barren such as areas of 
extreme corruption or magical wasteland. 
 
Force Doors 
This skill is used to break open locked doors, bend prison bars and 
lift gates. It is extremely useful when dungeoneering or breaking 
into and out of reinforced places such as castles. 
 
Each character may only force doors on a single door, window or 
other entryway once per turn (10 minutes). Those who fail may wait 
1 turn and try again, or may retest again immediately, at an FP cost 
determined by their proficiency level of STR. See the table below for 
this FP cost. 
 
RESTEST FORCE DOORS 
STR Proficiency 
FP Cost 
Untrained 
6FP 
Trained or Journeyman 
3FP 
Expert or Master 
2FP 
Grandmaster 
1FP 
 
 


Skills 
11 
 
Gambling 
A seedy way for a character to make a bit of extra coin in towns, the 
Gambling skill is used to play dice, card, and other wager-based 
games. 
 
Gossip 
Gossip is a catch-all skill for carousing and chatting with locals. It 
can be extremely useful for helping investigate mysteries, follow up 
rumours and find new opportunities for work. 
 
Haggle 
Haggle is the ability to discuss and negotiate sales and purchases 
with merchants. It can be useful to ensure the character receives the 
best price for treasure they are selling. When the skill is used in this 
way, it is usually an opposed haggle vs haggle test. 
 
If a character successfully haggles, then the purchase price of an 
item should be reduced by 1d10% x the character’s proficiency levels 
in haggle. The same goes for increasing the sale price of an item. 
 
Healing 
Healing is the practical knowledge of first aid and mundane surgery. 
A character may heal another character once per in-game day, 
provided that they have not been healed using this skill already, at 
the cost of 2FP. The amount of recovered HP is determined by a 
character’s proficiency level. Consult the table below for the amount 
of HP restored: 
HP RECOVERED 
Healing Proficiency 
HP 
Untrained 
+1 
Trained or Journeyman 
+1d4 
Expert or Master 
+2d4 
Grandmaster 
+3d4 
 
Any healed HP can instead be used to remove a bleeding condition, 
on a 1-1 basis. In addition, it may be used to stabilise an 
unconscious and dying character, which costs 0FP. 
 
Intimidate 
Intimidate is used to force a person into doing what the character 
wants through fear and threats. It is the domain of bullies and other 
thugs. 
 
Knowledge (Choose Type) 
Knowledge is the specific technical information of a precise field of 
study. In many ways, a Knowledge specialism could be anything 
from collectable coins to advanced metallurgy. Specialisms 
commonly include Arcana, Dungeoneering, Nature, History, Religion 
or a region within the game world. 
 
Listen 
This skill is used to listen in on conversations or through dungeon 
doors. A game master may wish to roll a character’s Listen test in 
secret, as to create uncertainty as to whether it was successful or not. 
 
Melee 
Melee is the ability to fight in hand-to-hand combat against similarly 
armed opponents. It is a core skill for adventurers who find 
themselves in fights a lot. A character’s melee skill also determines 
what type of weapons they may wield in melee combat. 
 
Perform (Choose Type) 
Perform is the ability to stage a performance in front of a group of 
people. Common specialisms include Acting, Dancing, playing an 
instrument, Singing, Storytelling, Sword-Swallowing, etc. 
 
 


Skills 
12 
 
Persuasion 
Persuasion broadly represents a character’s ability to charm, 
influence or otherwise convince a person into doing what the 
character wants them to do. It might be to waive a toll fee or 
convince a lord to evacuate his people in the face of imminent 
danger. 
 
Pray 
Pray is the willingness and ability to open up spiritually to the gods 
in order to seek and divine their wisdom. A pray test may be used 
by a character to gain an insight from their patron deity about one 
thing that troubles them.  
 
The Deities and Prayers section of this book details praying in more 
detail. 
 
Ranged 
Ranged is the ability to use missile weapons such as bows, 
crossbows and throwing daggers against foes. 
 
Search 
This skill is used to adequately search a location for a specific object, 
such as a secret door or treasure. It can be used once per turn (10 
minutes) to search a 10’ x 10’ area thoroughly. A game master may 
wish to roll a character’s Search test in secret, as to create 
uncertainty as to whether it was successful or not. 
 
Set/Remove Traps 
This is skill of laying of traps. It might involve the literal creation of 
mechanical traps to help a character hunt, but it might also be used 
to ask the game master whether an ambush the party has formulated 
has any obvious flaws. In addition, it used to remove and disable 
mechanical traps without dangerously springing them. 
 
Shield 
This skill is the ability to block incoming blows with functional 
devices such as buckler and heater shields. Some talents allow a 
character with this skill to bash an opponent or perform other tricks 
and feats. 
 
Sleight of Hand 
This allows a character to pick a creature’s pockets, unlock doors 
with thieves’ tools and otherwise perform dextrous activities with 
their hands.  
 
Spellcasting 
Spellcasting is the ability of a character to cast spells and is to 
wizards what the melee skill is to fighters. A higher proficiency in 
spellcasting generally allows a character to cast higher level spell as 
well as casting more consistently. The Magic and Spells section of 
this book details spellcasting in more detail. 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
Stealth 
Stealth allows a character to slip from view of most enemies, 
meaning they can move or attack stealthily. Attacks may reveal the 
character at the discretion of the game master. 
 
Streetwise 
Streetwise is the ability to navigate through urban environments. It 
might be used to find the way to a tavern, negotiate with cutpurses 
for example. 
 
 
 
Survival 
Survival is the ability to eke out an existence in inhospitable places. 
It will never furnish a character with food but might prevent them 
from freezing to death or dying of thirst. It can be used to scout a 
suitable place to rest.  
 
Tracking 
This is the ability to track down enemies and search them out. 
Depending on the size of the animal or group being tracked, the 
game master should offer certain situational bonuses or penalties. 


 
13 
 
Talents
Talents are organised into Talent Trees, as seen over the coming 
pages. A character must always start at the bottom of a Talent Tree, 
with the talent marked STARTING. 
 
When they gain additional talents, they may choose these from any 
of the available talents on their Talent Tree. For example, if a 
character already has Arcane Magic Initiate from Class: Wizard Talent 
Tree and they wish to progress up this tree, then they must take the 
next available talent, which is Expended Spell Book. Some talents 
lead to multiple others, in which case a character simply chooses 
one of the available ones. A character does not need every talent in 
a branch or a tree before they are able to switch to a new one. If a 
character follows a particular ‘path’ up a talent tree, they are free to 
then follow other ‘paths’ up the tree that they had previously not 
engaged with. 
 
Whenever a character may choose a talent, it can be from any of 
Talent Tree, always beginning at the bottom. This may mean a 
character has talents from multiple Talent Trees. The Talent Trees 
are detailed below: 
 
Heritage: Dwarf 
This pathway is available for dwarfish characters and focuses on that 
race’s strong physicality and enduring craftsmanship. It has a minor 
focus on combat too as all dwarfs are expected to defend their hold 
from time to time. 
 
Heritage: Elf 
This pathway is available for elven characters and allows them to 
unlock their heritage and culture as near immortals. Within this 
talent tree there is a strong focus on nature and performance skills. 
 
Heritage: Halfling 
This pathway is available for characters who are halflings. It focuses 
on that species’ love of food and cookery as well as their own 
understated and often forgotten sense of heroism.  
 
Heritage: Human 
This pathway is available for characters who are humans. Humans 
are the youngest of the races and the most adaptable. This skill tree 
focuses on their versatility and varied nature. 
 
Class: Cleric 
This talent tree is for those individuals who wish to mete out 
righteous justice to the foes of their gods. It’s a cleric’s job to keep 
the party alive and to maintain their allies’ bodies and minds.  
 
Class: Fighter 
This talent tree is one of martial combat. The pathway is for those 
who wish to don heavier armour, wield larger weapons and tear 
their way through inimical hordes and monstrous foes. 
 
Class: Thief 
This talent tree is for characters who wish to specialise in subterfuge 
and deceit. Thieves can climb walls, pick locks, assassinate enemies, 
and disable traps in dungeons. 
 
Class: Wizard 
This talent tree is all about magic. Characters who tread the path 
down this talent tree will find themselves becoming more and more 
powerful in the magical arts and forgotten lore. 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
Optional Rule: Restricted Talent Trees 
Class Restriction by Heritage 
It may be that a game master wishes to restrict certain class talent 
trees to certain heritages and they are within their rights to dictate 
this in their campaign. The reasons for this are clear: certain 
heritages have a greater proclivity and leaning towards particular 
professions, either culturally or relating to their species (a halfling 
for example, may be less inclined to be a fighter due to their short 
stature in relation to many other creatures). By restricting what 
talent trees can be pursued by a heritage a game master is able to 
reinforce elements of the heroic genre as well as place a premium 
value on a player’s choice of heritage for their character. 
 
CLASS RESTRICTION BY HERITAGE

Restriction by Mutually Exclusive Class 
If a game master uses this optional rule, then once a character has 
started to take talents from one particular talent tree, they may not 
take them from certain other talent trees. In this way, the game 
master ensures that each character could become a specialist in 
their area of expertise. Similar to Restriction by Heritage, this rule 
allows a game master to reinforce some elements of the genre by, 
for instance, stopping a wizard from becoming just as proficient 
with a sword as a fighter. Enforcement of this restriction is 
completely at the discretion of the game master.

Advanced Fantasy: Talents 
 
20 
 
Talent Descriptions 
Below is a detailed list of all the talents available to player characters. 
All talents are either passive, meaning they are always working or 
an active, which means they require the character to activate them. 
If they are active, the talent will list which step in combat they may 
be activated. 
 
Accurate Shot 
ACTIVE (MISSILE ATTACKS STEP) 
▶ When the character makes an attack test with the Ranged skill, 
they may choose to spend 1FP to gain a +3 bonus to their Ranged 
skill test. They may spend this after having rolled the test. 
▶ The character also gains +1 proficiency level in any skill from the 
Skill Pool of the talent tree that they chose this talent from. 
 
Accurate Strike 
ACTIVE (MELEE ATTACKS STEP) 
▶ When the character makes an attack test with the Melee skill, 
they may choose to spend 1FP to gain a +3 bonus to their Melee 
skill test. They may spend this after having rolled the test. 
▶ The character also gains +1 proficiency level in any skill from the 
Skill Pool of the talent tree that they chose this talent from. 
 
Adaptable 
PASSIVE 
▶ The character gains +1 proficiency level in any one skill. This 
bonus cannot take the skill above Master level. If the character 
already has the skill at Master level or better, then the character 
instead gains +1 proficiency level in any skill from the Heritage: 
Human Skill Pool. 
 
Advanced Memorisation 
PASSIVE 
▶ When casting all 2nd and 3rd level spells, the caster can ignore the 
‘forgotten’ entries on the casting tables. 
▶ The character also gains +1 proficiency level in any skill from the 
Skill Pool of the talent tree that they chose this talent from. 
 
Ageless Grace 
PASSIVE 
▶ The character may spend 1FP to not provoke an attack of 
opportunity. 
▶ The character also gains +1 proficiency level in any skill from the 
Skill Pool of the talent tree that they chose this talent from. 
 
Alchemical Initiate 
PASSIVE 
▶ The character may now craft alchemical items using the Craft 
(Alchemy) skill. 
 
▶ The character learns two formulae of their choice now. Formulae 
learnt this way must be recorded in a formula book. They are added 
to this book now. A list of such formulae can be found in Appendix 
Table 2A: Trained Craft (Alchemy) Items. 
 
▶ The character also gains +1 proficiency level in the Craft 
(Alchemy) skill. If the character already has Trained or better in the 
Spellcasting skill, then they gain +1 proficiency level in any skill 
from the Skill Pool of the talent tree that they chose this talent from 
instead. 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
Alchemical Journeyman 
PASSIVE 
▶ The character gains +1 proficiency level in the Craft (Alchemy) 
skill. If the character already has Journeyman or better in the Craft 
(Alchemy) skill, then they gain +1 proficiency level in any skill 
from the Skill Pool of the talent tree that they chose this talent from 
instead. 
▶ The character learns two Journeyman level formulae to add to 
their formula book now. A list of such formulae can be found in 
Appendix Table 2B: Journeyman Craft (Alchemy) Items. 
 
Antediluvial Traditions 
ACTIVE (SUPERNATURAL ABILITIES STEP) 
▶ The character may spend 4FP to speak with a tree for 1 turn (10 
minutes). The tree must be of deciduous hardwood, such as alder, 
ash, elm, oak or rowan. 
▶ The character gains +1 proficiency level in Craft (Poetry). 
▶ The character gains +1 proficiency level in Knowledge (Nature). 
▶ The character also gains +1 proficiency level in any skill from the 
Skill Pool of the talent tree that they chose this talent from. 
 
Arcane 1st Level Upcasting 
PASSIVE 
▶ The character may now spend the listed FP to upcast spells of 1st 
level from the arcane sphere. 
▶ The character also gains +1 proficiency level in any skill from the 
Skill Pool of the talent tree that they chose this talent from. 
 
Arcane 2nd Level Upcasting 
PASSIVE 
▶ The character may now spend the listed FP to upcast spells of 2nd 
level from the arcane sphere. 
▶ The character also gains +1 proficiency level in any skill from the 
Skill Pool of the talent tree that they chose this talent from. 
 
Arcane 3rd Level Upcasting 
PASSIVE 
▶ The character may now spend the listed FP to upcast spells of 3rd 
level from the arcane sphere. 
▶ The character also gains +1 proficiency level in any skill from the 
Skill Pool of the talent tree that they chose this talent from. 
 
Arcane 4th Level Upcasting 
PASSIVE 
▶ The character may now spend the listed FP to upcast spells of 4th 
level from the arcane sphere. 
▶ The character also gains +1 proficiency level in any skill from the 
Skill Pool of the talent tree that they chose this talent from. 
 
Arcane Conventions 
PASSIVE 
▶ The character chooses one spell school (such as Destruction, 
Necromancy or Illusion). Spells of that school cost one FP fewer to 
cast (minimum 0).

Assassinate 
ACTIVE (MELEE ATTACKS STEP) 
▶ When the character makes an attack test with the Melee skill, 
they may choose to spend 4FP to perform an assassination. 
▶ If a creature is subject to an assassination and the creature’s level 
is half or less than that of the character then the target creature must 
immediately save vs AGI or die instantly. This can only be 
performed on creatures of medium size or smaller. If the creature’s 
level is greater than half of the character or the creature succeeds 
save vs AGI, then the target creature only takes normal weapon 
damage. 
▶ The character also gains +1 proficiency level in any skill from the 
Skill Pool of the talent tree that they chose this talent from. 
 
Assess For Weakness 
ACTIVE (OTHER ACTIONS STEP) 
▶ The character can use their action to spend 1FP to test INT. On a 
success, the GM should tell the player one of the following about a 
creature they are currently fighting: Remaining Hit Points; Armour 
saves; or any other obvious weakness (such as trolls being allergic 
to fire). 
▶ The character also gains +1 proficiency level in any skill from the 
Skill Pool of the talent tree that they chose this talent from.

PASSIVE 
▶ The character may spend 6FP to automatically avoid damage from

Banish Demons 
ACTIVE (SUPERNATURAL ABILITIES STEP) 
▶ The user makes a Pray test. On a success, the character spends 
5FP and generates a number of banish points equal to their level. 
They assign these to demonic creatures they can see. If they spend 
a number of points equal to a creature’s level, the creature is 
banished. If they spend a number of points equal to double a 
creature’s level, it turns to ash. Unspent banish points are 
immediately wasted. 
▶ Banished creatures attempt to flee the user of this ability and make 
no attacks. They do this for 1 turn (10 minutes). 
▶ The character also gains +1 proficiency level in any skill from the 
Skill Pool of the talent tree that they chose this talent from.

ACTIVE (SUPERNATURAL ABILITIES STEP) 
▶ The user makes a Pray test. On a success, the character spends 
3FP and generates a number of banish points equal to their level.

number of points equal to a creature’s level, the creature is banished. 
If they spend a number of points equal to double a creature’s level, 
it turns to ash. Unspent banish points are immediately wasted. 
▶ Banished creatures attempt to flee the user of this ability and make

▶ The character also gains +1 proficiency level in any skill from the

▶ The character gains +1 proficiency level in the Armour skill. If 
the character already has Expert or better in the Armour skill, then 
they gain +1 proficiency level in any skill from the Skill Pool of 
the talent tree that they chose this talent from instead. 
 
Hero of Legend 
ACTIVE (MELEE ATTACKS STEP) 
▶ The character may spend 1FP to automatically succeed a melee 
attack test against a creature of a level less than half of this 
character. 
▶ The character also gains +1 proficiency level in any skill from the 
Skill Pool of the talent tree that they chose this talent from. 
 
Requirements and Restrictions 
A character must be of at least 8th level to take this talent. 
 
Hero of Myth 
ACTIVE (MELEE ATTACKS STEP) 
▶ In addition to their usual action, a character may spend 1FP to 
make a free melee attack test against any adjacent creature whose 
level is half or lower than that of the character’s. They may do this 
any number of times per round, but only once to each adjacent 
creature, spending 1FP each time. 
▶ The character also gains +1 proficiency level in any skill from the 
Skill Pool of the talent tree that they chose this talent from. 
 
Requirements and Restrictions 
A character must be of at least 12th level to take this talent. 
 
Holy Sensitivity 
ACTIVE (OTHER ACTIONS STEP) 
▶ A character may spend 2FP to immediately learn if they stand in 
holy or unholy ground. They may also spend an additional 2FP to 
learn what god the area is sacred to (if at all). 
▶ The character also gains +1 proficiency level in any skill from the 
Skill Pool of the talent tree that they chose this talent from. 
 
Holy Symbol 
PASSIVE 
▶ The character can now make use of a spellcasting focus when 
casting spells of the Divine sphere. This is a handheld, carried object, 
such as a rod, sceptre or holy cross agreed upon by the game master 
and the player. So long as the character has their spellcasting focus 
in their hand, they may ignore material spell components that do not 
list a cost in the spell description. 
▶ The character also gains +1 proficiency level in any skill from the 
Skill Pool of the talent tree that they chose this talent from. 
Home Comforts 
PASSIVE 
▶ Provided that the character has eaten, they may gain a +1 hit point 
for natural healing at the end of an extended rest. 
▶ The character also gains +1 proficiency level in the Craft 
(Cookery) skill. 
▶ The character also gains +1 proficiency level in any skill from the 
Skill Pool of the talent tree that they chose this talent from. 
 
Human Resolve 
PASSIVE 
▶ The character gains a permanent +3FP. 
▶ The character also gains +1 proficiency level in any skill from the 
Skill Pool of the talent tree that they chose this talent from. 
 
Intercultural Learning 
PASSIVE 
▶ The character gains +1 proficiency levels in any skill from either 
the Heritage: Elf Skill Pool, The Heritage: Dwarf Skill Pool, or 
the Heritage Halfling Skill Pool. 
▶ The character also gains two of the following languages: Elvish, 
Dwarfish, Gnomish or Goblinoid. 
Intuitive Elocution 
PASSIVE 
▶ The FP cost of all level 1 and 2 Arcane sphere spells is reduced 
by 2 (to a minimum of 0). 
▶ The character learns one spell of their choice from the Arcane 
Spell list of a level they have the talent for. 
▶ The character also gains +1 proficiency level in any skill from the 
Skill Pool of the talent tree that they chose this talent from. 
 
Invoke Miracle 
ACTIVE (SUPERNATURAL ABILITIES STEP) 
▶ The character may spend 6FP to automatically succeed at a 
spellcasting test, providing they meet all the components of the 
spell. 
▶ The character also gains +1 proficiency level in any skill from the 
Skill Pool of the talent tree that they chose this talent from. 
 
Landed Title 
PASSIVE 
▶ The character has achieved such martial prowess, thus proving 
themselves in battle repeatedly. They may approach a local ruler 
such as a king or duke and demand to be given some land (usually 
including a Citadel Stronghold) and a title befitting the domain. 
They qualify to rule this stronghold regardless of other criteria. 
▶ The character also gains +1 proficiency level in any skill from the 
Skill Pool of the talent tree that they chose this talent from. 
 
Lead the Charge 
ACTIVE (MELEE ATTACKS STEP) 
▶ When the character makes a melee attack test, they may choose 
to spend 2FP to allow all allies who can see them to reroll failed 
attack tests for one round. 
▶ The character also gains +1 proficiency level in any skill from the 
Skill Pool of the talent tree that they chose this talent from. 
 
Leader of Men 
PASSIVE 
▶ The character doubles the number of followers that they can have 
(based on PER). 
▶ The character also gains +1 proficiency level in any skill from the 
Skill Pool of the talent tree that they chose this talent from. 
 
Legacy of the Fae 
ACTIVE (SUPERNATURAL ABILITIES STEP) 
▶ The character may spend 4FP to detect all magic within 60’ or 
receive the direction and distance of the closest portal into Elfland. 
▶ The character gains +1 proficiency level in Knowledge 
(History). 
▶ The character gains +1 proficiency level in Knowledge (Elves). 
 
Requirements and Restrictions 
A character must be of at least 8th level to take this talent. 
 
Loremaster 
PASSIVE 
▶ The character gains a single +3 proficiency levels in any single 
Knowledge specialism of their choice. 
 
Marksman Initiate 
PASSIVE 
▶ The character gains +1 proficiency level in the Ranged skill. If 
the character already has Trained or better in the Ranged skill, then 
they gain +1 proficiency level in any skill from the Skill Pool of 
the talent tree that they chose this talent from instead.

PASSIVE

PASSIVE

Scattering Swing 
ACTIVE (MELEE ATTACKS STEP) 
▶ The character may spend 3FP to choose three medium-sized or 
smaller enemies adjacent to them and then make a special melee 
attack test. If they hit, then all three of the enemies are pushed 15’ 
away. On a failed attack test, one enemy is pushed 10’ away. 
▶ The character also gains +1 proficiency level in any skill from the 
Skill Pool of the talent tree that they chose this talent from. 
 
Shield Bash 
ACTIVE (MELEE ATTACKS STEP) 
▶ The character may spend 1FP to make a Shield attack test. The 
attack has Sweep (25’). All creatures of medium size or smaller hit 
by the attack are pushed 5’ away from the character and gain 2 
stunned conditions. This movement does not provoke attacks of 
opportunity. 
▶ The character also gains +1 proficiency level in any skill from the 
Skill Pool of the talent tree that they chose this talent from.

PASSIVE 
▶ The character gains +1 proficiency level in the Shield skill. If the 
character already has Journeyman or better in the Shield skill, then 
they gain +1 proficiency level in any skill from the Skill Pool of 
the talent tree that they chose this talent from instead. 
 
 
 
 
Shireling 
PASSIVE 
As a halfling, the character has just plucked up the courage to leave 
their comfortable home and go out on adventure. 
 
▶ The character gains +1 proficiency level in Stealth. This bonus 
cannot take the skill above Trained level. If the character already 
has the skill at Trained level or better, then the character instead 
gains +1 proficiency level in any skill from the Heritage: Halfling 
Skill Pool. 
 
FIRST TALENT 
If this is taken as a character’s first talent, then they gain a 
shortsword, a bedroll and 1d6x10gp.  
 
The character also gains +1 proficiency levels in any two skills from 
the Heritage: Halfling Pool and an additional +1 proficiency level 
in the Climb Skill, Melee skill and the Sleight of Hand skill.  
 
Requirements and Restrictions 
A character must have the Halfling heritage to choose this talent. 
 
Sídhe Trance 
PASSIVE 
▶ The character no longer needs to rest for 8 hours to gain the 
benefits of an extended rest. Instead, they may enter a trance for 4 
hours. During the trance, the character can see and hear normally, 
preventing unseen foes from sneaking up and ambushing a sleeping 
party. 
▶ The character also gains +1 proficiency level in any skill from the 
Skill Pool of the talent tree that they chose this talent from. 
 
Signature Spell 
PASSIVE 
▶ The caster chooses one spell. This spell is their signature spell and 
can never be ‘forgotten’ (see the Magic and Spells Section for more 
details on this). They may change their signature spell at any time, 
but it takes 1 week per spell level of the new spell and 100 x level 
of the new spell gold pieces. In addition this spell costs 1 fewer FP 
to cast. (to a minimum of 1). 
▶ The character also gains +1 proficiency level in any skill from the 
Skill Pool of the talent tree that they chose this talent from. 
 
Silver Tongued 
ACTIVE (OTHER ACTIONS STEP) 
▶ The character may spend 2FP to reroll any failed Persuasion skill 
test. They may only do this once per test. 
▶ The character also gains +1 proficiency level in any skill from the 
Skill Pool of the talent tree that they chose this talent from. 
 
Slow Metabolism 
ACTIVE (SUPERNATURAL ABILITIES STEP) 
▶ The character may spend 2FP immediately after an extended test. 
The character does not then need to eat for the rest of the day, until 
after the character takes another extended test. 
▶ The character may spend 2FP when underwater to take no other 
action but to hold their breath. While they maintain this inaction, 
they may hold their breath for a number of turns equal to their level. 
▶ The character also gains +1 proficiency level in any skill from the 
Skill Pool of the talent tree that they chose this talent from. 
 
Sly Riposte 
ACTIVE (MELEE ATTACKS STEP) 
▶ When the character successfully parries a melee attack, they may 
cause weapon damage as though they are the attacker at the cost of 
2FP. 
▶ The character also gains +1 proficiency level in any skill from the 
Skill Pool of the talent tree that they chose this talent from.

the talent tree that they chose this talent from instead. 
Warrior Master 
PASSIVE 
▶ The character gains +1 proficiency level in the Melee skill. If the 
character already has Master or better in the Melee skill, then they 
gain +1 proficiency level in any skill from the Skill Pool of the 
talent tree that they chose this talent from instead. 
 
Warrior’s Tools 
PASSIVE 
▶ The character gains two additional carrying capacity slots. One of 
the slots may only contain a weapon and the other may only contain 
a piece of armour. These are marked on the character sheet for use. 
▶ The character also gains +1 proficiency level in any skill from the 
Skill Pool of the talent tree that they chose this talent from. 
 
Weight Distribution 
PASSIVE 
▶ The character gains two additional carrying capacity slots. 
▶ The character also gains +1 proficiency level in any skill from the 
Skill Pool of the talent tree that they chose this talent from. 
 
Whirlwind Blade 
ACTIVE (MELEE ATTACKS STEP) 
▶ The character may spend 5FP to make a melee attack test to give 
a melee attack a 40’ sweep. 
▶ The character must be wielding a two-handed weapon to perform 
this action. 
▶ The character also gains +1 proficiency level in any skill from the 
Skill Pool of the talent tree that they chose this talent from. 
 
 
 
 


 
32 
 
Magic and Spells
Many characters will gain access to spells. While some of these spells 
will be awarded by a benevolent deity to their faithful, and others 
will be learned through rigorous study. All are dangerous, and all 
are useful. 
 
One Type of Magic 
Magic is dangerous, powerful and unknowable. Because of this, 
mortal creatures cannot contain many forms of magical energy. 
Therefore, a character may never take talents or gain access to 
more than one sphere of magic, nor may they learn to trace runes 
if they know a sphere of magic. 
 
Casting Spells 
To cast a spell a character must have the appropriate talent, have 
learned the spell and then must successfully make a spellcasting 
test, which requires an action. In combat, all spells are activated 
during the Supernatural Abilities step. All spells list an FP value to 
cast. This FP value is only used if the spell is successfully cast. 
 
Reading Spell Entries 
When a spell is cast, whether successfully or unsuccessfully, consult 
the entry for the spell. There are four entries for each spell: critical 
failure (if a natural 1 was rolled on the spellcasting test); a failure; 
a success and a critical success (if a natural 20 was rolled on the 
spellcasting test). 
 
Each spell will also have a number of entries explained below: 
▶ Fails: The spell fails and does not produce any desired effect. If 
the entry includes this, then the FP cost of the spell is not used.  
▶ Forgotten: Spells must be forced into the brain of the caster and 
sometimes they escape. If this entry applies, the caster cannot cast 
this spell again until they have taken an extended rest, as it must be 
once more coded into their memory.  
▶ Miscast: The wrong intonation or hand gesture can sometimes 
result in a seriously dangerous situation for the caster. If this entry 
applies, the character should immediately roll on the spell’s miscast 
table. 
 
Upcasting 
Spells have an additional entry called Upcasting. This allows a 
character to spend additional FP to modify the spell in some way, 
provided they have the relevant talent. 
 
Durations, Targets and Spheres 
▶ All spells will list their legitimate targets. Such ranges are given in 
feet or apply only to the caster themselves. 
▶ All spells will list durations; this will be given explicitly but may 
vary depending on the level of the caster. 
▶ Each spell entry will list a sphere or spheres. Different talents 
provide access to spells of different spheres. A character may only 
learn spells for those levels and spheres they have access to. 
 
Components 
▶ V means ‘verbal’. If a character cannot speak, then they may not 
cast this spell. 
▶ G means ‘gesture’. If a character cannot move their hands and 
body freely to make the correct motions, then they may not cast this 
spell. 
▶ M means ‘Material’. There will be some listed item that caster must 
have on their person which the spell consumes. If the listed item 
does not have a gp value also listed, then the item can be replaced 
with generic spell components (see equipment). 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
Spellcasting Focus 
Some characters have access to a spellcasting focus. This is usually 
some kind of hand-held object, such as a holy symbol for clerics or 
an instrument for bards. Characters who have a spellcasting focus in 
their hand (it is not enough for it to simply be on their person) may 
ignore the material component of spells provided the component 
does not list a cost in gold pieces. Material components with a listed 
value in gold pieces are still required and consumed when the 
spellcaster casts the listed spell.  
 
Spell Schools 
All spells belong to one or more spell schools. This is the type of 
magic that they draw upon. Some talents allow characters to learn 
and cast spells of particular schools more easily. All schools have an 
opposed school, which can be seen below. 
Abjuration 
Abjuration magic is protective. It is used to provide wards and 
defences against both magical and mundane attacks. 
Alteration 
Alteration, sometimes called ‘transmutation’ allows a character to 
change the universe in some non-destructive way, such as 
transmuting stone into mud or unlocking doors. 
Charm 
Charm magic allows a caster to control and dominate the minds of 
other creatures. It is sometimes subtle in nature and other times it is 
dangerously explicit. 
Conjuration 
Conjuration magic summons creatures and items from elsewhere, 
sometimes even other planes of existence, to the caster. 
Destruction 
Destruction magic concerns itself with the elemental forces such as 
fire, earth, air, and water. It is devastating in nature and channels 
that against the caster’s foes. 
Divination 
Divination magic allows a caster to see the future through portents 
and truth-seeing. 
Illusion 
Illusion magic allows a caster to create vast imaginary visions, alter 
memories, and disguise themselves and their allies from others. 
Necromancy 
Necromancy is magic associated with death. It allows a caster to 
contact and communicate with the deceased and, at higher levels, 
may even allow the raising of the dead. 
 
 
 


Magic and Spells 
33 
 
Spell Books 
Some characters, such as those learning spells through the Arcane 
and Expanded Spell Book talents require a spell book. Spell books 
are physical representations of that character’s magical lore and 
must be guarded carefully.  
 
Adding Spells 
A spell may be copied into a spell book by a character. This costs a 
number of gold pieces equal to 100 x spell’s level. The successful 
chance of this copying is based on the INT proficiency level of the 
character, as seen on the table below: 
 
CHANCE OF COPYING SPELL 
INT 
% Chance 
Untrained 
50% 
Trained 
75% 
Journeyman 
80% 
Expert 
90% 
Master 
95% 
Grandmaster 
99% 
 
If a character fails to copy a spell, they may attempt again after 
paying the necessary number of gold pieces. 
 
Counterspelling 
Counterspelling is the attempt by one caster to try and disrupt 
another spellcaster’s magic as it is cast. It can be a fraught and 
highly dangerous activity and works as follows: 
 
Counterspelling an Opponent 
As a spell is a cast by a creature, another creature declares they are 
attempting to counterspell. This declaration takes place outside of 
normal initiative order and must happen before the caster makes 
their own spellcasting test. To be able to declare a counterspell 
attempt, a creature must have at least Journeyman proficiency in 
Spellcasting. A creature may only attempt a counterspell once per 
round. 
 
As the attacker, the creature attempting the counterspell 
immediately makes an opposed spellcasting test with their 
opponent.  
 
Once the opposed spellcasting test is resolved, the victor of the 
opposed test rolls on the table below: 
 
COUNTERSPELL TABLE (ATTACKER WINS) 
D6 
Outcome 
1 
The original spell is not cast but the counterspeller suffers 
5 arcane damage that ignores armour saves. 
2-3 
The original spell is not cast but the counterspeller suffers 
3 arcane damage that ignores armour saves. 
4-5 
The original spell is not cast but the counterspeller suffers 
1 arcane damage that ignores armour saves. 
6 
The original spell is countered and does not cast. 
 
COUNTERSPELL TABLE (DEFENDER WINS) 
D6 
Outcome 
1 
The original spell is cast as intended. 
2-3 
The original spell is cast as intended and the 
counterspeller suffers 2 arcane damage that ignores 
armour saves. 
4-5 
The original spell is cast as intended and the 
counterspeller suffers 5 arcane damage that ignores 
armour saves. 
6 
The original spell is cast as a critical success and the 
counterspeller suffers 5 arcane damage that ignores 
armour saves. 
 
Life Burn 
A character who does not have enough FP to cast a spell may 
choose to Life Burn. This involves spending experience levels to 
gain enough FP to cast a spell, on a 1-1 basis. For example, a 9th 
level character currently with 4FP remaining, attempting to cast a 
spell which requires 6FP to cast, may spend two experience levels 
(reducing their level to 7th) in order to cast the spell. 
Crafting Spells 
It is only natural that as spellcaster accrues power that they may 
wish to craft new spells to use within the campaign world. A game 
master should use the following guidelines to help players craft 
spells. All spells are crafted using the craft (spells) skill.  
 
Proposing a New Spell 
Creating new spells is an art, not a science, and a player should 
negotiate with a game master as to what level and FP cost a spell 
should be. A good place to start would be to look at the spells in 
this book and consider whether a proposed new spell is weaker or 
stronger than other particular spells to help both player and game 
master judge what qualities the spell should have. 
 
Arcane Spellcrafting 
Only spells of the Arcane sphere may be crafted. This is because 
they are the expressed manipulation of elemental forces. Bardic, 
Divine and Primal spells cannot be crafted as they are bestowed by 
outside forces. 
 
Healing Magic 
Spells cannot be crafted to restore hit points or fatigue, this is the 
domain of the gods, and therefore separate from the Arcane 
sphere. 
 
Required Skill, Cost and Time 
A character should consult the table below to calculate the skill, 
cost and time required to craft a spell. 
 
SPELLCRAFT TABLE 
Spell 
Level 
Craft (Spells) 
Prof. Required 
Cost 
Time 
1st 
T 
100gp x FP 
1d6 days 
2nd 
J 
250gp x FP 
1d6 months 
3rd 
E 
600gp x FP 
1d6 months 
4th 
M 
1,500gp x FP 
4d6 months 
5th 
G 
10,000gp x FP 
1d6+6 years 
 
 
 
 
 


Magic and Spells 
34 
 
Spell Descriptions 
1st Level Spells 
Alarum (1FP Cost) 
Schools: Abjuration, Illusion 
Components: V, G, M (a small bell) 
Duration: 8 hours 
Range: Touch 
Spheres: Arcane, Divine 
The approach of the gnolls awakens the campers with the ringing of 
an alarum bell. 
Casting: 
▶ Critical Failure: Fails, Forgotten. 
▶ Failure: Fails. 
▶ Success: The caster traces a radius of up to 30’. Any creature not 
designated by the caster that crosses into the radius during the 
duration causes a harsh ringing, like that of bells, to resound 
throughout the area. 
▶ Critical Success: The caster traces a radius of up to 30’. Any 
creature not designated by the caster that crosses into the radius 
during the duration causes a harsh ringing, like that of bells, to 
resound throughout the area. This ringing is only heard by those 
creatures that the caster designates upon the casting of the spell. 
Upcasting: 
A character may spend additional FP on a successful cast of Alarum. 
For every additional 2FP spent, they may increase the radius of the 
area affected by 10’. 
Sorcerous Change: 
A character may spend additional FP on a successful cast of Alarum. 
They may spend 3FP to cause any creature triggering the Alarum to 
immediately gain two stunned conditions. 
 
Alter Self (1FP Cost) 
Schools: Alteration, Illusion 
Components: V, G 
Duration: 1 hour/caster’s level 
Range: Self 
Spheres: Arcane 
With a movement of their palm over their face, the caster immediately 
reorders their physiognomic structures. 
Casting: 
▶ Critical Failure: Fails, Forgotten. 
▶ Failure: Fails. 
▶ Success: The caster changes their face to another member of their 
species, including their perceived sex. They may also alter their 
height by up to 12”. They may not however replicate the real face of 
another creature, nor may they use this to alter their form to a 
different species: a dwarf cannot use this to give themselves an elven 
face. 
▶ Critical Success: The caster changes their face to another member 
of their species, including their perceived sex. They may also alter 
their height by up to 24”. They may replicate the real face of another 
creature, providing they can picture that face clearly. They may they 
not use this to alter their form to a different species: a dwarf cannot 
use this to give themselves an elven face. 
Upcasting: 
A character may spend additional FP on a successful cast of Alter 
Self. If the caster spends an additional 2FP, the spell may be cast on 
a touched ally rather than themselves. 
Sorcerous Change: 
A character may spend additional FP on a successful cast of Alter 
Self. They may spend 2FP allows the caster to alter their species too. 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
Arcane Shield (1FP Cost) 
Schools: Abjuration, Conjuration 
Components: V, G 
Duration: 2 rounds/caster’s level 
Range: Self 
Spheres: Arcane 
A glimmering magical film enwraps the caster, protecting them from 
harm. 
Casting: 
▶ Critical Failure: Fails, Forgotten. 
▶ Failure: Fails. 
▶ Success: For the duration, the caster gains a 6+ power save. 
▶ Critical Success: For the duration, the caster gains a 4+ power 
save. 
Upcasting: 
A character may spend additional FP on a successful cast of Arcane 
Shield. For every additional 2FP spent, they may give this power 
save onto an additional creature within 30’. 
Sorcerous Change: 
A character may spend additional FP on a successful cast of Arcane 
Shield. They may spend 2FP to make this casting a 5+ power save 
on a success, or a 3+ power save on a critical success. 
 
Bane (2FP Cost) 
Schools: Abjuration, Conjuration 
Components: V, G 
Duration: 1 round/caster’s level 
Range: 30’ 
Spheres: Divine 
With a muttered curse the priest brings upon doom and death to their 
foes. 
Casting: 
▶ Critical Failure: Fails, Forgotten. 
▶ Failure: Fails. 
▶ Success: The caster chooses one target within range. The target 
must save vs PER or suffer a -3 penalty to all tests made. 
▶ Critical Success: The caster chooses up to three targets within 
range. The targets must save vs PER or suffer a -3 penalty to all tests 
made. 
Upcasting: 
A character may spend additional FP on a successful cast of Bane. 
For every additional 2FP spent, they may choose an additional 
target. 
 
 
Bless (2FP Cost) 
Schools: Abjuration, Conjuration 
Components: V, G 
Duration: 1 round/caster’s level 
Range: 30’ 
Spheres: Divine 
With an intonation to their god, the cleric empowers their allies to 
greater feats of power. 
Miscast: 
▶: The caster’s god is offended with them. All further spellcasting 
tests suffer a -3 penalty until the caster takes an extended rest. 
Casting: 
▶ Critical Failure: Fails, Forgotten, Miscast. 
▶ Failure: Fails. 
▶ Success: For the duration, one target gains a +3 bonus to all tests 
made. 
▶ Critical Success: For the duration, up to three targets gains a +6 
bonus to all tests made. 
Upcasting: 
A character may spend additional FP on a successful cast of Bless. 
For every additional 2FP spent, they may give the above bonus to 
an additional creature within 30’. 
 
 


Magic and Spells 
35 
 
Blind/Deafen (2FP Cost) 
Schools: Abjuration, Conjuration 
Components: V, G, M (a blindworm’s eye) 
Duration: 1 round/caster’s level 
Range: 30’ 
Spheres: Divine 
An uttered word and the ogre clutches its eyes in abject panic. 
Miscast: 
▶: The caster is blinded for 1d4 rounds. 
Casting: 
▶ Critical Failure: Fails, Forgotten, Miscast. 
▶ Failure: Fails. 
▶ Success: The caster chooses one target within range. The target 
must save vs PER or become blinded or deafened. 
▶ Critical Success: The caster chooses up to three targets within 
range. The targets must save vs PER or become blinded or deafened. 
Upcasting: 
A character may spend additional FP on a successful cast of 
Blind/Deafen. For every additional 2FP spent, they may choose an 
additional target. 
 
Blur (2FP Cost) 
Schools: Alteration 
Components: V, G 
Duration: 1 round/caster’s level 
Range: Self 
Spheres: Arcane, Divine 
Like a shimmering outline, the bard streaks past her foes. 
Miscast: 
▶: The caster gains a slowed condition that remains until they take 
an extended rest. 
Casting: 
▶ Critical Failure: Fails, Forgotten, Miscast. 
▶ Failure: Fails. 
▶ Success: The caster’s speed doubles for the duration. 
▶ Critical Success: The caster’s speed doubles for the duration, and 
they gain a 5+ power save due to their preternatural speed. 
Upcasting: 
A character may spend additional FP on a successful cast of Blur. If 
2FP is spent, they may triple their speed. 
Sorcerous Change: 
A character may spend additional FP on a successful cast of Blur. 
For every additional 2FP spent, they may choose an additional 
creature to gain the benefits of a successful casting of Blur. They 
may only spend up to 6FP this way. 
 
Bolt (1FP Cost) 
Schools: Destruction 
Components: V, G 
Duration: Instant 
Range: 80’ 
Spheres: Arcane 
A translucent dart of energy leaves the caster and strikes their foes 
within unerring accuracy. 
Casting: 
▶ Critical Failure: Fails, Forgotten. 
▶ Failure: Fails. 
▶ Success: The caster throws two darts that each cause 1 arcane 
damage against targets within range. The caster must have line of 
sight to the targets. The dart never misses and does not allow an 
armour save. 
▶ Critical Success: The caster throws a number of darts x caster 
level (or five, whichever is higher) that do to 2 arcane damage each. 
The darts never miss and do not allow an Armour Save. 
Upcasting: 
A character may spend additional FP on a successful cast of Bolt. 
For every additional 2FP spent, they gain an additional dart as 
described above. They may only use this to create up to three 
additional darts. 
Sorcerous Change: 
A character may spend additional FP on a successful cast of Bolt. 
They may spend 1FP to change the damage from arcane to fire, acid, 
or lightning. 
Charm Person (2FP Cost) 
Schools: Charm, Illusion 
Components: V, G, M (a piece of gingerbread) 
Duration: Varies (see below) 
Range: 80’ 
Spheres: Arcane 
Lambent green chains slither from the caster’s finger tips like snakes, 
seeking out their foe’s eyes. 
Miscast: 
▶: The caster must immediately save vs PER or fall in love with one 
of the intended targets. 
Casting: 
▶ Critical Failure: Fails, Forgotten, Miscast. 
▶ Failure: Fails. 
▶ Success: The caster may charm (see below) up to 2d6 levels’ 
worth of humanoids. Each creature affected may roll a save vs PER 
to resist. 
▶ Critical Success: The caster may charm (see below) up to 3 x 
caster’s level’s worth of humanoids. Each creature affected may roll 
a save vs PER to resist. Creatures of 2nd level or lower do not get a 
save. 
Upcasting: 
A character may spend additional FP on a successful cast of Charm 
Person. For every additional 1FP spent, they gain an additional 
level’s worth of creature to charm (see below). They may only spend 
6FP in this way. 
Sorcerous Change: 
A character may spend additional FP on a successful cast of Charm 
Person. They may spend 1FP to make the charmed creature treat 
another creature as its friend (rather than the caster). 
 
Charmed: 
A creature that is charmed treats the caster as a friend and ally. They 
are not under the caster’s direct command but will generally heed 
the caster’s requests as would a close friend. A creature retests its 
Save vs PER based on its Intellect proficiency level: 
▶ Untrained: Once per month. 
▶ Trained and Journeyman: Once per week. 
▶ Expert: Once per day. 
▶ Master: Once per hour. 
▶ Grandmaster: Once per turn (10 minutes). 
 
▶ Charmed Creatures count as followers as per the rules for 
Personality. 
 
Chill Touch (1FP Cost) 
Schools: Destruction, Necromancy 
Components: V, G 
Duration: Instant 
Range: Touch 
Spheres: Arcane, Divine 
The caster’s hands become white with hoarfrost and their touch 
becomes that of the grave. 
Casting: 
▶ Critical Failure: Fails, Forgotten. 
▶ Failure: Fails. 
▶ Success: The caster touches a creature which takes 3 cold damage. 
A melee attack test is not required. 
▶ Critical Success: The caster touches a creature which takes 5 cold 
damage. A melee attack test is not required. 
Upcasting: 
A character may spend additional FP on a successful cast of Chill 
Touch. For every additional 2FP spent, the target suffers an 
additional 1 cold damage as described above. 
Sorcerous Change: 
A character may spend additional FP on a successful cast of Chill 
Touch. They may spend 2FP to make the target also gain a slowed 
condition.

Components: V, G

Twisting shoots emerge from the ground, grasping every creature in
