Heat Metal (2FP Cost) 
Schools: Alteration 
Components: V, G 
Duration: 5 rounds (see below) 
Range: 30’ 
Spheres: Arcane, Divine 
The orc throws its battle axe to the floor, which glows white hot with 
heat. 
Casting: 
▶ Critical Failure: Fails, Forgotten. 
▶ Failure: Fails. 
▶ Success: The caster chooses a large-sized object or smaller within 
range. The object begins to heat, based on the table below: 
Success: Heat Metal 
Round 
Effect 
1 
The metal is very warm, no effect. 
2 
Contact with the metal is painful, 2 fire damage. 
3 
Contact with the metal is dangerous, 4 fire damage. 
4 
Contact with the metal is painful, 2 fire damage. 
5 
The metal is very warm, no effect. 
 
▶ Critical Success: The caster chooses a large-sized object or 
smaller within range. The object begins to heat, based on the table 
below: 
Critical Success: Heat Metal 
Round 
Effect 
1 
Contact with the metal is painful, 2 fire damage. 
2 
Contact with the metal is dangerous, 4 fire damage. 
3 
The metal melts, 6 fire damage. 
4 
Contact with the metal is dangerous, 4 fire damage. 
5 
Contact with the metal is painful, 2 fire damage. 
If the item is held by a creature, it may immediately drop the item 
in order to avoid the damage. 
Upcasting: 
A character may spend additional FP on a successful cast of Heat 
Metal. If 2FP is spent, the success table starts at round 2. 
Sorcerous Change: 
A character may spend additional FP on a successful cast of Heat 
Metal. They may spend 1FP to reverse the damage to cold instead 
of fire. 
 
 
 
 


Magic and Spells 
38 
 
Helbutler’s Burning Bolts (1FP Cost) 
Schools: Destruction 
Components: V, G 
Duration: Instant 
Range: 120’ 
Spheres: Arcane 
Grinning darts of living flame snake from the caster’s fingertips 
seeking armour to melt and flesh to sear. 
Miscast: D4 
▶ 1: The caster gains 1 burning condition. 
▶ 2: There is an explosion of flaming elemental energy. The caster 
and all creatures within 15’ suffer 4 fire damage, (save vs AGI for 
half damage). 
▶ 3:  Choking, pyroclastic smoke billows out from the caster. All 
within a 20’ radius of the caster (including the caster), must save vs 
END or become blinded for 1d4 rounds and suffer 1 fire damage.  
▶ 4: An impish, childish giggling can be heard from the bolts. 1d4 
bolts attempt to burn non-living flammable objects within 30’ of the 
caster. What the bolts attempt to burn is up to the game master, but 
they should prioritise paper, oil and anything else obviously highly 
flammable or explosive first. 
Casting: 
▶ Critical Failure: Fails, Forgotten, Miscast. 
▶ Failure: Fails. 
▶ Success: The caster throws one bolt that causes 2 fire damage 
against one target within range. The caster must have line of sight 
to the target. The bolt never misses and does not allow an armour 
save. 
▶ Critical Success: The caster throws a number of bolts x caster 
level (or five, whichever is higher) that do to 4 fire damage each. 
The darts never miss and do not allow an armour save. 
Upcasting: 
A character may spend additional FP on a successful cast of 
Helbutler’s Burning Bolts. For every additional 3FP spent, they gain 
an additional bolt as described above. They may only use this to 
create up to two additional bolts. They may also spend 2FP x the 
number of bolts to change the damage to 1 fire damage and 1 
burning condition. 
Sorcerous Change: 
A character may spend additional FP on a successful cast of 
Helbutler’s Burning Bolts. They may spend 1FP to change the 
damage from arcane to fire to cold. This cannot be done on an 
upcasted version of the spell. 
 
Irenic Benediction (2FP Cost) 
Schools: Abjuration 
Components: V, G 
Duration: 1 round/caster’s level 
Range: 30’ 
Spheres: Divine 
The caster extends a peace-making aura about themselves in attempt 
to de-escalate the moment. 
Casting: 
▶ Critical Failure: Fails, Forgotten. 
▶ Failure: Fails. 
▶ Success: The caster is given irenic benediction (see below). 
▶ Critical Success: The caster and up to three additional allies are 
given irenic benediction (see below). 
A character may spend additional FP on a successful cast of Irenic 
Benediction. For every additional 2FP spent, they may give irenic 
benediction (see below) to an additional creature within 30’. 
 
Irenic Benediction: 
No one may make an attack test against a creature with irenic 
benediction without first making a hard (-3) save vs PER. If a 
creature with irenic benediction makes an attack test, or casts a 
spell then they immediately lose their irenic benediction. 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
Light (1FP Cost) 
Schools: Alteration, Conjuration 
Components: V, G 
Duration: 1 turn/caster’s level 
Range: Touch 
Spheres: Arcane,, Divine 
A gentle blow on the end of the wizard’s staff and soon enough the 
whole area is bathed in radiance. 
Casting: 
▶ Critical Failure: Fails, Forgotten. 
▶ Failure: Fails. 
▶ Success: The caster chooses a handheld object such as a sword 
or staff. This item radiates light in a 30’ radius. A creature holding 
the object may dismiss the light at any time but doing so means the 
spell must be recast. 
▶ Critical Success: The caster chooses a handheld object such as a 
sword or staff. This item radiates light in a 30’ radius. The radius 
may be dimmed (even extinguished) at will. Reducing the light to 
nothing does not mean the spell has to be recast, the holder of the 
object may instantly brighten the area again. The duration is 
doubled. 
Upcasting: 
A character may spend additional FP on a successful cast of Light. 
For every additional 1FP spent, the spell increases the affected area 
by an additional 10’. 
Sorcerous Change: 
A character may spend additional FP on a successful cast of Light 
They may spend 1FP to snuff out all natural light sources within 30’ 
instead of the spell’s usual effects. 
 
Minor Divination (2FP Cost) 
Schools: Divination 
Components: V, G 
Duration: 1 turn 
Range: Touch 
Spheres: Arcane 
The caster accurately predicts the spear trap, shouting a word of 
warning to their comrade. 
Casting: 
▶ Critical Failure: Fails, Forgotten. 
▶ Failure: Fails. 
▶ Success: The caster rolls 1d20 and records the number. They may 
at any time within the next turn (10 minutes) exchange the value just 
rolled with the value of any test or save they witness (including their 
own, their allies and their enemy’s). The caster may only ever have 
1 of these values on standby at once – casting the spell additional 
times replaces the d20 value.  
▶ Critical Success: The caster rolls 3d20 and records the three 
numbers. They may at any time within the next turn (10 minutes) 
exchange any of the values just rolled with the value of any test or 
save they witness (including their own, their allies and their 
enemy’s). The caster may only ever have three of these values on 
standby at once – casting the spell additional times replaces all of 
the values.

Cloud of Fog (3FP Cost) 
Schools: Alteration, Conjuration 
Components: V, G 
Duration: 1 round/caster’s level 
Range: 120’ 
Spheres: Arcane 
Billowing fog covers the area, smothering everything in blinding 
cold. 
Miscast:  
▶: A 10’ radius black cloud appears above the caster’s head. The 
cloud is centred on them and will move with the caster when they 
move. The cloud pours down endless freezing rain and remains for 
1d4 days. For this time, anyone who spends a significant amount of 
time under the cloud (more than 8 hours) gains 2 drained 
conditions whenever they take an extended rest and do not remove 
2 like they usually would. 
Casting: 
▶ Critical Failure: Fails, Forgotten, Miscast. 
▶ Failure: Fails, Forgotten. 
▶ Success: A cloud of fog billows out from the caster in a 15’ radius. 
The fog is centred on the caster and moves with them if they move. 
Creatures being viewed through the fog appear as rough outlines. 
Ranged attack tests made against creatures in the fog count as hard 
(-3). All creatures in the fog count as having +1 Proficiency level in 
the Stealth skill. 
▶ Critical Success: A cloud of fog billows out from the caster in a 
25’ radius. The fog is centred on the caster and moves with them if 
they move. Creatures being viewed through the fog appear as rough 
outlines. Ranged attack tests made against creatures in the fog 
count as very hard (-6). All creatures in the fog count as having +2 
Proficiency levels in the Stealth skill. 
Upcasting: 
A character may spend additional FP on a successful cast of Cloud 
of Fog. For every additional 2FP spent, the spell increases the radius 
created by an additional 5’. 
Sorcerous Change: 
A character may spend additional 2FP on a successful cast of Cloud 
of Fog. If they do this, the fog instead appears as a thin hovering 
cloud that dispenses freezing rain. The radius does not grant bonuses 
to stealth or protection from ranged attack tests, but the rain may 
be otherwise useful in some situations.

from their form, confounding any would-be attackers. 
Miscast: 
▶: The caster creates an exact duplicate of themselves. It has their 
memories and talents, acting like them in every sense. 
However, it hates them. 
Casting: 
▶ Critical Failure: Fails, Forgotten. 
▶ Failure: Fails, Forgotten. 
▶ Success: The caster creates two 
Illusionary Duplicates (see below).  
▶ Critical Success: The caster creates 
six Illusionary Duplicates (see below). 
Upcasting: 
A character may spend additional FP 
on a successful cast of Illusionary 
Duplication. For every additional 
2FP spent, they gain an additional 
Illusionary Duplicate (see below). 
They may only spend 6FP in this 
way.  
 
Illusionary Duplicate: 
An illusionary duplicate is an exact copy of the caster in every way. 
They stand within 5’ of the character and copy their actions. If a 
creature hits the character, randomise the hit across the duplicates. 
A duplicate has 1HP and an armour save of 5+. 
 
Infernal Messenger (3FP Cost) 
Schools: Conjuration, Illusion 
Components: V, G 
Duration: Special (see below) 
Range: Self 
Spheres: Arcane 
A winged demon arrives from a rising black smoke, ready to deliver 
the caster’s message. 
Casting: 
▶ Critical Failure: Fails, Forgotten. 
▶ Failure: Fails, Forgotten. 
▶ Success: The caster summons a small demon. The demon’s sole 
purpose on this plane is to deliver a message of the caster’s choosing 
to a person they name. The message may not be longer than 25 
words long. The demon stays on this plane until exorcised or until 
it has delivered its message. It has a fly speed of 30’ per round.  
▶ Critical Success: The caster summons a small demon. The 
demon’s sole purpose on this plane is to deliver a message of the 
caster’s choosing to a person they name. The message may not be 
longer than 50 words long. The demon stays on this plane until 
exorcised or until it has delivered its message. It has a fly speed of 
50’ per round. 
Upcasting: 
A character may spend additional FP on a successful cast of Infernal 
Messenger. For every 1FP, they may increase the length of the 
message by ten words. 
Sorcerous Change: 
A character may spend additional FP on a successful cast of Infernal 
Messenger. They may spend 2FP to make the demon invisible. 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
Infravision (4FP Cost) 
Schools: Alteration, Divination 
Components: V, G, M (a cat’s eye) 
Duration: 1 turn/caster’s level 
Range: 30’ 
Spheres: Arcane, Divine 
The druid waves their spindly fingers and utters a word. Suddenly 
their companions can see in the lightless corridor. 
Miscast: D3 
▶1: The caster is blinded. 
▶2-3: All creatures within 30’ must save vs PER. On a failure, 
their skin takes on a slight yellow glow for the duration, 
making it very easy for enemies to see them, especially 
in the dark. 
Casting: 
▶ Critical Failure: Fails, Forgotten, Miscast. 
▶ Failure: Fails, Forgotten. 
▶ Success: The caster and up to three other 
creatures in range gain infravision (see below) 
for the duration. 
▶ Critical Success: The caster and up to six 
other creatures in range gain infravision (see 
below) for the duration. 
Upcasting: 
A character may spend additional FP on a 
successful cast of Infravision. For every 
additional 1FP spent, they may choose another 
creature within range which gain infravision 
(see below) for the duration. 
 
Infravision: 
A creature with infravision can see in the dark up to 60’ If a creature 
with infravision is given the subject of this spell, then their 
infravision is increased by an additional 60’. 
 
Invisibility (5FP Cost) 
Schools: Conjuration, Illusion 
Components: V, G 
Duration: 1 turn/caster’s level 
Range: Self 
Spheres: Arcane 
With an uttered word, the wizard melts into the air, taking on an 
unseen form. 
Miscast: D3 
▶1: The caster is made invisible as a usual casting. However, none 
of their carried equipment is. 
▶2: Something goes terribly wrong, and the caster transforms part 
of themselves permanently invisible. Roll a d4 for location: (1) head; 
(2) arms; (3) legs; (4) torso. 
▶3: For 1d4 days, every single step the caster takes is accompanied 
by a loud ringing alarum bell. 
Casting: 
▶ Critical Failure: Fails, Forgotten, Miscast. 
▶ Failure: Fails, Forgotten. 
▶ Success: The caster is invisible (see below) for the duration. 
▶ Critical Success: The caster makes themselves and up to six allies 
invisible (see below) for the duration. 
Upcasting: 
A character may spend additional FP on a successful cast of 
Invisibility. For every additional 3FP spent, they may choose another 
creature within 10’ which becomes invisible (see below) for the 
duration. 
 
Invisible: 
An invisible creature cannot be seen by other creatures with normal 
sight. All of their carried equipment is made invisible too. They may 
interact with the world normally but if they make an attack test, use 
a supernatural ability or cast a spell, then the invisibility instantly 
wears off. Creatures that are aware of the invisible creature and try 
to attack it may do so, but the attack test becomes very hard (-6). 
 
 


Magic and Spells 
44 
 
Major Illusion (4FP Cost) 
Schools: Conjuration, Illusion 
Components: V, G 
Duration: 1 turn/caster’s level 
Range: Touch 
Spheres: Arcane 
A monstrous dragon flaps over the heads of the attacking gnolls 
causing sheer panic. Little did they know, it wasn’t even real… 
Casting: 
▶ Critical Failure: Fails, Forgotten. 
▶ Failure: Fails, Forgotten. 
▶ Success: The caster creates an illusion that is no larger than huge 
sized. The illusion can take any form but cannot interact with the 
world physically and is ultimately harmless. 
▶ Critical Success: The caster creates an illusion that is no larger 
than gargantuan sized. The illusion can take any form but cannot 
interact with the world physically and is ultimately harmless. 
Upcasting: 
A character may spend additional 3FP on a successful cast of Major 
Illusion. If they do this, the duration becomes 1 day/caster’s level. 
Sorcerous Change: 
A character may spend additional FP on a successful cast of Major 
Illusion. They may spend 2FP to increase the size of the illusion up 
to gargantuan size.  
 
Mordant Missile (3FP Cost) 
Schools: Destruction, Conjuration  
Components: V, G, M (a drop of vinegar) 
Duration: Instant 
Range: 80’+ 
Spheres: Arcane 
A sickly green and yellow arrow made of corrosive acid streaks across 
the room towards the foe, splashing and hissing on impact. 
Miscast: D4 
▶1: A stinging yellow cloud of fog billows out from the caster. All 
creatures within 20’ of the cast (not including the caster) must save 
vs END or take two stunned conditions. 
▶2: The ground beneath the caster turns into a 10’ deep, 15’ wide 
pit of strong, green acid. The caster is plunged into the acid. Anyone 
in the acid takes 2 acid damage per round. 
▶3: The caster summons and throws a murky green dart of stinging 
liquid, but the dart ignores the instruction and flies towards the 
nearest ally. The target must save vs AGI or suffer 4 acid damage. 
If they are wearing metal armour, then their AP is reduced by 1. 
▶4: The caster summons a dark cloud that covers the area for a ½ a 
mile radius centred on the caster. The cloud deluges acid rain and 
anyone under the cloud takes 2 acid damage per round. The acid is 
not strong enough to affect buildings or armour. The cloud lasts for 
1 turn (10 minutes). 
Casting: 
▶ Critical Failure: Fails, Forgotten, Miscast. 
▶ Failure: Fails, Forgotten 
▶ Success: The caster chooses one target within range. That target 
must save vs AGI or suffer 4 acid damage, or half as much on a 
successful save. If they are wearing metal armour, then their armour 
saves are permanently reduced by 1. 
▶ Critical Success: The caster chooses one target within range. That 
target must save vs AGI or suffer 8 acid damage or half as much on 
a successful save. If they are wearing metal armour, then their 
armour saves are permanently reduced by 3. 
Upcasting: 
A character may spend additional FP on a successful cast of Mordant 
Missile. For every additional 2FP spent, they may increase the 
damage by 1. 
Sorcerous Change: 
A character may spend additional FP on a successful cast of Mordant 
Missile. They may spend 2FP to change the damage to fire damage. 
If they do this, the target’s armour save is unaffected, but they gain 
a burning condition. 
 
Nondetection (3FP Cost) 
Schools: Abjuration, Illusion 
Components: V, G, M (a small willow-branch brush) 
Duration: 1 turn/caster’s level 
Range: 30’ 
Spheres: Divine 
With a sweep of their hand, the cleric disguises the passage of 
themselves and their allies. 
Casting: 
▶ Critical Failure: Fails, Forgotten. 
▶ Failure: Fails, Forgotten. 
▶ Success: The caster exudes a zone of nondetection (see below) 
in a 30’ radius around themselves. 
▶ Critical Success: The caster exudes a zone of nondetection (see 
below) in a 30’ radius around themselves, but the usual duration is 
doubled. 
 
Nondetection: 
While inside the zone of nondetection, a character leaves no 
footprints, regardless of the terrain, and they disturb no foliage. They 
generally leave absolutely no trace of their passage while in the 
zone, even campfires are not detectable if they were used entirely 
within the zone. 
 
Open Locks, Whoever Knocks (3FP Cost) 
Schools: Alteration, Conjuration 
Components: V, G, M (a piece of key) 
Duration: Instant 
Range: 120’ 
Spheres: Arcane, Divine 
The caster waves their hands and their comrade’s shackles unlock 
and the prison door slides open. 
Miscast: 
▶: The door springs to life, crossbars and rivets transforming into 
teeth. Anyone within 10’ of the door must save vs AGI or take 2 
piercing damage. The door then transforms back into a normal door. 
Casting: 
▶ Critical Failure: Fails, Forgotten, Miscast. 
▶ Failure: Fails, Forgotten. 
▶ Success: The caster opens a single, non-magical door or lock 
within range. 
▶ Critical Success: For one turn, the caster may open up to caster’s 
level magical or non-magical doors or locks within range silently. In 
addition, any lock opened in this manner may not be locked again 
by any mundane means for 8 hours unless the caster wishes it to be 
locked. 
 
Protection from Missiles (5FP Cost) 
Schools: Abjuration, Divination 
Components: V, G 
Duration: 1 round/caster’s level 
Range: 30’ 
Spheres: Divine 
The incoming orc arrows patter harmlessly of the skin of the caster. 
Casting: 
▶ Critical Failure: Fails, Forgotten. 
▶ Failure: Fails. 
▶ Success: The caster and up to two other allies are given 
protection from missiles (see below). 
▶ Critical Success: The caster and up to six other allies are given 
protection from missiles (see below). 
Upcasting: 
A character may spend additional FP on a successful cast of 
Protection from Missiles. For every additional 2FP spent, they may 
give protection from missiles (see below) to an additional creature 
within 30’. 
 
Protection from Missiles: 
A creature that is protected from missiles may reroll all failed 
armour saves (including cover saves but not power saves) taken 
against attacks from mundane, non-magical sources such as thrown 
rocks, crossbow bolts and arrows. 
 


Magic and Spells 
45 
 
Reeking Fog (4FP Cost) 
Schools: Conjuration, Destruction 
Components: V, G, M (a fragment of rotting egg) 
Duration: 1 round/caster’s level 
Range: 120’ 
Spheres: Arcane, Divine 
A vaporous green fog stinking of death and sewage billows out of a 
spot of the caster’s choosing. 
Miscast: D3 
▶1: The caster summons a cloud as a success entry but does not 
have control of where it appears within range, nor do they control 
where it moves. The cloud is alive and wants to envelop as many 
people in its gaseous body as possible. 
▶2: The caster is cursed. Whenever they speak, the words simply 
come out as flatulence.  
▶3: The caster’s breath smells like raw sewage for 1d10+10 days, 
making it intensely hard for anyone to hold a conversation with 
them. 
Casting: 
▶ Critical Failure: Fails, Forgotten, Miscast. 
▶ Failure: Fails, Forgotten. 
▶ Success: The caster summons a 15’x15’ stinking cloud within 
range. Those within the cloud must save vs END at the start of their 
turn or gained 2 drained conditions due to inhaling the disgusting 
smell. The creature cannot fail this save more than once. A successful 
save means that creature will never be affected by this cloud. 
▶ Critical Success: The caster summons a 30’x30’ stinking cloud 
within range. Those within the cloud must save vs END at the start 
of their turn or gained 4 drained conditions due to inhaling the 
disgusting smell. A successful save means that creature will never be 
affected by this cloud. The creature cannot fail this save more than 
once. The caster may move the cloud up to 10’ per round as their 
action. 
Upcasting: 
A character may spend additional FP on a successful cast of Reeking 
Fog. For every additional 2FP spent, the spell increases the radius 
created by an additional 5’. 
Sorcerous Change: 
A character may spend additional FP on a successful cast of Reeking 
Fog. They may spend 1FP to make the fog colourless (but not 
odourless). 
 
Serpent Sentinel (3FP Cost) 
Schools: Abjuration, Conjuration 
Components: V, G, M (a bronze band in the shape of a snake) 
Duration: 1 hour/caster’s level 
Range: Varies (see below) 
Spheres: Arcane, Divine 
A shimmering spirit serpent coils about the party’s camp, protecting 
them from harm. 
Casting: 
▶ Critical Failure: Fails, Forgotten. 
▶ Failure: Fails, Forgotten. 
▶ Success: The caster creates a serpent sentinel (see below) large 
enough to cover a 30’ radius. 
▶ Critical Success: The caster creates a serpent sentinel (see 
below) large enough to cover a 60’ radius. 
Upcasting: 
A character may spend additional FP on a successful cast of Serpent 
Sentinel. For every additional 2FP spent, they may give the 
sentinel’s attacks an additional point of piercing damage. 
 
Serpent Sentinel: 
The serpent sentinel is a spectral snake roughly 1’ in diameter that 
coils itself in a circle about an area of the casters choosing, such as 
around their camp. The snake never moves and cannot be 
commanded once summoned. However, any creature of small size 
or larger that enters the area surrounded by the serpent awakes it. 
The serpent makes one melee test at Journeyman (11) causing 4 
piercing damage on a successful hit before falling back asleep. The 
serpent may make a number of attacks this way equal to the caster’s 
level. After the attacks have been made the sentinel fades into mist.  
 
 
 
 
Sigil Seal Against Monsters (4FP Cost) 
Schools: Abjuration, Destruction 
Components: V, G 
Duration: 1 turn/caster’s level 
Range: 10’ 
Spheres: Arcane, Divine 
The caster traces a sigil on the door, forbidding any monstrous foe to 
pass through. 
Miscast: D4 
▶ 1-2: The spell is forgotten and cannot be re-cast under any 
circumstances for 1d6 days. 
▶ 3-4: The object that the caster was trying to put a sigil seal on 
dissolves into fine mist. 
Casting: 
▶ Critical Failure: Fails, Forgotten, Miscast. 
▶ Failure: Fails, Forgotten. 
▶ Success: The caster places a sigil seal upon a single door, 
archway, window or other portal. Any monster attempting to pass 
through the portal must make a hard (-3) save vs PER or be unable 
to pass through the portal. They may retake this save once per turn.  
▶ Critical Success: The caster places a sigil seal upon a single door, 
archway, window or other portal. Any monster attempting to pass 
through the portal must make a hard (-3) save vs PER or be unable 
to pass through the portal. They may never retake this save and must 
wait for the spell’s duration to end or for the dispel magic to remove 
the sigil seal. 
Upcasting: 
A character may spend additional FP on a successful cast of Sigil 
Seal Against Monsters. They may spend 3FP to increase the duration 
to 1 hour per level of the caster. 
 
Spectral Steel (4FP Cost) 
Schools: Conjuration, Illusion 
Components: V, G 
Duration: 1 round/caster’s level 
Range: Variable (see below) 
Spheres: Divine 
With an uttered prayer the cleric summons a spectral war hammer 
that strikes down their opponents. 
Miscast: D4 
▶ 1-2: Any weapon held by the caster glows white hot, melting or 
burning within 1d3 rounds. If the caster is still holding it at the end 
of that time, they take 3 fire damage. 
▶ 3-4: 2d3 spectral weapons fly out from the caster in random 
directions. Anyone in their path is subject to a melee attack test that 
causes 2 holy damage on a successful hit. 
Casting: 
▶ Critical Failure: Fails, Forgotten, Miscast. 
▶ Failure: Fails, Forgotten. 
▶ Success: The caster summons a spectral weapon (see below). 
Attacks made by the weapon use the caster’s melee skill. The 
damage caused by the weapon is 3 holy damage. 
▶ Critical Success: The caster summons 1d3 spectral weapons (see 
below). Attacks made by the weapon use the caster’s melee skill. 
The damage caused by the weapon is 5 holy damage. 
Upcasting: 
A character may spend additional FP on a successful cast of Spectral 
Steel. For every additional 4FP spent, the caster summons an 
addition spectral weapon (see below). 
 
Spectral Weapon: 
A spectral weapon appears within 30’ of the caster. It can take the 
form of any mundane weapon, such as a hammer or sword. Without 
using their action, the priest can command the weapon to move up 
to 30’ per round and it may make one attack per round. The weapon 
counts as magical. The attack test uses the caster’s melee skill. 
 
 


Magic and Spells 
46 
 
Suggestion (3FP Cost) 
Schools: Charm, Illusion 
Components: V, G. M (a small coin) 
Duration: Instant 
Range: 80’ 
Spheres: Arcane 
The caster’s voice becomes soothing and all consuming… 
Casting: 
▶ Critical Failure: Fails, Forgotten. 
▶ Failure: Fails, Forgotten. 
▶ Success: The caster may make a suggestion (see below)  of up 
to six levels’ worth of humanoids. Each creature affected may roll a 
save vs PER to resist. 
▶ Critical Success: The caster may charm (see below) up to 3 x 
caster’s level’s worth of humanoids. Each creature affected may roll 
a save vs PER to resist. Creatures of 2nd level or lower do not get a 
save. 
Upcasting: 
A character may spend additional FP on a successful cast of 
Suggestion. For every additional 1FP spent, they gain an additional 
level’s worth of creature to make a suggestion (see below) towards. 
They may only spend 6FP in this way. 
Sorcerous Change: 
A character may spend additional FP on a successful cast of 
Suggestion. They may spend 4FP to make the suggestion deadly to 
the target(s), such as ‘throw yourself out of the window’. 
 
Suggestion: 
The caster makes a suggestion to the target creature. The suggestion 
must be mostly mundane in nature, such as ‘move along’; ‘you don’t 
need to search us’; or ‘you should ignore what you have just seen’. 
Suggestions must not imperil the target physically and they will 
automatically ignore any suggestion such as ‘chop off your own 
hand’ or ‘throw yourself into the fireplace’.  
 
Web (4FP Cost) 
Schools: Abjuration, Divination 
Components: V, G, M (a piece of spiderweb) 
Duration: 1 round/caster’s level 
Range: 80’ 
Spheres: Arcane 
Thick ropes of webs appear in twisting masses across the battlefield. 
Miscast: D3 
▶ 1: The caster summons a mass of webs as success, but instead 
targets their nearest ally. Any creature in the webs must save vs STR 
or not be able to move. The webs are not flammable. 
▶ 2: A mass of webs appears centred on the caster in a 20’ radius 
(including where the caster is).  
▶ 3: The caster summons a horde of poisonous spiders, which 
arrives one round later and swarm across all nearby creatures within 
30’ radius of the caster’s current location, inflicting scores of bites. 
Anyone within the area at the end of the following round takes 4 
piercing damage which ignore armour saves. 
Casting: 
▶ Critical Failure: Fails, Forgotten, Miscast. 
▶ Failure: Fails, Forgotten. 
▶ Success: The caster sprays a single 15’x15’ surface with sticky 
webs. Any creature in the webs must save vs STR or not be able to 
move. The save may be repeated at the end of each round. The webs 
are not flammable. 
▶ Critical Success: The caster sprays up to eight points within range 
with web. Each point is covered in an area of 15’x15’ with sticky 
webs. Any creature in the webs must save vs STR or not be able to 
move. The save may be repeated at the end of each round. The webs 
are very dense and flammable and if set alight burn away over the 
course of 3 rounds, causing 2 fire damage to all within them per 
round. 
Upcasting: 
A character may spend additional FP on a successful cast of Web For 
every additional 2FP spent, the spell increases the area of the web 
by an additional 5’. 
Sorcerous Change:

For every additional 1FP spent, they may choose one creature in the 
targeted area to be unaffected by this casting. 
Wink (3FP Cost) 
Schools: Alteration 
Components: V, G 
Duration: Instant 
Range: 40’ 
Spheres: Arcane 
With a whispered word the caster ‘winks’ out of existence, appearing 
in the distance. 
Miscast: D4 
▶ 1-2: The spell transports the target to the intended destination but

▶ 3-4: The spell transports the target into a solid surface, make a

▶ Critical Failure: Fails, Forgotten, Miscast.

▶ Success: The caster or a willing creature within range is

▶ Critical success: The caster chooses up to ten willing creatures 
within range are transported up to 40 feet to a location the caster 
can see. 
Upcasting: 
A character may spend additional FP on a successful cast of Wink. 
For every additional 2FP spent, they may teleport and additional 
creature. 
Sorcerous Change: 
A character may spend additional FP on a successful cast of Wink. 
They may spend 2FP to hold this successful casting ‘in reserve’. At 
any time within 1 turn they may activate the wink for free, not 
casting an action, even if it is the enemy’s initiative, instantly

Wizard’s Seal (5FP Cost) 
Schools: Abjuration, Divination 
Components: V, G 
Duration: Varies (see below) 
Range: 10’ 
Spheres: Arcane 
The caster places a protective ward upon the door. Even the dragon 
will not be able to smash its way in now. 
Miscast: D4 
▶ 1-2: The spell is forgotten and cannot be re-cast under any

▶ 3-4: The object that the caster was trying to put a wizard’s seal on 
dissolves into fine mist. 
Casting: 
▶ Critical Failure: Fails, Forgotten, Miscast. 
▶ Failure: Fails, Forgotten. 
▶ Success: The caster places a wizard’s seal upon a single door,

▶ Critical Success: The caster places a wizard’s seal upon a single

freely. The wizard’s seal remains in place for 1 year per level of the 
caster.

Magic and Spells 
47 
 
3rd Level Spells 
Animate Corpse (7FP Cost) 
Schools: Necromancy 
Components: V, G, M (a corpse) 
Duration: Permanent 
Range: Touch 
Spheres: Arcane, Divine 
With a raise of their outstretched hands, the corpses of the caster’s 
enemies rise to do their master’s bidding in death. 
Miscast: D3 
▶ 1: 2d4 nearby corpses animate as zombies (see Bestiary) but they 
are under no one’s control. 
▶ 2: Rotten hands claw up out of the ground around the caster and 
claw the caster for 5 piercing damage. 
▶ 3: An angry soul appears in the form of a banshee (see Bestiary), 
disturbed that the caster has tried to force them back into their old 
body. The banshee is under no one’s control and hates the caster. 
Casting: 
▶ Critical Failure: Fails, Forgotten, Miscast. 
▶ Failure: Fails, Forgotten. 
▶ Success: The caster creates a single undead skeleton or zombie. 
The creature must have been medium sized humanoid or smaller 
beforehand. The creature is under the caster’s direct control and 
responds to their instructions to the best of its ability. 
▶Critical Success: The caster creates a number of undead skeletons 
or zombies of no greater than 3d6+Caster Level. The creatures do 
not need to have been humanoids and could be larger, such as 
dragons, giants and dinosaurs. The creature is under the caster’s 
direct control and responds to their instructions to the best of its 
ability. 
Upcasting: 
A character may spend additional FP on a successful cast of Animate 
Corpse. For every additional 2FP spent, the caster may animate an 
additional corpse. 
 
Charm Monster (6FP Cost) 
Schools: Charm, Illusion 
Components: V, G, M (a gilt ball on a chain) 
Duration: Varies (see below) 
Range: 80’ 
Spheres: Arcane, Divine 
Verdant charms of enchantment slither from the caster, seeking out 
the manticore’s eyes. 
Miscast: 
▶: The caster must immediately save vs PER or fall in love with one 
of the intended targets. 
Casting: 
▶ Critical Failure: Fails, Forgotten, Miscast. 
▶ Failure: Fails, Forgotten. 
▶ Success: The caster may charm (see below) up to 3d6 levels’ 
worth of monsters. Each creature affected may roll a save vs PER to 
resist. 
▶ Critical Success: The caster may charm (see below) up to 3 x 
caster’s level’s worth of monsters. Each creature affected may roll a 
save vs PER to resist. Creatures of 2nd level or lower do not get a 
save. 
Upcasting: 
A character may spend additional FP on a successful cast of Charm 
Monster. For every additional 2FP spent, they gain an additional 
level’s worth of creature to charm (see below). They may only spend 
12FP in this way. 
Sorcerous Change: 
A character may spend additional FP on a successful cast of Charm 
Monster. They may spend 1FP to make the charmed cr 
eature treat another creature as its friend (rather than the caster). 
Charmed: 
A creature that is charmed treats the caster as a friend and ally. They 
are not under the caster’s direct command but will generally heed 
the caster’s requests as would a close friend. A creature retests its 
Save vs PER based on its Intellect proficiency level: 
 
 
▶ Untrained: Once per month. 
▶ Trained and Journeyman: Once per week. 
▶ Expert: Once per day. 
▶ Master: Once per hour. 
▶ Grandmaster: Once per turn (10 minutes). 
 
▶ Charmed Creatures count as followers as per the rules for 
Personality. 
 
Confusion (5FP Cost)

Components: V, G, M (a small bag of marbles) 
Duration: Instant 
Range: 80’+ 
Spheres: Arcane 
With a slithering yellow light, the attacking bandits suddenly lose all 
sense of who they are, some maiming each other, and some stood 
about gormlessly. 
Miscast: 
▶: The caster must immediately save vs PER or be subjected to 
confusion (see below). 
Casting: 
▶ Critical Failure: Fails, Forgotten, Miscast. 
▶ Failure: Fails, Forgotten. 
▶ Success: The caster chooses up to three targets to save vs PER. 
If a target fails, then they are confused (see below). 
▶ Critical Success: The caster chooses up to six targets to save vs 
PER. If a target fails, then they are confused (see below). 
Upcasting: 
A character may spend additional FP on a successful cast of 
Confusion. For every additional 2FP spent, they may choose an 
additional target. 
Sorcerous Change: 
A character may spend additional 3FP on a successful cast of 
Confusion. If they do this, the target suffers a -3 penalty to its save 
vs PER to resist the effects of the spell. 
 
Confused: 
A creature that is confused may save vs PER at the end of each 
round to end the confusion. While confused, each round they do not 
behave as normal. Instead, roll a d6 on the table below to determine 
their action: 
Confusion 
d6 
Action 
1 
The subject attacks their friends if able. 
2 
The subject does nothing. 
3 
The subject does nothing. 
4 
The subject moves in a random direction. 
5 
The subject moves in a random direction. 
6 
The subject may act normally 
 


Magic and Spells 
48 
 
Control Person (8FP Cost) 
Schools: Charm, Illusion 
Components: V, G, M (a small piece of silver chain) 
Duration: Varies (see below) 
Range: 80’ 
Spheres: Arcane, Divine 
With a mouthed utterance, the heroic defenders turn their weapons 
upon their own people! 
Miscast: 
▶: The caster must immediately save vs PER or fall in love with one 
of the intended targets. 
Casting: 
▶ Critical Failure: Fails, Forgotten, Miscast. 
▶ Failure: Fails, Forgotten. 
▶ Success: The caster may control (see below) up to 4d6 levels’ 
worth of humanoids. Each creature affected may roll a save vs PER 
to resist. 
▶ Critical Success: The caster may control (see below) up to 5 x 
caster’s level’s worth of humanoids. Each creature affected may roll 
a save vs PER to resist. Creatures of 3rd level or less do not get a 
save. 
Upcasting: 
A character may spend additional FP on a successful cast of Control 
Person. For every additional 1FP spent, they gain an additional 
level’s worth of creature to control (see below). They may only 
spend 8FP in this way. 
Sorcerous Change: 
A character may spend additional FP on a successful cast of Control 
Person. They may spend 1FP to make the charmed creature treat 
another creature as its master (rather than the caster). 
Control: 
A creature that is controlled is slavishly under the command of the 
caster, completing any action that the caster decrees to the best of 
their ability, including suicidal and dangerous commands. A creature 
retests its Save vs PER based on its Intellect proficiency level: 
▶ Untrained: Once per week. 
▶ Trained and Journeyman: Once per day. 
▶ Expert: Once per hour. 
▶ Master: Once per turn (10 minutes). 
▶ Grandmaster: Once per round. 
▶ Controlled Creatures count as followers as per the rules for 
Personality. 
 
Dispel Magic (5FP Cost) 
Schools: Abjuration 
Components: V, G, M 
Duration: Instant 
Range: 10’ 
Spheres: Arcane, Bardic, Divine, Primal 
The caster places their hands on the object, causing its dweomer to 
fade and die. 
Miscast: D4 
▶1-2: Something goes terribly wrong, and the caster instantly forgets 
everything they know about magic for 1d4 days.  
▶3-4: The spell rebounds into the caster’s own head. They may not 
cast this spell for 1d4 days. 
Casting: 
▶ Critical Failure: Fails, Forgotten. 
▶ Failure: Fails, Forgotten. 
▶ Success: The caster may attempt to dispel the magic of one item, 
creature or spell-like effect with a base chance of 75%. (see Dispel 
Attempt below). 
▶ Critical Success: The caster may attempt to dispel the magic of 
one item, creature or spell-like effect with a base chance of 99%. (see 
Dispel Attempt below). 
Upcasting: 
A character may spend additional FP on a successful cast of Dispel 
Magic. For every additional 1FP spent, the base chance of the dispel 
increases by 5%, up to 99%. 
Dispel Attempt 
The caster chooses one magical item, creature, enchantment or spell-
like effect within range and attempts to dispel it. The base chance of 
successfully dispelling a spell is determined by the casting roll. A 
success nullifies the magic entirely. 
Enervate (7FP Cost) 
Schools: Destruction, Necromancy  
Components: V, G, M (a shard of ice) 
Duration: Instant 
Range: 30’ 
Spheres: Arcane, Divine 
With a bewitching bolt, the caster drains their foes life and vitality. 
Miscast:  
▶: The caster suffers 4 cold damage (which ignores armour saves), 
healing their intended target for 4HP. 
Casting: 
▶ Critical Failure: Fails, Miscast. 
▶ Failure: Fails, Forgotten. 
▶ Success: The caster chooses one target within range that must 
save vs PER. On a failure, the target suffers 4 cold damage. For 
every point of damage caused (after armour saves have been taken), 
the caster regains 1HP. 
▶ Critical Success: The caster chooses one target within range that 
must save vs PER. On a failure, the target suffers 10 cold damage. 
For every point of damage caused (after armour saves have been 
taken), the caster regains 1HP. 
Upcasting: 
A character may spend additional FP on a successful cast of 
Enervate. For every additional 2FP spent, the target suffers an 
additional 1 cold damage as described above. The caster also gains 
HP from this damage as described above. 
Sorcerous Change: 
A character may spend additional FP on a successful cast of 
Enervate. They may spend 2FP to make the target also gain a slowed 
condition or 1d4 stunned conditions. 
 
Fireball (6FP Cost) 
Schools: Conjuration, Destruction 
Components: V, G, M (a drop of bat guano and sulphur) 
Duration: Instant 
Range: Variable 
Spheres: Arcane 
The caster shoots an explosive ball of flaming death from their hands. 
Miscast: D4 
▶ 1: A small fireball explodes on the caster, causing 4 fire damage 
to the caster. 
▶ 2: A large fireball explodes centred on the caster, causing 1 x 
caster level fire damage to the caster and all creatures within 20’. 
▶ 3: The caster summons the wrong element, generating a spray of 
ice that showers everything within 20’ of the caster, causing no 
damage but potentially dousing any existing flames. 
▶ 4: The caster’s skin is badly sunburned across their entire body, 
and physical motion causes sharp pains (expressed as a -3 penalty 
to all tests until the sunburn heals after one week). 
Casting: 
▶ Critical Failure: Fails, Forgotten, Miscast. 
▶ Failure: Fails, Forgotten. 
▶ Success: The caster launches a fireball up to 100’, doing 8 fire 
damage to all creatures within a 20’ radius (save vs AGI halves). The 
caster chooses a point and must make a ranged attack test to hit 
that point. Failure to hit causes the fireball to scatter 1d8x5’ in a 
random direction before exploding. 
▶ Critical Success: The caster launches a fireball up to 100’, doing 
12 fire damage to all creatures within a 20’ radius (save vs AGI 
halves). In addition, each creature also takes 2 burning conditions. 
The caster chooses a point and the fireball hits unerringly, not 
requiring an attack test.  
Upcasting: 
A character may spend additional FP on a successful cast of Fireball. 
For every additional 2FP spent, the damage of the spell increases by 
1. 
Sorcerous Change: 
A character may spend additional FP on a successful cast of Fireball. 
They may spend 2FP to change the damage from fire to acid or 
lightning.

The caster traces a sigil on the doorway which explodes out when

▶ Critical Success: The caster places a glyph of warding upon a

with or attempts to open the object, the glyph of warding explodes

A character may spend additional FP on a successful cast of Glyph

Lightning Bolt (6FP Cost) 
Schools: Conjuration, Destruction 
Components: V, G, M (a vial full of iron shavings) 
Duration: Instant 
Range: Variable 
Spheres: Arcane 
A bolt of white lightning leaps from the wizard’s hand and bounces 
around the chamber, striking down everyone it touches. 
Miscast: D4 
▶1: The caster sends out a bolt of lightning 15’ long in a random 
direction. The bolt moves for 100’, bouncing off of objects and 
creatures. Creatures hit must take 4 lightning damage (save vs AGI

armour. 
▶2: The caster charges themselves with electrical energy. For one 
turn, anyone standing within 10’ of them and wearing metal armour

Casting: 
▶ Critical Failure: Fails, Forgotten, Miscast. 
▶ Failure: Fails, Forgotten. 
▶ Success: The caster releases a lightning bolt that travels out from

creature, it bounces off in a random direction. Any creature struck 
by the bolt suffers 6 lightning damage (save vs AGI halves) or 
double if they are wearing metal armour. 
▶ Critical Success: The caster releases a lightning bolt that travels 
out from them until the bolt has moved 180’. If the bolt hits an object 
or creature, it bounces off in a random direction. Any creature struck

Major Divination (7FP Cost) 
Schools: Divination 
Components: V, G 
Duration: 1 hour 
Range: Touch 
Spheres: Arcane 
The caster’s preternatural ability to foresee manifests itself as 
something close to the true manipulation of events. 
Casting:

▶ Success: The caster rolls 3d20 and records the three numbers. 
They may at any time within the next hour exchange any of the

of the values just rolled with the value of any test or save they 
witness (including their own, their allies and their enemy’s). The 
caster may only ever have six of these values on standby at once – 
casting the spell additional times replaces all of the values.

Polymorph (7FP Cost) 
Schools: Alteration 
Components: V, G, M (a chameleon’s tooth) 
Duration: 1 turn/caster’s level 
Range: Self or 30’ 
Spheres: Arcane 
The petulant child will regret trespassing in this garden, when he 
realises its wizard owner has transformed him into a frog. 
Miscast: 
▶1: The caster transforms themselves into a frog. This is a curse.  
▶2: The caster transforms themselves into a bat. This is a curse.  
▶3: The caster transforms themselves into an owl. This is a curse.  
▶4: The caster transforms themselves into a weasel. This is a curse.  
Casting: 
▶ Critical Failure: Fails, Forgotten, Miscast. 
▶ Failure: Fails, Forgotten. 
▶ Success: The caster or one target is transformed into any creature 
that the caster has seen previously. The target must be of the same 
level or lower than the caster. When in this new form, they retain 
their hit points, but their core attributes and skills change to that of 
the new form. All their equipment is transformed with them. The 
new form must of the same level of the caster or fewer. If the target 
is unwilling, then they may save vs PER to resist. If an unwilling 
target is transformed, then their equipment is not transformed with 
them. 
▶ Critical Success: The caster or one target is transformed into any 
creature that the caster has seen previously. When in this new form, 
they retain their hit points, but their core attributes and skills change 
to that of the new form. All their equipment is transformed with 
them. The new form must of the same level of the caster or fewer. 
If the target is unwilling, then they may make a hard (-3) save vs 
PER to resist. If an unwilling target is transformed, then their 
equipment is not transformed with them. 
 
Portal Through Artifice (6FP Cost) 
Schools: Alteration 
Components: V, G, M (a small key) 
Duration: 1 round/caster’s level 
Range: Touch 
Spheres: Arcane, Divine 
The caster traces a circle on the wall, which shimmers, revealing a 
tunnel into the castle. 
Miscast: 
▶: The spell doesn’t work properly. A six-foot wide, ten foot deep 
hole is gouged in the intended wall, possibly causing a structural 
collapse. 
Casting: 
▶ Critical Failure: Fails, Forgotten, Miscast. 
▶ Failure: Fails. 
▶ Success: The caster creates a hole in a manufactured wall that is 
six feet in diameter. The hole is magically maintained and acts like 
a portal from one side of the wall to the other. When the spell ends, 
the wall is undamaged, returning to its original shape. The wall must 
have been manufactured, such as a timber, wattle and daub or stone 
wall and it must be no thicker than ten feet. 
▶ Critical Success: The caster creates a hole in a manufactured wall 
that is ten feet in diameter. The hole is magically maintained and 
acts like a portal from one side of the wall to the other. When the 
spell ends, the wall is undamaged, returning to its original shape. 
The wall must have been manufactured, such as a timber, wattle and 
daub or stone wall and it must be no thicker than twenty feet. 
Upcasting: 
A character may spend additional FP on a successful cast of Portal 
through Artifice. If the caster spends an additional 2FP, the duration 
is increased to one turn per caster’s level. 
Sorcerous Change: 
A character may spend additional FP on a successful cast of Portal 
Through Artifice. They may spend 2FP to allow the portal to be 
opened in natural obstacles as well as manufactured ones. 
 
 
 
 
 
 
Protection from the Elements (6FP Cost) 
Schools: Alteration 
Components: V, G, M (a damp drop of ashy paste) 
Duration: 1 round/caster’s level 
Range: 60’ 
Spheres: Arcane, Divine 
The evil druid summons lightning bolts from the sky which bounce 
off the caster harmlessly. 
Miscast: D4 
▶ 1: The caster summons a lightning bolt directly onto their head, 
suffering 12 lightning damage. 
▶ 2-4: Elemental energy courses out of the eyes of caster, melting 
out their eyes (and causing 5 fire damage). Additionally, all creatures 
within 30’ of the caster must save vs AGI or take 5 lightning damage. 
Casting: 
▶ Critical Failure: Fails, Forgotten, Miscast. 
▶ Failure: Fails, Forgotten. 
▶ Success: The caster gains Elemental Resistance (See below), as 
do all allied creatures within 10’ of the caster. 
▶ Critical Success: The caster gains Elemental Immunity (See 
below), as do all allied creatures within 15’ of the caster. 
Upcasting: 
A character may spend additional FP on a successful cast of 
Protection from the Elements. For every additional 2FP spent, 
increase the radius affected by 5’. 
Sorcerous Change: 
A character may spend additional FP on a successful cast of 
Protection from the Elements. They may spend 2FP to change the 
spell to produce to the following effect, instead of its usual effect: 
The caster creates a 15’ hemisphere centred on themselves that lasts 
for 1 turn/caster’s level. While in the hemisphere, all rain, snow, 
sleet and other unpleasant meteorological effects do not enter the 
hemisphere. The temperature within the hemisphere maintains a 
comfortable 18°C. 
 
Elemental Resistance: 
▶ Damage Type: The caster chooses one type of damage this spell 
will confer protection from: cold, fire, or lightning.  
▶ Save bonus: All saves verses the chosen elemental-based damage, 
even if it is magical, are made as easy (+3) tests. 
▶ Elemental damage: The creature gains resistance to their chosen 
damage type. 
Elemental Immunity: 
▶ Damage Type: The caster chooses one type of damage this spell 
will confer protection from: cold, fire, or lightning.  
▶ Save Success: All saves versus the chosen elemental-based 
damage, even if it is magical, are automatically passed. 
▶ Elemental damage: Affected creatures are immune to the chosen 
type of damage.

Tickle of Death (5FP Cost) 
Schools: Destruction 
Components: V, G 
Duration: Permanent 
Range: 30’ 
Spheres: Arcane 
The orc laughs. And laughs. And laughs. And then he dies. 
Miscast D3: 
▶1: The caster laughs themselves to death in 1d4 rounds.  
▶2-3: One random ally within 30’ is made the subject of this spell,

▶ Critical Failure: Fails, Forgotten, Miscast. 
▶ Failure: Fails, Forgotten.

Vessel of Mortality Circumvention (6FP Cost) 
Schools: Abjuration Necromancy 
Components: V, G, M (a small container such as a jar) 
Duration: Permanent 
Range: 30’ 
Spheres: Arcane, Divine 
The caster’s soul leaves their body, finding a hiding spot in a jar. 
Miscast: 
▶: The spell fails catastrophically. The caster is disembodied and has 
the statistics of a ghost. They have 1 year and a day to find a new 
body for the caster or their soul is destroyed. 
Casting: 
▶ Critical Failure: Fails, Forgotten, Miscast. 
▶ Failure: Fails, Forgotten. 
▶ Success: The caster’s soul leaves their body and enters a nearby 
container such as a lamp, jug, jar or box. Their body dies. While in 
this vessel the caster can do nothing apart from speak telepathically 
to any creature within range. They can also attempt to leave the jar, 
by entering an empty body within 30’ (such as recently deceased 
body). If the body is inhabited by somebody else, then the caster 
must succeed an opposed PER vs PER test to successfully enter the 
body. If they are successful, they enter the body, but the previous 
soul is not removed. Each day, the opposed test is repeated with a 
success indicating that that the caster has control of the body, and a 
failure indicating that the previous occupant has control. 
▶ Critical Success: The caster’s soul leaves their body and enters a 
nearby container such as a lamp, jug, jar or box. Their body dies. 
While in this vessel the caster can do nothing apart from speak 
telepathically to any creature within range. They can also attempt to 
leave the jar, entering an empty body within 30’ at will (such as 
recently deceased body). If the body is inhabited by somebody else, 
then the caster suppresses the original inhabitant and takes full 
control of the body. 
Upcasting: 
A character may spend additional FP on a successful cast of Vessel 
of Mortality Circumvention. For every additional 1FP spent, the 
range of the spell increases by 10’. 
Sorcerous Change: 
A character may spend additional FP on a successful cast of Vessel 
of Mortality Circumvention. They may spend 3FP to instantly jump 
their soul from one body to another, not requiring them to enter a 
vessel first. If the body already contains a soul, then follow the rules 
for what happens in such a situation described above.

Components: V, G, M (a concave glass gem)

tornadoes. The hut takes on the appearance and architecture of the 
area that it is in, as not to provoke attention. 
▶ Critical Success: The caster summons a three-story house with 
one door. The size of the building is roughly 40’x80’x60’. Within the 
house are various parlours, including bedrooms with beds enough 
for 20 humanoids. Additionally, there is a dining room table and a 
clockwork butler who has the resources to prepare a fine meal for 
up to 20 people. The area within the house is always comfortable 
for those within and can withstand any sort of mundane weather 
effect, even earthquakes, hurricanes, and tornadoes. The house takes 
on the appearance and architecture of the area that it is in, as not to 
provoke attention. In addition, the house is impervious to all non-
magical attacks.

4th Level Spells 
Chain Lightning (10FP Cost) 
Schools: Conjuration, Destruction 
Components: V, G, M (an iron tuning fork) 
Duration: Instant 
Range: Variable 
Spheres: Arcane 
Lightning flickers from foe to foe, leaving a seared corpse and the 
stench of ozone as it does. 
Miscast: D4 
▶1: The caster shoots a bolt of lightning from their hand, hitting the 
nearest creature within 30’. The target suffers 8 lightning damage. 
The target may save vs END to halve the damage. After this, the 
chain lightning leaps to another target within 15’ of the first and rolls 
a d4. On any result but a 1, the new target suffers 8 lightning 
damage. The target may save vs END to halve the damage. This 
process may be repeated until the d4 comes up as a 1 or until there 
are no available targets left. When this happens, the lightning bolt 
flickers out. 
▶2: The caster charges themselves with electrical energy. For one 
turn, anyone standing within 10’ of them and wearing metal armour 
must save vs AGI or take 2 lightning damage every round. 
▶3: The caster zaps themselves for 6 lightning damage. 
▶4: The caster zaps themselves and every creature within 25’ for 6 
lightning damage (save vs AGI haves). 
Casting: 
▶ Critical Failure: Fails, Forgotten, Miscast. 
▶ Failure: Fails, Forgotten. 
▶ Success: The caster shoots a blast of lightning from their hand, 
hitting a target within 30’. The target suffers 8 lightning damage. The 
target may save vs END to halve the damage. After this, the caster 
chooses another target, within 15’ of the first and rolls a d4. On any 
result but a one, the new target suffers 8 lightning damage. The 
target may save vs END to halve the damage. This process may be 
repeated as many times as the caster wishes, or until the d4 comes 
up as a 1. When this happens, the lightning bolt flickers out. The 
same creature cannot be targeted more than once. 
▶ Critical Success: The caster shoots a blast of lightning from their 
hand, hitting a target within 40’. The target suffers 12 lightning 
damage. The target may save vs END to halve the damage. After 
this, the caster chooses another target, within 15’ of the first and rolls 
a d6. On any result but a one, the new target suffers 12 lightning 
damage. The target may save vs END to halve the damage. This 
process may be repeated as many times as the caster wishes, or until 
the d6 comes up as a 1. When this happens, the lightning bolt 
flickers out. The same creature cannot be targeted more than once. 
Upcasting: 
A character may spend additional FP on a successful cast of Chain 
Lightning. For every additional 2FP spent, the damage of the spell 
increases by 1. 
Sorcerous Change: 
A character may spend additional FP on a successful cast of Chain 
Lightning. They may spend 2FP to change the damage from 
lightning to acid, cold or fire. 
 
 
 
Cloud of Demise (10FP Cost) 
Schools: Conjuration, Destruction 
Components: V, G, M (a sliver of yew and a piece of wool) 
Duration: 1 round/caster’s level 
Range: 120’ 
Spheres: Arcane 
An undulating cloud of horrid death spreads across the battlefield,

▶3: The caster’s transforms part of their bloodstream into a stinging

saves.

▶ Critical Failure: Fails, Forgotten, Miscast. 
▶ Failure: Fails, Forgotten. 
▶ Success: The caster summons a 30’x30’ cloud of demise within

damage ignores all armour saves. A creature in the cloud of demise

▶ Critical Success: The caster summons a 60’x 60’ cloud of demise 
within range. Those within the cloud must save vs END at the start 
of their turn or suffer 6 poison damage due to inhaling to vapours. 
This damage ignores all armour saves. A creature in the cloud of 
demise must retake this save every round.

Components: V, G 
Duration: Permanent

Spheres: Arcane, Divine

rapture as a door grows and fits itself back into the frame perfectly.

▶3: The caster is cursed. Each non-living, inanimate item they hold

mist.  
Casting: 
▶ Critical Failure: Fails, Forgotten, Miscast. 
▶ Failure: Fails, Forgotten. 
▶ Success: The caster magically creates one item out of an organic

▶ Critical Success: The caster magically creates one item out of an

Felicitous Sanctuary (SPECIAL FP Cost) 
Schools: Alteration 
Components: V, G, M (special, see below) 
Duration: Permanent 
Range: 6 miles per FP (see below) 
Spheres: Arcane, Divine 
As the orcs closed in on all sides, the wizard thought it was time to 
get herself, and her friends, out of this mess. 
Casting: 
▶ Critical Failure: Fails, Forgotten. 
▶ Failure: Fails, Forgotten. 
▶ Success: The caster either creates a Felicitous Sanctuary or 
retreats to one (see below). 
▶ Critical success: The caster either creates a Felicitous Sanctuary 
or retreats to one (see below). 
Upcasting: 
A character may spend additional FP on a successful cast of 
Felicitous Sanctuary. For every additional 2FP spent the caster may 
teleport an additional willing creature to their sanctuary when 
retreating to it. 
 
Creating a Felicitous Sanctuary: 
When creating a felicitous sanctuary, the caster consumes a jewel 
worth at least 1000gp and spends 9FP. They then designate a place 
their sanctuary, as determined by the magic sphere through which 
they cast this spell: 
▶ Arcane: The sanctuary must be a wizard’s tower, library or other 
location deemed to be the reasonable residence of an arcane caster, 
such as a guild hall.  
▶ Divine: The sanctuary must be a chapel or temple of some kind, 
with a dedication to their patron deity. 
▶ Primal: The sanctuary must be a hidden druid’s grove or a series 
of standing stones. 
 
The caster may only ever have one sanctuary ‘active’ at once, and if 
they create a new one, their old ceases to function as a sanctuary. 
 
Retreating to a Felicitous Sanctuary: 
When the caster wishes to retreat to their sanctuary, they must 
successfully cast this spell. The cost in FP is 1 per 12 miles from 
the sanctuary when the spell is cast. If the spell is successfully cast, 
the caster teleports themselves and up to six other willing creatures 
immediately to their sanctuary. 
 
 
 
Levitate (9FP Cost) 
Schools: Alteration 
Components: V, G, M (the wing feather of any bird) 
Duration: 1 turn/caster’s level 
Range: Variable (see below) 
Spheres: Arcane 
The caster’s feet lift daintily from the ground and soon enough the 
wizard is running about the woods amongst the boughs and birds.

▶1: The caster is thrown 1d10 x 10’ into the air in a high arc before 
beginning to fall back to the ground, taking 2 bludgeoning damage 
per 10’ fallen. 
▶2: The spell works incorrectly. In a 20’ radius centred on the caster; 
the gravity is reversed for 1d6 rounds. Characters fall upwards at a 
rate of 20’/round. If the caster moves, the radius does not move with 
them. 
▶3: The caster causes all creatures within 20’ to gain a fly speed of 
10’/round. This effect lasts for 1 turn. 
Casting: 
▶ Critical Failure: Fails, Forgotten, Miscast. 
▶ Failure: Fails, Forgotten. 
▶ Success: The caster gains a fly speed of 30’/round. 
▶ Critical Success: The caster chooses up to six targets. Each target 
gains a fly speed of 30’/round. 
Upcasting: 
A character may spend additional FP on a successful cast of Levitate. 
For every additional 2FP spent, the caster grants the fly speed to an 
additional creature. 
Sorcerous Change: 
A character may spend additional FP on a successful cast of Levitate. 
For every additional 2FP spent, the fly speed granted to the target is 
increased by 10’.

▶ Critical Success: The caster casts three wishes (see below). 
 
Wish: 
The casting of a wish is a campaign altering moment. The caster may 
conceivably ask for anything and at this moment, a dialogue must 
be opened between the game master and the player. A game master 
is well-within their rights to reject any wish (as the will of the gods) 
that they believe will totally annihilate the campaign in a way that is 
not fun for the other players. However, this spell is in the game for 
a reason: players should be able to cast wishes should they have 
access to them. The game master should do their best to 
accommodate the desires of the caster and should not be capricious 
or arbitrarily misunderstand the specific wordings of the wish, 
especially when the intent of the caster is clear. 
 
Word of Death (13FP Cost) 
Schools: Alteration 
Components: V 
Duration: Permanent 
Range: 120’ 
Spheres: Arcane, Divine 
With a pointed finger, the lich utters a power word of reality, 
compelling the death of their target. 
Miscast: 
▶: The caster dies, unless undead. 
Casting: 
▶ Critical Failure: Fails, Forgotten, Miscast. 
▶ Failure: Fails, Forgotten, Miscast. 
▶ Success: The caster chooses one target within range that they can 
see. The target must save vs END or die. If they are 10th level or 
lower, they automatically fail this save. 
▶ Critical Success: The caster chooses one target within range that 
they can see. The target must save vs END or die. If they are 20th 
or lower, they automatically fail this save. 
 
 
 


60 
Deities and Prayers
Many fantasy campaigns will include the presence of real gods, 
whose powers can be called upon. The Cleric and Paladin talent 
trees are evidence of this. Here you will find details on how to 
mediate pray tests, and the sorts of boons a god may grant a 
character who is suitably devout. 
Pray Tests 
Pray is one of the core skills of Advanced Fantasy and when a 
character makes a pray test, the game master should consult the 
table below to mediate the outcome based on the pray proficiency 
level of the character. A character can never gain the benefits of a 
pray test simultaneously to the benefits of another pray test. If they 
would be subject to the benefit of two benefits at once, the previous 
one immediately ceases. Whenever a character is asked to choose an 
ally as a result of a pray test, they may instead choose themselves. 
A generic deity is provided below, as well as a series of example 
deities that game masters can adapt to suit their game worlds. 
Patron Deities 
When a character is first created, their player should choose a patron 
deity. This is the deity that they feel that their character most closely 
identifies with. All pray tests when praying to their patron deity are 
made as challenging (+0). If a character wishes to pray to a deity 
other than their patron, then the pray test is made as very hard  
(-6). 
Listed Gods 
All the gods below are given as examples. Game masters are advised 
to use these to devise gods specific to their own campaigns as 
needed.

65 
Runes and Runesmithing
Runes are signs and sigils traced with such exquisite precision that 
they unlock the fundamental power of the universe. A skill coveted 
by dwarfs and dragonfolk, few have the craftsmanship to smith 
runes. 
Tracing Runes 
Runes are ultimately very complex symbols and are ‘traced’ onto 
objects such as weapons, armour and stones. ‘Tracing’ for a rune is 
what ‘casting’ is for a spell and the core skill used in tracing runes 
is Craft (Runes). 
Reading Rune Entries 
When a rune is traced using a successful Craft (Runes) test, the 
runesmith consults the rune’s entry in this chapter. Runes can be 
traced on the following objects, which can result in different effects 
being produced by the same rune. 
▶Armour: This is the effect the rune has when traced on armour.
A piece of armour may only have one active rune on it at once.
▶Weapon: This is the effect the rune has when traced on a weapon.
A weapon may only have one active rune on it at once.
▶Stone: This is the effect the rune has when traced on a small stone
known as a rune stone. The cost of rune stones is listed in the
Equipment, Gear and Services section of this book.
▶Flesh: This is the effect the rune has when traced on living flesh.
A living creature may only have one active rune on it at once.
Rune Magic 
All stones, armour and weapons with a rune traced on them count 
as magical. Flesh does not, however. 
Active Runes 
When a creature successfully traces a rune, they have poured a little 
bit of their essence into the creation of the runic power. A character 
therefore may only ever have one rune active at once. Were a 
character to trace a new rune, then their previous one would lose its 
power. Certain talents allow a character to have more than one active 
rune.  
Active Runes on Stones 
Stones are meant to contain runes. Therefore, a single rune may be 
traced up to six times onto a set of six rune stones. This still only 
counts as having one rune active. 
Rune Descriptions 
Petty Rune Descriptions 
Petty Rune of Battle 
▶Armour: When traced on armour, this rune grants the armour a
6+ power save. If the armour already has a power save, then this
rune has no effect.
▶Weapon: When traced on a weapon, the weapon deals critical hits
on a 19 as well as a 20. If a character already has a talent that allows
them to do this, then the rune has no effect.
▶Stone: When this rune is traced onto a stone, that stone allows
the bearer to reroll 1 failed attack test, after which the stone
crumbles to dust.
▶Flesh: This costs 2FP to trace on flesh. When traced on living
flesh, the creature may move away from enemies without provoking
an attack of opportunity at the cost of 1FP.
Petty Rune of Beasts 
▶Armour: When traced on armour, the wearer’s gains a +3 bonus
to all Animal Handling tests.
▶Weapon: When traced on a weapon, the wielder may freely
choose the weapon’s damage type from bludgeoning, piercing or
slashing.
▶Stone: When this rune is traced onto a stone, that stone allows
the bearer to summon one wolf which is under their command, after
which the stone crumbles to dust. The wolf counts toward the
character’s follower limit.
▶Flesh: When traced on living flesh, the creature gains natural
claws, which are natural weapons dealing 2 slashing damage.
Petty Rune of Blood 
▶Armour: When traced on armour, this rune reduces all incoming
slashing, piercing or bludgeoning damage by 1, to a minimum of
one.
▶Weapon: When traced on a weapon, a character may spend 2FP
to activate this rune after a successful attack test to deal an
additional 2 damage.
▶Stone: When this rune is traced onto a stone, the stone may be
held to a wound, allowing the bearer to restore 3HP at the cost of
3FP, after which the stone crumbles to dust.
▶Flesh: This costs 2FP to trace on flesh. When traced on living
flesh, the creature gains 1 bleeding condition. If the creature is
unwilling, then a Craft (Runes) attack test is required.
Petty Rune of Corrosion 
▶Armour: When traced on armour, this rune reduces all incoming
acid damage by 1, to a minimum of one.
▶Weapon: When traced on a weapon, this rune causes the weapon
to deal 1 additional point of acid damage on a successful attack.
▶Stone: When this rune is traced onto a stone, that stone causes 3
acid damage if thrown at a target within 30’.
▶Flesh: When traced on living flesh, the creature smells faintly of
vinegar and can instantly detect if the air of a place is poisonous.
Petty Rune of Fire 
▶Armour: When traced on armour, this rune reduces all incoming
fire damage by 1, to a minimum of one.
▶Weapon: When traced on a weapon, this rune causes the weapon
to deal 1 additional point of fire damage on a successful attack.
▶Stone: When this rune is traced onto a stone, that stone causes 3
fire damage if thrown at a target within 30’. 
▶Flesh: When traced on living flesh, the creature becomes immune
to the effects of cold weather and always feels pleasantly warm.


Runes and Runesmithing 
66 
 
Petty Rune of Light 
▶ Armour: When traced on armour, this rune causes the armour to 
produce bright light in a radius of 30’ at the wearer’s command. 
▶ Weapon: When traced on a weapon, the wielder of the weapon 
may choose to choose to spend 6FP to emit a blast of dazzling light. 
All enemies within 30’ of the wielder must save vs PER or be 
blinded for 1d4 rounds.  
▶ Stone: When this rune is traced onto a stone, that stone causes a 
target to gain 2 stunned conditions if thrown at a target within 30’. 
▶ Flesh: When traced on living flesh, the creature gains infravision 
up to 50’. It has no effect on creatures that already have infravision.  
 
Petty Rune of Ice 
▶ Armour: When traced on armour, this rune reduces all incoming 
cold damage by 1, to a minimum of one. 
▶ Weapon: When traced on a weapon, this rune causes the weapon 
to deal 1 additional point of cold damage on a successful attack. 
▶ Stone: When this rune is traced onto a stone, that stone causes 3 
cold damage if thrown at a target within 30’. 
▶ Flesh: When traced on living flesh, the creature becomes immune 
to the effects of extremely warm weather and always feels pleasantly 
cool.  
 
Petty Rune of Nature 
▶ Armour: When traced on armour, this rune gives the wearer a +3 
bonus to all Knowledge (Nature) tests. 
▶ Weapon: When traced on a weapon, this rune allows the bearer 
to spend 2FP to transform the weapon into a living vine on a 
successful attack test. This vine is strong enough to hold a character 
and be climbed up. If used on an enemy of medium size or smaller, 
the enemy must save vs AGI or become entangled and helpless in 
the vine. A target of 8th level or higher cannot be entangled this way. 
▶ Stone: When this rune is traced onto a stone, a creature holding 
the stone may spend 1FP to dissolve the stone into a vessel of water. 
After 1 turn (ten minutes), the vessel dissolves into water too. 
▶ Flesh: When traced on living flesh, the creature may spend 3FP 
when lost in the wilderness to immediately find their way.  
 
Petty Rune of Radiance 
▶ Armour: When traced on armour, this rune reduces all incoming 
holy damage by 1, to a minimum of one. 
▶ Weapon: When traced on a weapon, this rune causes the weapon 
to deal 1 additional point of holy damage on a successful attack. 
▶ Stone: When this rune is traced onto a stone, that stone causes 3 
holy damage if thrown at a target within 30’. 
▶ Flesh: This costs 2FP to trace on flesh. While traced on flesh, the 
creature gains +3 bonus to Healing tests while the rune is active. 
 
Petty Rune of Serpents 
▶ Armour: When traced on armour, this rune reduces all incoming 
poison damage by 1, to a minimum of one. 
▶ Weapon: When traced on a weapon, this rune causes the weapon 
to deal 1 additional point of poison damage on a successful attack. 
▶ Stone: When this rune is traced onto a stone, that stone causes 3 
poison damage if thrown at a target within 30’. 
▶ Flesh: This costs 2FP to trace on flesh. While traced on flesh, the 
creature may talk to snakes in a language that they understand. 
 
Petty Rune of Shadows 
▶ Armour: When traced on armour, this rune reduces all incoming 
necrotic damage by 1, to a minimum of one. 
▶ Weapon: When traced on a weapon, this rune causes the weapon 
to deal 1 additional point of necrotic damage on a successful attack. 
▶ Stone: When this rune is traced onto a stone, that stone causes 3 
necrotic damage if thrown at a target within 30’. 
▶ Flesh: This costs 2FP to trace on flesh. While traced on flesh, the 
creature gains +3 bonus to Stealth tests while the rune is active. 
 
 
 
 
 
 
Petty Rune of Storms 
▶ Armour: When traced on armour, this rune reduces all incoming 
lightning damage by 1, to a minimum of one. 
▶ Weapon: When traced on a weapon, this rune causes the weapon 
to deal 1 additional point of lightning damage on a successful attack. 
▶ Stone: When this rune is traced onto a stone, that stone causes 3 
lightning damage if thrown at a target within 30’. 
▶ Flesh: When traced on living flesh, the creature’s hair stands on 
end and they can detect the presence of any metal within 120’, 
including the nature of the metal, such as a gold ring or iron rod.  
 
Petty Rune of Succour 
▶ Armour: When traced on armour, the wearer’s natural healing 
rate is increased to 2HP per day. 
▶ Weapon: When traced on a weapon, this rune gives the wielder 
+1FP per day. 
▶ Stone: When this rune is traced onto a stone, the stone may be 
used to restore 2FP, after which the stone crumbles to dust. 
▶ Flesh: When traced on living flesh, the creature removes 4 
drained conditions per extended rest.  
 
Petty Rune of Swiftness 
▶ Armour: When traced on armour, the wearer’s speed increases by 
10’ per round. 
▶ Weapon: When traced on a weapon, this rune gives the wielder 
+1 to attack tests with the weapon. 
▶ Stone: When this rune is traced onto a stone, that stone causes 2 
bludgeoning damage if thrown at a target within 30’. Ranged attack 
tests with the stone have a +3 bonus. 
▶ Flesh: When traced on living flesh, the creature halves the amount 
of time required for an extended rest. 
 
Petty Rune of Trickery 
▶ Armour: When traced on armour, this rune gives the wearer a +3 
bonus to all Persuasion tests. 
▶ Weapon: When traced on a weapon, this rune causes the weapon 
to deal 0 damage, while still seemingly completely normal. 
▶ Stone: When this rune is traced onto a stone, that stone produces 
a 10’ radius puff of smoke that lasts 1 turn (10 minutes) when thrown 
at a place within 30’. 
▶ Flesh: This costs 2FP to trace on flesh. When the rune is initially 
traced, the creature may choose to alter their entire face to another 
of their species. This cannot be used to mimic another specific 
creature, it merely changes the perceived superficial appearance 
such as eye colour, facial features, age, etc. 


67 
Equipment, Gear and Services
Below is a guide to all of the items typically found in a campaign of 
Advanced Fantasy, as well as full rules on how to use them in the 
game. 
Coins and Treasure 
Coins are abundant in Advanced Fantasy, having been left over from 
countless ancient empires. Coins form vast treasure hoards which 
are left at the bottom of forgotten caverns or make up the beds of 
avaricious dragons.  
Coins can be subdivided into several denominations in Advanced 
Fantasy: 
▶A copper piece (cp) is worth 1/100 of a gold piece.
▶A silver piece (sp) is worth 1/10 of a gold piece.
▶An electrum piece (ep) is worth ½ of a gold piece.
▶A gold piece (gp).
▶A platinum piece (pp) is worth 10gp.
A coin or jewel, such as a diamond, can be stacked into groups of 
100 per carry slot of encumbrance. 
Lodgings and Services 
Living Costs 
All characters must play living costs. The rules below determine the 
cost of specific items and services. In general, however, a character 
must spend the following on their living costs: 
▶1gp x their level per day.
This useful abstraction helps game masters calculate quickly how 
much a player character should be spending. 
The justification for the increase with level is that while characters 
start out inexperienced, ill-equipped (and poor), they eventually 
become more competent, successful (and rich). A level 1 character 
who specialises in fighting is going to be paying for cheap ale and 
will be sleeping in common rooms. They will also probably clean 
their armour and maintain their weapons themselves. A similar 
character who is level 5 will be consuming better ale, eating better 
food, sleeping in better lodgings and might even be paying a retainer 
to maintain their adventuring equipment. As characters gain more 
experience and money, it’s only natural that they generally expect 
more, and therefore their living costs increase. 
This is also a useful way for a game master to maintain a healthy 
way of slowly draining a party’s funds, giving them reason to 
continue adventuring.
