▶Alchemical Components. A pouch containing 100gp’s worth of
alchemical components, used for crafting potions, poisons and the
like.
▶Arrow, Quiver. A quiver containing arrows. Can be used as
ammunition for bows. They have a d8 as the ammo die.
▶Backpack. A leather backpack that can be worn without issue.
Provides 4 extra carrying capacity slots without taking up one itself.
▶Bedroll. A bedroll with padded lining and a pillow. Can be used
to provide some comfort when out adventuring. 
▶Bolt, Quiver. A quiver containing bolts. Can be used as
ammunition for crossbows. They have a d8 as the ammo die.
▶Caltrops. Curiously designed iron spikes, roughly 3” in diameter.
No matter how they are thrown, they always land with a spike 
pointing up. Can be used as makeshift defences and are particularly 
useful against charging foes. 
▶Crowbar. Sometimes called a prybar, a crowbar is a hardened
piece of steel that’s used to pry open doors and other sealed areas. 
▶Garlic. A common herb often found growing wild in the woods.
It is useful for adventurers who may find themselves fighting certain
undead, such as vampires.
▶ Grappling Hook. A three-pronged steel hook with a hoop at one
end for a rope. Grappling hooks are useful when casting a rope up 
a cliff or other sheer surface. 
▶Formula Book. A manuscript book used by enchanters and
alchemists to transcribe their formulae into. 
▶Holy Water. A vial of water blessed by a priest. Holy water has
many uses, but to certain monstrous horrors, such as vampires and
werewolves, holy water has a caustic, deeply corrosive effect.
▶Iron Spikes. Iron spikes have a multitude of uses that cannot be
comprehensively covered here. They are, however, clearly useful 
when climbing and when used to seal a door shut in a pinch. 
▶ Lantern, Bullseye. Bullseye lanterns are very useful as their light
shines out in a 40’ cone from one side of the lantern. This cone can
be shut off at a moment’s notice with an in-built sliding mechanism.
A bullseye lantern consumes 1 pint of oil of every two hours (12
turns).


Equipment, Gear and Services 
68 
 
▶ Lantern, Hooded. Hooded lanterns are used to create light that 
shines out in a 20’ radius from all sides of the lantern side of the 
lantern. This light can be shut off at a moment’s notice with an in-
built sliding mechanism. A hooded lantern consumes 1 pint of oil of 
every two hours (12 turns). 
▶ Mirror. A small handheld mirror is useful for seeing round 
corners, especially when what might be round there could have a 
petrifying  gaze, such as a basilisk or gorgon. 
▶ Oil. A single pint of oil can be used to power a lantern. It may be 
spilled on the ground or used to cover a weapon. Lantern oil is 
flammable (though not particularly explosive). 
▶ Pole, 10’. A ten-foot pole is incredibly useful. It can be used to 
vault over pits, probe for traps or any other multitude of activities. 
▶ Powder horn and shot. Powder and shot for blackpowder 
weapons. They have a d8 as the ammo die. 
▶ Rations. A mixture of hard tack, cheese, oatmeal and salted meat. 
A character can sustain themselves off a single ration per day. 
▶ Rope (Hempen). A hemp rope is sturdy and has many uses such 
as in climbing, tying up prisoners or swinging from roof beams. 
▶ Rope (Silk). A silk rope has all the functionality of a hemp rope 
but is much, much stronger. 
▶ Rune Stone. Can have runes transcribed on them for various 
magical powers (see Runes and Runesmithing section of this book). 
▶ Sack (Small). A cloth bag that can hold up to 4 carrying capacity 
slots worth of items. However, when it contains any items, it must 
be carried with two hands. 
▶ Sack (Large). A cloth bag that can hold up to 8 carrying capacity 
slots worth of items. However, when it contains any items, it must 
be carried with two hands. 
▶ Spell Book. A manuscript book used by some spellcasters to 
transcribe their spells into. 
▶ Spell Components. Can be used to cast spells where M (material) 
is listed as a component. Stands in for any material component that 
does not have a listed gp value. 
▶ Tent. A large tent used in camping to protect individuals from the 
harshness of weather. Up to two people may sleep in a single tent. 
▶ Thieves’ Tools. A collection of picks, breaks, tongs, needles, and 
other equipment in a rollout case. Can be used to pick locks and 
disable traps. 
▶ Tinder Box. A small wooden case containing dried tinder as well 
as a piece of flint and striking steel. Can be used to start fires. 
▶ Torches. A length of wood crowned with fabric soaked in pitch. 
When lit, torches provide a light in a 30’ radius and burn out after 
one hour. 
▶ Waterskin. A treated leather bag which serves as a vessel for 
water. Extremely useful when travelling in arid climes. 
 
Ammunition 
Tracking every single crossbow bolt and arrow can be onerous. 
Therefore, you do not track ammunition per shot, instead after every 
combat where the character discharged a shot with the weapon, you 
should roll the ammunition dice type for the specified ammunition. 
This is given in parentheses next to the ammunition. A natural 1 
indicates that quiver of ammunition has run out. 
 
Spellcasting and Sage Advice 
Characters may seek out sages to consult on various concerns of 
theirs as well as requesting the help of magicians and priests to 
perform spells for them. The cost of such specialist services is 
provided below. 
 
Spellcasting 
There will come times when characters need a magician or priest to 
cast a spell for them – this will often be healing and benedictory 
spells such as remove curse, raise dead, or restoration. It may a times 
stretch into other spells such as scry for example. In all cases, the 
cost of such a service, if a capable enough spellcaster can be located 
and convinced to work for the party, will charge roughly the 
following: 
 
(100 x level of the spell) x the amount of FP to cast the spell 
usually. 
 
In addition to the above cost, the party must also provide the cost 
of any spell components required. 
 
 
 
Sage Advice 
It may be necessary for player characters to speak to sages and seek 
out expert advice on a variety of subjects. If characters do find a 
sage, roll 1d20+10 to determine the sage’s level. They are assumed 
to be grandmaster proficiency level in the knowledge specialism 
that they are being sought out for. Use the specialist target 
numbers for the sage’s level and make a knowledge test to 
determine if the sage has the information the characters are seeking. 
The cost of such knowledge is determined using the following 
formula: (1d6 x 10) x level of the sage.

the Vehicles and Structures section of this book.

Equipment, Gear and Services 
69 
 
Henchmen and Hirelings 
There will no doubt come a time when a character wishes to 
recruit a hireling to help them with the business of adventuring. 
Characters may only have a number of hirelings equal to or less 
than their follower limit, determined by their PER proficiency 
level. These hirelings often take the forms of peasants, fighters, 
thieves and other such characters whose statistics may be found in 
the Bestiary section. 
 
Approaching A Recruit 
A game master may wish to call for appropriate gossip and 
streetwise tests for a character to be able to locate a potential 
recruit. The recruiter should test persuasion to convince the target 
to join them. The persuasion test should be made with the 
appropriate modifiers, as based on the table below. This test 
modifier also becomes the hireling’s Loyalty Modifier (see below). 
 
RECRUIT OFFER/LOYALTY TABLE 
Offer to Hireling 
Modifier to 
Test  
No share of any treasure. 2sp a day/level of 
the hireling. 
-9 
No share of any treasure. 5sp a day/level of 
the hireling. 
-6 
1 half share of any treasure, 5sp a day/level 
of the hireling. 
-3 
1 half share of any treasure, 1gp a day/level 
of the hireling.  
+0 
1 full share of any treasure, 1gp a day/level of 
the hireling. 
+3 
1 full share of any treasure, 2gp a day/level of 
the hireling. 
+6 
1 full share of any treasure, 5gp a day/level of 
the hireling. 
+9 
 
It is perfectly possible that any such offer may be further 
moderated. The expectation a hireling does not contribute to 
combat would make it easier for a character to recruit them 
(usually a +6 bonus), and that the hireling gets first pick of any 
treasure, including magical treasure (+3). 
 
Loyalty Tests and Modifiers 
Each hireling has a Loyalty Modifier. This is the test modifier for 
the initial Persuasion test made to recruit the hireling. There are 
certain situations when a hireling should test their loyalty. To do 
this, the character who employs them should save vs PER, with 
the hireling’s Loyalty Modifier as a bonus or penalty to the save 
vs PER. If the loyalty test is failed, then the hireling chooses to 
leave the characters employ, at least for the time being. If the 
loyalty test is a success, then the hireling remains in the 
character’s employ. A loyalty test should be made in the following 
circumstances: 
▶ After significant danger: After a moment of significant peril, such 
as a particularly fraught combat or if the hireling is reduced to 0HP. 
This does not mean that a loyalty test should be made after every 
combat.  
▶ In response to maltreatment: If the hireling is mistreated by their 
employer, or their employer does not intervene if they are being 
mistreated by others, then a loyalty test should be made, with an 
additional -6 modifier. Examples of this include constant ridicule of 
the hireling or designating the hireling to be the person who opens 
every door in the dungeon. 
▶ After the completion of a quest or a period of significant 
imperilment: After a quest is completed, a hireling may have 
thoughts as to doing something else with their time, especially if 
they had acquired some treasure of if the adventure involved a 
protracted and serious risk of harm or death.  
 
Hireling Experience and Levels 
As a hireling takes part in encounters, they will gain experience 
points. They only gain experience equal to their treasure share 
value in relation to their level. For example, a hireling entitled to a 
full share of the treasure gains experience points at the exact same 
rate as other characters in the party. A hireling entitled to only a 
half share gains experience points at half the rate of the rest of the 
group. 
A hireling gains levels at the exact same rate as a player character, 
requiring the same amount of experience points for each level. A 
hireling that is higher than level 1 when hired starts with the 
minimum amount of experience points required to have achieved 
that level.  
 
As a hireling gains levels, their target numbers reduce, and their hit 
points increase, as based on the tables below:

Hireling Skills and Attributes 
As a hireling gains levels, they gain core attributes, major skills and 
minor skills. What a hireling gains each level is based on the table 
below. When a hireling gains a core attribute, they may put up 
any of their core attributes by one proficiency level. When they 
gain a minor skill, they choose one from the skill pool below to 
increase by one proficiency level. When they gain a major skill, 
they choose one from the skill pool below to increase by one 
proficiency level.

Hireling Spellcasters 
When a hireling learns a proficiency level in spellcasting, they 
automatically learn one spell of a level they can now cast (1st level 
for Trained; 2nd level for Journeyman, and so on).  
 
These spells are taken as recharge abilities. And their recharge 
works as follows: the highest-level spell they can cast recharges on 
a 6+, the second highest level spell recharges on a 5+, and so on. 
 
Converting Hireling to Player Characters 
It may be that eventually a player wishes to change ‘promote’ one 
of their hirelings to the status into a player character. To do this, 
the player simply creates a player character with the amount of 
experience points of their hireling, doing their best to replicate the 
hireling’s abilities based on what they could do when they were an 
NPC.

Optional Rule: Blackpowder Weapons  
Below is the table for blackpowder weapons. It is strictly the decision of the game master as to whether these are allowed in a campaign or not. 
All these pieces of equipment require the ranged skill to be trained to operate. 
Traits 
Armour Traits 
Each piece of armour has a number of traits as listed in its entry. 
These are detailed below: 
▶ Front-Facing: This piece of armour only protects the character 
from attacks in the front and side, not from the rear. 
▶ Helmet: This piece of armour allows a character reroll armour 
saves caused by critical hits, after which the helmet is destroyed.  
 
Weapon Traits 
Each weapon has a number of traits as listed in its entry. These are 
detailed below: 
▶ Balanced: This weapon is curiously designed to allow its user to 
make the most of its weight. Instead of the STR melee damage 
bonus, attacks from this weapon may use the AGI missile damage 
bonus, even though it is a melee weapon. 
▶ Charge: This weapon is meant to be used from the back of a 
mount. When it is used on a charging steed, the damage from this 
weapon is doubled. 
▶ Flexible: This weapon is designed to allow a person to use either 
one hand or two when wielding it. If two hands are used, the 
weapon gains the qualities listed in parentheses. 
▶ Offhand: This weapon is light enough that can be used in the 
offhand with another weapon. In this way, a character may dual-
wield weapons. 
▶ Parry: This weapon is partially designed to deflect blows. It may 
be used to perform parries. 
▶ Penetrate: This weapon is penetrates through enemies, hitting 
those behind. Any enemy directly behind the target within the 
penetrate value of the weapon is also hit. 
▶ Reload: This weapon requires time to reload. For each level of 
Reload, it takes an additional consecutive action to reload. 
▶ Rend: This weapon tears through armour. For each level of Rend, 
the target’s Armour saves are made a -1. 
▶ Scatter: When this weapon is thrown, it lands regardless of the 
outcome of the attack test. If the attack test fails, then roll a d8 and 
use it to randomly determine the direction. It scatters 1d4 x 5’. 
▶ Two-Handed: This weapon is heavy and requires two hands to 
wield in combat. 
▶ Unwieldy: This weapon is hard to move and aim. When aiming 
at anything capable of movement, the attack test suffers a -3 
penalty.

74 
 
Strongholds and Domains
As characters become richer, more powerful and more experienced 
they may eventually turn their attention toward the acquisition of a 
stronghold. This may mean different things to different characters. 
For example, a fighter may wish to construct or acquire a castle, 
while a wizard may desire a tower in which to conduct arcane 
research and train apprentices. Some strongholds may be able to 
exert their power over the local area and in doing so, grant their 
ruler’s a domain as well as a stronghold. 
 
Strongholds and Domains 
Strongholds 
Strongholds are physical locations such as castles, wizard towers, 
druidic stone circles and guild halls. They represent a character’s 
seat of power. 
 
Domains 
Domains are areas of land that may contain villages, mines, forests 
or open terrain. A character cannot control a domain if they do not 
have a stronghold from which to project their power. Similarly, not 
all strongholds, such as guild halls, have the ability to grant their 
ruler control over a domain. 
 
Stronghold Attributes 
Like characters, strongholds have their own core attribute for which 
they gain proficiency levels. These are different attributes to those 
used by characters and are described below. 
 
Might (MIG) 
Might is the efficacy of the militarism of a stronghold. It determines 
how quickly a force can be recruited, armed and trained to defend 
the stronghold, as well as defensive structures and strategies in the 
stronghold. 
 
Governance (GOV) 
Governance is the effectiveness of the rulership of a stronghold. A 
stronghold with a low proficiency in governance is hard to hold and 
maintain. Governance is often derived directly from the ruler of a 
stronghold and covers the stability of the power maintaining the 
stronghold. 
 
Infrastructure (INF) 
Infrastructure refers to the physical buildings, rooms and assets of a 
stronghold that aid in the administration of the place. A castle with 
a high proficiency level in Infrastructure probably is accessible by 
good roads, has granaries to withstand, siege and well-maintained 
farmlands. 
 
Subterfuge (SUB) 
Subterfuge covers the stronghold’s diplomacy and ability to resist 
conspiracy, root out spies and install agents in other places. 
 
Loyalty (LOY) 
Loyalty is obedience, trustworthiness and allegiance of those who 
work and operate in a stronghold. A low loyalty leaves a stronghold 
open to subversive attacks and treachery. 
 
 
 
 
 
 
Stronghold Attribute Tests 
Stronghold Attribute tests and saves are made exactly like those 
of a normal character, though the target numbers may be different 
for each proficiency level (see Stronghold Target Numbers table). 
The only difference is that a character may not burn FP to increase 
such a test or save. 
 
Wealth Burn 
While a character may not burn FP to succeed at a stronghold 
attribute test or save, they may burn wealth. This involves spending 
1,000gp for each +1 increase to the test or save and represents the 
ruler greasing palms and paying bribes to achieve their intention. As 
with normal character tests, a ruler may not wealth burn opposed 
tests. 
 
Assets 
As well as having the above attributes, a stronghold also has assets. 
Assets are qualities, buildings, people and other elements of 
infrastructure and administration that benefit the stronghold.  
 
Types of Assets 
There are four types of assets: Agents, Buildings, Hirelings, and 
Resources. Agents are characters that will perform various deeds 
relating to subterfuge for the ruler. Buildings are physical constructs 
on the site of the stronghold. Hirelings are specialist workers that 
live and work in and around the stronghold. Resources are strategies 
and systems of organisation used by the stronghold.  
 
At the start of each stronghold round (3 months) the stronghold will 
gain a certain amount of income, which may be modified by events. 
Certain assets or events will drain the income and stored wealth of 
a stronghold.

Strongholds and Domains 
75 
 
Running a Stronghold 
A game master adjudicates the running of strongholds across a series 
of rounds. One round of this scale is one quarter of the year (or 3 
months if a usual 12-month calendar is used). In a stronghold round, 
the ruler of stronghold completes the following steps: 
 
Stronghold Steps Each Round 
1. Income and Population Step 
2. Stronghold Action Step 
3. Purchase Assets Step 
4. Event Step 
 
1. Income and Population Step 
During this step the ruler of a stronghold receives and updates their 
income for that stronghold round. If they have a domain, which may 
have a population, they also update this now. 
 
2. Stronghold Actions Step 
Each stronghold round, the ruler of the stronghold may take one 
stronghold action. This may be to raise a fighting force, adjust taxes, 
root out enemy spies or prospect for resources. 
 
3. Purchase Assets Step 
Now the ruler may use any wealth they have access to, including the 
wealth of the stronghold, to purchase assets. This may include 
upgrades to a keep, lavish furnishings, workshops, outposts, 
administrators etc. A ruler may only purchase one building or 
resource asset per stronghold round, though they may purchase as 
many agent or hireling assets as they like, so long as they have the 
money. 
 
4. Event Step 
The ruler now rolls a d4. A result of a ‘1’ means that the stronghold 
has been subject to an event. Roll on the appropriate table for the 
stronghold to determine the event. 
 
 
Income and Population 
In this first step the ruler of a domain calculates their most recent 
income and adds that to their total. If the stronghold also has a 
domain, they must test LOY to see if their population grows or 
shrinks. If their LOY test is successful, consult the Population 
Growth table below, if it is a failure, consult the Population 
Decline table below:

Types of Strongholds 
Claiming a Stronghold 
To claim and control a stronghold, a character must fulfil certain 
criteria. If this is not the case, they cannot control that particular type 
of stronghold and the place remains abandoned until control is 
claimed by another. 
 
Citadel 
Citadels are reinforced castles and fortifications. They provide both 
a literal and symbolic expression of power and are often built to 
withstand a siege of many months.  
 
Citadel Requirements 
▶ The character must be of at least 10th level. 
▶ The character must have at least 10 talents in one of the following 
talent trees: Class: Barbarian; Class: Fighter; Class: Paladin; Class: 
Ranger; Class: Runesmith; Class: Warlock; Class: Warlord. 
▶ Domain: A citadel allows the ruler to claim a domain. 
 
Citadel Actions 
A character in control of a citadel may take one of the following 
actions as a Stronghold Action. 
 
Action: Direct Construction 
The ruler takes direct control of any building work going on around 
the stronghold. The cost of Building Assets this stronghold round 
is reduced by 20%. 
 
Action: Hold Court 
The character holds court in their citadel, hearing the news and pleas 
of their subjects. The cost of Resource Assets this stronghold round 
is reduced by 20%. 
 
Action: Hold Feast 
A great feast is held in the citadel this stronghold round. For the next 
three stronghold rounds, this citadel gains a +3 bonus to LOY tests. 
 
Action: Search for Agents 
The ruler makes an SUB test for the stronghold. On a success, they 
roll 1d6, revealing the existence of up to that many outsider agents 
(if the number rolled is higher than the number of outsider agents 
in the stronghold, then all are revealed). The game master then rolls 
1d6 for each agent revealed. On a 1-3, the agent is alerted to their 
cover being blown before it can be addressed, and they flee the 
stronghold. On a 4-6, they are apprehended by those in the 
stronghold. 
 
Action: Use Assassin 
If the character has an assassin in another stronghold, they direct 
them to kill someone. An opposed SUB vs GOV test is made. On a 
success, the assassin kills the target. The strongholds then make a 
further opposed SUB vs SUB test. On a success, the assassin escapes 
after their attempt (whether the attempt was successful or not). On 
a failure, the assassin is apprehended by the opposing stronghold. 
 
Action: Use Spy 
If the character has a spy in another stronghold, they direct them to 
gather information by making an SUB test. On a success, the spy 
gives a detailed account of what they have been directed to 
investigate by their master. On a critical failure, they are revealed 
and captured. 
 


Strongholds and Domains 
76 
 
Citadel Assets 
A character in control of a citadel may purchase the following assets. 
 
Agent - Assassin 
▶ Maximum: Determined by ruler’s PER. 
▶ Cost: 2,000gp per stronghold round. 
▶ Description: An assassin is a hidden agent that can be used to 
kill people in other places and strongholds. 
 
Agent - Spy 
▶ Maximum: Determined by ruler’s PER. 
▶ Cost: 400gp per stronghold round. 
▶ Description: A spy will attempt to infiltrate another stronghold 
or similar place. They can be used to gather information or sabotage 
the assets of other places. A spy counts as one of the ruler’s followers 
as dictated by the rules for PER. 
 
Building - Exterior Door 
▶ Maximum: Unlimited. 
▶ Cost: 600gp. 
▶ Description: A reinforced wooden door placed in a wall of the 
stronghold. The door has 4 structure points and a 4+ armour save if 
attacked. 
 
Building - Exterior Portcullis 
▶ Maximum: Unlimited. 
▶ Cost: 1,000gp. 
▶ Description: A drawbridge over a moat placed in a wall of the 
stronghold. The drawbridge has 4 structure points and a 4+ armour 
save if attacked. 
 
Building - Exterior Portcullis 
▶ Maximum: Unlimited. 
▶ Cost: 2,000gp. 
▶ Description: A portcullis placed in a wall of the stronghold. The 
door has 4 structure points and a 3+ armour save if attacked. 
 
Building - Moat – 100’ long 
▶ Maximum: Unlimited. 
▶ Cost: 1,500gp. 
▶ Description: A 20’ deep, 20’ wide moat filled with water. 
 
Building – Secret Passages 
▶ Maximum: 1. 
▶ Cost: 6,000gp. 
▶ Description: The stronghold is crisscrossed with many secret 
passages between rooms and levels. With this asset, the 
stronghold gains +1 proficiency level Subterfuge. 
 
Building - Stone Keep 
▶ Maximum: 1. 
▶ Cost: 25,000gp. 
▶ Description: A reinforced stone keep in 
the centre of the stronghold. The keep has 
34 structure points and a 2+ armour save 
if attacked. 
 
Building - Stone Tower 
▶ Maximum: Unlimited. 
▶ Cost: 10,000gp. 
▶ Description: A stone tower. The 
tower has 24 structure points and a 2+ 
armour save if attacked. 
 
Building - Stone Wall – 100’ Long 
▶ Maximum: Unlimited. 
▶ Cost: 4,000gp. 
▶ Description: A 100’ long 
section of stone wall. The 
wall 
has 
16 
structure 
points and a 2+ armour 
save if attacked. 
 
 
 
Building - Wooden Keep 
▶ Maximum: 1. 
▶ Cost: 10,000gp. 
▶ Description: A reinforced wooden keep in the centre of the 
stronghold. The keep has 24 structure points and a 4+ armour save 
if attacked. Any magical fire damage the wall takes counts as having 
the siege quality. 
 
Building - Wooden Tower 
▶ Maximum: Unlimited. 
▶ Cost: 2,500gp. 
▶ Description: A wooden tower. The tower has 16 structure points 
and a 3+ armour save if attacked. Any magical fire damage the wall 
takes counts as having the siege quality. 
 
Building - Wooden Wall – 100’ Long 
▶ Maximum: Unlimited. 
▶ Cost: 1,000gp. 
▶ Description: A 100’ long section of wooden wall. The wall has 12 
structure points and a 4+ armour save if attacked. Any magical fire 
damage the wall takes counts as having the siege quality. 
 
Hireling – Castellan 
▶ Maximum: 1.

stronghold. A description of statistics can be found in the Bestiary, 
though this fighter does 
not have a ranged 
weapon.

Resource – Trusted Retainers

Dwarf Hold 
Dwarf holds are mountain redoubts maintained by dwarfen clans. 
They allow dwarfen heroes to mine deeply and are maintained over 
the centuries as centres of tradition and history.  
 
Dwarf Hold Requirements 
▶ The character must be of at least 10th level. 
▶ The character must have the Dwarf Heritage. 
▶ Domain: A dwarf hold does not allow the ruler to claim a domain. 
 
Depth Points 
Dwarfs are natural miners and delve deeply into the earth to seek 
out its treasures and secrets. Some assets of a dwarf hold require 
depth points. These are an abstraction of how deeply the dwarfs 
have mined. Depth points must be tracked by the ruler, and more 
can be gained with the Delve Deeper stronghold action. A single 
hold may only ever have 100 total depth points. 
 
Dwarf Hold Actions 
A character in control of a dwarf hold may take one of the following 
actions as a Stronghold Action. 
 
Action: Delve Deeper 
The ruler directs teams of dwarfen miners, opening up new passages 
and delver deeper into the roots of the world. The ruler spends 
3,500gp and the stronghold gains 1d6 depth points. 
 
Action: Direct Construction 
The ruler takes direct control of any building work going on around 
the stronghold. The cost of Building Assets this stronghold round 
is reduced by 20%. 
 
 
 
Action: Hold Feast 
A great feast is held in the hold this stronghold round. For the next 
three stronghold rounds, this citadel gains a +3 bonus to LOY tests. 
 
Action: Search for Agents 
The ruler makes an SUB test for the stronghold. On a success, they 
roll 1d6, revealing the existence of up to that many outsider agents 
(if the number rolled is higher than the number of outsider agents 
in the stronghold, then all are revealed). The game master then rolls 
1d6 for each agent revealed. On a 1-3, the agent is alerted to their 
cover being blown before it can be addressed, and they flee the 
stronghold. On a 4-6, they are apprehended by those in the 
stronghold. 
 
Action: Use Spy 
If the character has a spy in another stronghold, they direct them to 
gather information by making an SUB test. On a success, the spy 
gives a detailed account of what they have been directed to 
investigate by their master. On a critical failure, they are revealed 
and captured. 
Dwarf Hold Assets 
A character in control of a dwarf hold may purchase the following 
assets. 
 
Agent - Spy 
▶ Required Depth Points: 0. 
▶ Maximum: Determined by ruler’s PER. 
▶ Cost: 400gp per stronghold round. 
▶ Description: A spy will attempt to infiltrate another stronghold 
or similar place. They can be used to gather information or sabotage 
the assets of other places. A spy counts as one of the ruler’s followers 
as dictated by the rules for PER. 
 
Building – Dwarfen Brewery 
▶ Required Depth Points: 4 
▶ Maximum: Unlimited. 
▶ Cost: 4,000gp. 
▶ Description: The dwarf hold gains a brewery that is operated by 
a brewmaster, providing delicious beer to the hold. Must be built in 
a hex with jungle or forest terrain. Provides +1,000gp per stronghold 
round. With this asset, the stronghold gains +1 proficiency level 
Loyalty but can only gain this benefit once with multiple breweries 
not providing more Loyalty. 
 
Building – Dwarfen Mine 
▶ Required Depth Points: 6. 
▶ Maximum: Unlimited. 
▶ Cost: 10,000gp. 
▶ Description: A dwarfen mine to extract resources from the roots 
of the world. When the mine is first constructed, roll on the following 
table to determine what type of mine has been set up, and how much 
gold it provides per stronghold round.

▶ Description: The stronghold is crisscrossed with many secret

gains +1 proficiency level Subterfuge.

Strongholds and Domains

Establishment 
Breweries, coaching companies, warehouses: all manner of businesses constitute an establishment stronghold. Establishments offer little political 
power to most but can help enrich a savvy adventurer. 
 
Establishment Type and Requirements 
There are myriad types of establishments possible, from luxury jewellers to fishmongers and some companies are harder to acquire and run than 
others. A game master and player must decide what type of establishment is being opened and then meet the level requirement and the pay the 
initial cost. An establishment does not allow the ruler to claim a domain. 
 
ESTABLISHMENT TYPE

Establishment Income 
An establishment does not gain income in a regular and predictable 
way. Certain assets give the stronghold wealth dice. These are 
determined by the type of establishment. During the income step the 
ruler rolls all of the stronghold’s wealth dice for that round. Any 
natural ‘1’s are removed, and the remaining dice are added together 
and multiplied by 100. The stronghold then gains that number of 
gold pieces. 
 
Establishment Actions 
A character in control of an establishment may take one of the 
following actions as a Stronghold Action. 
 
Action: Direct Management 
The ruler takes direct control of any building work going on around 
the stronghold. The cost of Resource Assets this stronghold round 
is reduced by 20%. 
 
Action: Issue Purchase Orders 
The ruler seeks to extend the establishment quickly, issuing 
purchase orders. In taking this action, the ruler may purchase up to 
3 resource or building assets instead of the usual 1 this stronghold 
round.  
 
Action: Search for Agents 
The ruler makes an SUB test for the stronghold. On a success, they 
roll 1d6, revealing the existence of up to that many outsider agents 
(if the number rolled is higher than the number of outsider agents 
in the stronghold, then all are revealed). The game master then rolls 
1d6 for each agent revealed. On a 1-3, the agent is alerted to their 
cover being blown before it can be addressed, and they flee the 
stronghold. On a 4-6, they are apprehended by those in the 
stronghold. 
 
Action: Use Spy 
If the character has a spy in another stronghold, they direct them to 
gather information by making an SUB test. On a success, the spy 
gives a detailed account of what they have been directed to 
investigate by their master. On a critical failure, they are revealed 
and captured. 
 
Establishment Generic Assets 
A character in control of an establishment may purchase any of the 
below assets, regardless of the type of establishment. 
 
Agent - Spy 
▶ Maximum: Determined by ruler’s PER. 
▶ Cost: 400gp per stronghold round. 
▶ Description: A spy will attempt to infiltrate another stronghold 
or similar place. They can be used to gather information or sabotage 
the assets of other places. A spy counts as one of the ruler’s followers 
as dictated by the rules for PER. 
Hireling – Accountant 
▶ Maximum: 1. 
▶ Cost: 500gp per stronghold round. 
▶ Description: Accountants organise the incoming and outgoing 
wealth of the establishment, allowing for a greater amount of wealth 
to be accumulated. The ruler no longer removes any natural ‘1’s 
when rolling wealth dice, instead the natural ‘1’ is added to the total. 
 
Hireling – Board Member 
▶ Maximum: 4. 
▶ Cost: 500gp per stronghold round. 
▶ Description: Board members are expensive members of the 
running board of the company. For each board member hired, the 
ruler may gain +1 proficiency level to any single stronghold core 
attribute. In addition, if the board has four members (and is 
therefore full), the wealth dice type is increased by 1 for all wealth 
dice. For example, a d6 would become a d8, a d8 would become a 
d10 etc. The dice types are listed here, from lowest to highest: d4, 
d6, d8, d10, d12, d20. 
 
Hireling – Manager 
▶ Maximum: 1 per Branch Building Asset. 
▶ Cost: 100gp per stronghold round. 
▶ Description: Managers are staff responsible for the running of a 
branch. Having a manager allows the ruler to reroll one natural ‘1’ 
rolled on a wealth die per stronghold round. 
 
Hireling – Fighter, Novice 
▶ Maximum: 3 per Branch Building Asset. 
▶ Cost: 3gp per stronghold round. 
▶ Description: A novice fighter that will act as a warrior for the 
stronghold. A description of statistics can be found in the Bestiary, 
though this fighter does not have a ranged weapon. 
 
Hireling – Fighter, Seasoned 
▶ Maximum: 1 per Guild Hall Building Asset. 
▶ Cost: 6gp per stronghold round. 
▶ Description: A seasoned fighter that will act as a warrior for the 
stronghold. A description of statistics can be found in the Bestiary, 
though this fighter does not have a ranged weapon. 
 
Resource - Efficient Administrators 
▶ Maximum: 1. 
▶ Cost: 6,000gp initial cost and -10% to base income per stronghold 
round. The value of this deduction is calculated after all income has 
been determined, but before any other deductions have been 
removed. 
▶ Description: The staff that work within the stronghold are well 
placed to ensure the careful running of day-to-day business. With 
this asset, the stronghold gains +1 proficiency level Subterfuge.

Resource – Inventory Management 
▶ Maximum: 1. 
▶ Cost: 8,000gp initial cost and -5% to base income per stronghold 
round. The value of this deduction is calculated after all income has

▶ Description: Careful and well-managed systems are set up

Resource – Non-Financial Reimbursement

▶ Description: The staff that work within the business are well 
rewarded in non-financial terms. It may be that their birthdays are 
celebrated with parties, or that their medical costs are covered or 
that they are entitled to a certain amount of time off per year. With 
this asset, the establishment gains +1 proficiency level Governance 
and Loyalty. 
 
Common Goods and Services Assets 
A character in control of a Common Goods and Services 
establishment may purchase any of the below assets. 
 
Branch - Building 
▶ Maximum: 5 per city, 3 per town, 1 per village or outpost. 
▶ Cost: 2,500gp. 
▶ Description: The ruler opens a new branch of the establishment 
such as a new alehouse, market stall or shop. Each branch 
automatically adds 3 wealth dice to the stronghold income. 
 
Resource – Common Staff 
▶ Maximum: 1. 
▶ Cost: 500gp initial cost and -2% to base income per stronghold 
round. The value of this deduction is calculated after all income has 
been determined, but before any other deductions have been 
removed. 
▶ Description: Drawn from the ordinary peasantry, the staff in the 
establishment do their best. With this asset, the stronghold gains +1 
proficiency level Infrastructure.  
 
Resource – Dodgy Suppliers 
▶ Maximum: 1. 
▶ Cost: 1,000gp initial cost and -5% to base income per stronghold 
round. The value of this deduction is calculated after all income has 
been determined, but before any other deductions have been 
removed. 
▶ Description: The business owners make agreements with certain 
unsavoury merchants to supply the establishment with lower quality 
product that can be shifted at a higher mark up. With this asset, the 
stronghold gains -1 proficiency level Loyalty, but all wealth dice 
are increased by one dice type. For example, a d6 would become a 
d8, a d8 would become a d10 etc. The dice types are listed here, 
from lowest to highest: d4, d6, d8, d10, d12, d20. 
 
Resource – Gregariousness 
▶ Maximum: 1. 
▶ Cost: 1,000gp initial cost and -2% to base income per stronghold 
round. The value of this deduction is calculated after all income has 
been determined, but before any other deductions have been 
removed.

Mage’s Tower 
Mage’s towers are the haunts of wizards and sorcerers. They offer a 
permanent, safe home for mages of all stripes to conduct arcane 
research and hone their magical craft. 
 
Mage’s Tower Requirements 
▶ The character must be of at least 10th level. 
▶ The character must have at least 10 talents in one of the following 
talent trees: Class: Alchemist; Class: Sorcerer; Class: Warlock; 
Class: Wizard. 
▶ Domain: A mage’s tower allows the ruler to claim a domain, 
though the domain may only be a maximum number of hexes as 
determined by the ruler’s INT proficiency, as determined below: 
 
MAGE’S TOWER DOMAIN SIZE 
Ruler’s Intellect 
Max Number of Hexes 
Untrained 
0 
Trained 
0 
Journeyman 
6 
Expert 
6 
Master 
18 
Grandmaster 
18 
 
Mage’s Tower Actions 
A character in control of a mage’s tower may take one of the 
following actions as a Stronghold Action. 
 
Action: Direct Construction 
The ruler takes direct control of any building work going on around 
the stronghold. The cost of Building Assets this stronghold round 
is reduced by 20%. 
 
Action: Hold Conclave 
The ruler of the tower calls a conclave of mages. For the duration of 
this stronghold round, any 1st, 2nd, 3rd, or 4th level spells cast within 
the tower do not require components. 
 
Action: Search for Agents 
The ruler makes an SUB test for the stronghold. On a success, they 
roll 1d6, revealing the existence of up to that many outsider agents 
(if the number rolled is higher than the number of outsider agents 
in the stronghold, then all are revealed). The game master then rolls 
1d6 for each agent revealed. On a 1-3, the agent is alerted to their 
cover being blown before it can be addressed, and they flee the 
stronghold. On a 4-6, they are apprehended by those in the 
stronghold. 
 
 
 
 
 
Action: Use Assassin 
If the character has an assassin in another stronghold, they direct 
them to kill someone. An opposed SUB vs GOV test is made. On a 
success, the assassin kills the target. The strongholds then make a 
further opposed SUB vs SUB test. On a success, the assassin escapes 
after their attempt (whether the attempt was successful or not). On 
a failure, the assassin is apprehended by the opposing stronghold. 
 
Action: Use Spy 
If the character has a spy in another stronghold, they direct them to 
gather information by making an SUB test. On a success, the spy 
gives a detailed account of what they have been directed to 
investigate by their master. On a critical failure, they are revealed 
and captured. 
 
Mage’s Tower Assets 
A character in control of a mage’s tower may purchase the following 
assets. 
 
Agent - Assassin 
▶ Maximum: Determined by ruler’s PER. 
▶ Cost: 2,000gp per stronghold round. 
▶ Description: An assassin is a hidden agent that can be used to 
kill people in other places and strongholds. 
 
Agent - Spy 
▶ Maximum: Determined by ruler’s PER. 
▶ Cost: 400gp per stronghold round. 
▶ Description: A spy will attempt to infiltrate another stronghold 
or similar place. They can be used to gather information or sabotage 
the assets of other places. A spy counts as one of the ruler’s followers 
as dictated by the rules for PER. 
 
Building - Moat – 100’ long 
▶ Maximum: Unlimited. 
▶ Cost: 1,500gp. 
▶ Description: A 20’ deep, 20’ wide moat filled with water. 
 
Building – Mystical Shroud 
▶ Maximum: 1. 
▶ Cost: 25,000gp. 
▶ Description: The stronghold is shrouded in a magical illusion. If 
the ruler wishes, any creature approaching the stronghold is 
redirected by the illusion, finding themselves unable to locate the 
stronghold. A creature casting dispel magic on themselves can find 
their way to the stronghold so long as they also succeed a hard (-3) 
save vs PER.  
 
Building – Orrery 
▶ Maximum: 1. 
▶ Cost: 14,000gp. 
▶ Description: The stronghold has an orrery – a complex 
mechanical model of astrological bodies used to divine the future. 
With this asset, the ruler may reroll a stronghold event if they do not 
like the event they have generated. They must accept the second 
result. 
 
Building – Runes of Levitation 
▶ Maximum: 1. 
▶ Cost: 25,000gp. 
▶ Description: The tower can now fly, with a movement rate of 30’ 
per round or 24 miles per day. It generally hovers 50’ off the ground 
but may be raised up to 500’. The ruler may direct the movement of 
the tower if they desire. If the tower ever moves, it instantly loses 
any domain it is attached to. 
 
Building – Secret Passages 
▶ Maximum: 1. 
▶ Cost: 6,000gp. 
▶ Description: The stronghold is crisscrossed with many secret 
passages between rooms and levels. With this asset, the stronghold 
gains +1 proficiency level Subterfuge.

Temple 
Temples are the redoubts and refuges of clerics and paladins. They 
are holy places designed as sanctuaries against the darkness and 
protections from evil. 
 
Temple Requirements 
▶ The character must be of at least 10th level. 
▶ The character must have at least 10 talents in one of the following 
talent trees: Class: Cleric; Class: Paladin. 
▶ Domain: A temple allows the ruler to claim a domain, though the 
domain may only be a maximum number of hexes as determined by 
the ruler’s PER proficiency, as determined below:

Temple Actions 
A character in control of a temple may take one of the following 
actions as a Stronghold Action. 
 
Action: Direct Construction 
The ruler takes direct control of any building work going on around 
the stronghold. The cost of Building Assets this stronghold round 
is reduced by 20%. 
 
Action: Convene Synod 
Senior priests within the stronghold meet to discuss matters of faith 
and governance. For the next three stronghold rounds, this citadel 
gains a +3 bonus to SUB and LOY tests. 
 
Action: Inquisition 
The ruler makes an SUB test for the stronghold. On a success, they 
roll 1d6, revealing the existence of up to that many outsider agents 
(if the number rolled is higher than the number of outsider agents 
in the stronghold, then all are revealed). The game master then rolls 
1d6 for each agent revealed. On a 1, the agent is alerted to their 
cover being blown before it can be addressed, and they flee the 
stronghold. On a 2-6, they are apprehended by those in the 
stronghold. 
 
Action: Use Spy 
If the character has a spy in another stronghold, they direct them to 
gather information by making an SUB test. On a success, the spy 
gives a detailed account of what they have been directed to 
investigate by their master. On a critical failure, they are revealed 
and captured.

▶ Cost: 5,000gp initial cost and -10% to base income per stronghold 
round. The value of this deduction is calculated after all income has 
been determined, but before any other deductions have been 
removed. 
▶ Description: A place of charity and shelter where the poor and 
destitute can find relief from a harsh world. With this asset, the 
stronghold gains +1 proficiency level Loyalty.  
 
Resource – Armoury 
▶ Maximum: 1. 
▶ Cost: 12,000gp. 
▶ Description: The stronghold installs an armoury to ensure the 
proper maintenance of its weapons and armour. With this asset, the 
stronghold gains +1 proficiency level Might. 
 
Resource – Chapterhouse 
▶ Maximum: 1. 
▶ Cost: 14,000gp. 
▶ Description: The temple has a chapterhouse in which the 
gathered priests may discuss a variety of topics from cult doctrine to 
the intrigue with lay authorities. With this asset, the stronghold gains 
+1 proficiency level Subterfuge. 
 
Resource – Clerical Stables 
▶ Maximum: 1. 
▶ Cost: 10,000gp initial cost and -10% to base income per stronghold 
round. The value of this deduction is calculated after all income has 
been determined, but before any other deductions have been 
removed. 
▶ Description: This asset procures horses, and the men required to 
train and maintain them as warhorses. With this asset, the stronghold 
gains +1 proficiency level Might. The asset provides warhorses for 
20% of the clerics maintained by the stronghold. 
Resource – Deep Consecration 
▶ Maximum: 1. 
▶ Cost: 8,000gp initial cost and -4% to base income per stronghold 
round. The value of this deduction is calculated after all income has 
been determined, but before any other deductions have been 
removed. 
▶ Description: The stronghold is a deeply holy place. All undead 
creatures and demons suffer 1 fire damage each minute they are in 
the stronghold. 
 
Resource – Donations 
▶ Maximum: Ruler’s Level 
▶ Cost: 4,000gp. 
▶ Description: This sacred place accepts the donations of the 
common folk to pay for its actions. This provides +500gp per 
stronghold round. 
 
Resource – Falconry Mews 
▶ Maximum: 1. 
▶ Cost: 8,000gp initial cost and -5% to base income per stronghold 
round. The value of this deduction is calculated after all income has 
been determined, but before any other deductions have been 
removed. 
▶ Description: The stronghold has a set of mews for keeping birds 
of prey and messenger pigeons. This allows the ruler to receive and 
send messages to other strongholds without magical means. With 
this asset, the stronghold gains +1 proficiency level Infrastructure.  
 
Resource – Granaries and Wells 
▶ Maximum: 1. 
▶ Cost: 10,000gp. 
▶ Description: The stronghold installs siege granaries and wells. 
This gives the citadel enough food and water to last for 1d4+4 
stronghold rounds before running out of food if besieged. With this 
asset, the stronghold gains +1 proficiency level Infrastructure. 
 
Resource – Laymen 
▶ Maximum: 1. 
▶ Cost: 4,000gp initial cost and -4% to base income per stronghold 
round. The value of this deduction is calculated after all income has 
been determined, but before any other deductions have been 
removed. 
▶ Description: The stronghold maintains a class of servants and 
retainers that are non-ordained lay folk who work in the temple. 
With this asset, the stronghold gains +1 proficiency level 
Infrastructure. 
 
Resource - Ordained Administrators 
▶ Maximum: 1. 
▶ Cost: 6,000gp initial cost and -10% to base income per stronghold 
round. The value of this deduction is calculated after all income has 
been determined, but before any other deductions have been 
removed. 
▶ Description: The most important members of the temple are all 
ordained priests within the recognised priesthood. With this asset, 
the stronghold gains +1 proficiency level Governance.  
 
Resource – Reliquary 
▶ Maximum: 1. 
▶ Cost: 10,000gp initial cost and -10% to base income per stronghold 
round. The value of this deduction is calculated after all income has 
been determined, but before any other deductions have been 
removed. 
▶ Description: A sacred storehouse of magical objects collected by 
various priors, abbots and wandering saints. With this asset, the 
stronghold adds one random magic item to its Reliquary per 
stronghold round. Generate this magic item by rolling on Magic Item 
Table A in the Treasure section. A reliquary generates 5 items, and 
then an additional 5,000gp must be sacrificed to re-empower the 
reliquary. 
 


Strongholds and Domains 
89 
 
Thieves’ Guild 
Thieves’ guilds are organised criminal networks used to enrich the 
ganglords and beggarkings that run them.  
 
Thieves’ Guild Requirements 
▶ The character must be of at least 10th level. 
▶ The character must have at least 10 talents in the: Class: Thief 
talent tree 
▶ Domain: A thieves’ guild does not allow the ruler to claim a 
domain. 
 
Thieves’ Guild Income 
A thieves’ guild does not gain income in a regular and predictable 
way. Certain assets give the stronghold wealth dice. These are each 
1d6. During the income step the ruler rolls all of the stronghold’s 
wealth dice for that round. Any natural ‘1’s are removed, and the 
remaining dice are added together and multiplied by 100. The 
stronghold then gains that number of gold pieces. 
 
Thieves’ Guild Actions 
A character in control of a thieves’ guild may take one of the 
following actions as a Stronghold Action. 
 
Action: Direct Management 
The ruler takes direct control of any building work going on around 
the stronghold. The cost of Resource Assets this stronghold round 
is reduced by 20%. 
 
Action: Extend Turf 
The ruler attempts to extend the turf of the thieves’ guild into 
another city. This provokes an opposed MIG vs MIG test from the 
guild and any criminal organisations that stand in the guilds way. 
On a success, the guild successfully muscles in on the new area and 
may purchase a guildhall asset there. This may provoke reprisals, 
vengeance and even an all out gang war against rivals in the new 
area.  
 
Action: Oversee Heist 
The ruler directs a major heist within a city, or engages in another 
aspect of criminal activity of significance. The ruler makes an 
opposed SUB test against the target faction or stronghold. On a 
success, the ruler gains an item of value as determined by the INF 
proficiency level of the stronghold. On a failure, the ruler must 
personally test stealth. A failure of this stealth test results in the 
ruler being discovered by the authorities and perhaps even 
apprehended. In addition, on a failure, the stronghold loses all of its 
income in the next stronghold round as its members go to ground. 
 
HEIST TABLE 
GOV 
Wealth Gained 
Untrained 
1d4 x 100gp 
Trained 
1d4 x 150gp 
Journeyman 
1d6 x 250gp 
Expert 
1d6 x 500gp 
Master 
1d6 x 1,000gp 
Grandmaster 
1d6 x 2,500gp 
 
Action: Search for Agents 
The ruler makes an SUB test for the stronghold. On a success, they 
roll 1d6, revealing the existence of up to that many outsider agents 
(if the number rolled is higher than the number of outsider agents 
in the stronghold, then all are revealed). The game master then rolls 
1d6 for each agent revealed. On a 1-3, the agent is alerted to their 
cover being blown before it can be addressed, and they flee the 
stronghold. On a 4-6, they are apprehended by those in the 
stronghold. 
Action: Use Assassin 
If the character has an assassin in another stronghold, they direct 
them to kill someone. An opposed SUB vs GOV test is made. On a 
success, the assassin kills the target. The strongholds then make a 
further opposed SUB vs SUB test. On a success, the assassin escapes 
after their attempt (whether the attempt was successful or not). On 
a failure, the assassin is apprehended by the opposing stronghold. 
 
Action: Use Spy 
If the character has a spy in another stronghold, they direct them to 
gather information by making an SUB test. On a success, the spy 
gives a detailed account of what they have been directed to 
investigate by their master. On a critical failure, they are revealed 
and captured. 
 
Thieves’ Guild Assets 
A character in control of a thieves’ guild may purchase the following 
assets. 
 
Agent - Assassin 
▶ Maximum: Determined by ruler’s PER. 
▶ Cost: 2,000gp per stronghold round. 
▶ Description: An assassin is a hidden agent that can be used to 
kill people in other places and strongholds. 
 
Agent - Spy 
▶ Maximum: Determined by ruler’s PER. 
▶ Cost: 400gp per stronghold round. 
▶ Description: A spy will attempt to infiltrate another stronghold 
or similar place. They can be used to gather information or sabotage 
the assets of other places. A spy counts as one of the ruler’s followers 
as dictated by the rules for PER. 
 
Building – Guild Hall 
▶ Maximum: 1 per city. 
▶ Cost: 12,000gp. 
▶ Description: A guild hall is a safe and secure location within the 
heart of a city. It is used by guild members and the watch, while 
generally aware of the location of such a guild hall, would not dare 
to desecrate such a place with their presence. 
 
Building – Secret Passages 
▶ Maximum: 1. 
▶ Cost: 6,000gp. 
▶ Description: The stronghold is crisscrossed with many secret 
passages between rooms and levels. With this asset, the stronghold 
gains +1 proficiency level Subterfuge. 
 
 
 
Building – Sewers and Alleys 
▶ Maximum: 1. 
▶ Cost: 6,000gp. 
▶ Description: The stronghold makes use of sewer passages and 
narrow, unmarked alleyways across the city in which it is located. 
+1 proficiency level Infrastructure.

90

Hireling – Blackmailer 
▶ Maximum: 1. 
▶ Cost: 300gp per stronghold round. 
▶ Description: Blackmailers are specialist criminals that gather

order to help maintain the guild’s stranglehold over officials. With 
this asset, the stronghold gains +1 proficiency level Subterfuge. 
 
Hireling – Court Wizard 
▶ Maximum: 1. 
▶ Cost: 120gp per stronghold round.

and spellcaster for the stronghold. A description of statistics can be

Hireling – Enforcer Master 
▶ Maximum: 2. 
▶ Cost: 300gp per stronghold round. 
▶ Description: Enforcers are experienced criminals with an

▶ Maximum: 50 per Guild Hall Building Asset. 
▶ Cost: 3gp per stronghold round.

▶ Cost: 6gp per stronghold round.

Resource – Beggar Legion

▶ Cost: 4,000gp.

Resource – Bruisers and Bully Boys 
▶ Maximum: 1.

round. The value of this deduction is calculated after all income has 
been determined, but before any other deductions have been 
removed. 
▶ Description: The guild employs a series of thugs and bullies to 
control the streets. With this asset, the stronghold gains +1 
proficiency level Might. 
 
Resource - Efficient Administrators 
▶ Maximum: 1. 
▶ Cost: 6,000gp initial cost and -10% to base income per stronghold 
round. The value of this deduction is calculated after all income has 
been determined, but before any other deductions have been 
removed. 
▶ Description: The staff that work within the stronghold are well 
placed to ensure the careful running of day-to-day business. With 
this asset, the stronghold gains +1 proficiency level Subterfuge.  
 
 
 
Resource – Expert Fences 
▶ Maximum: 1. 
▶ Cost: 6,000gp initial cost and -10% to base income per stronghold

▶ Description: Specialist criminals that will buy stolen goods and 
sell it on at a reliable price. With this asset, the stronghold gains +1

Resource – Greased Palms 
▶ Maximum: 1. 
▶ Cost: 4,000gp initial cost and -5% to base income per stronghold 
round. The value of this deduction is calculated after all income has 
been determined, but before any other deductions have been 
removed. 
▶ Description: The guild maintains consistent bribes of watchmen 
and various other officials. With this asset, the stronghold gains +1 
proficiency level Subterfuge and Loyalty.  
 
Resource – On Our Payroll 
▶ Maximum: 1. 
▶ Cost: 4,000gp initial cost and -5% to base income per stronghold

been determined, but before any other deductions have been

▶ Description: This asset requires the Greased Palms asset. The 
guild directly employs several lawmen, judges and other high-
ranking members of the judicial system. With this asset, the 
stronghold gains +1 proficiency level Governance and they no

Resource – Protection Racket 
▶ Maximum: 6 per Guild Hall Building Asset. 
▶ Cost: 1,000gp. 
▶ Description: The guild employs a set of racketeers to extort 
protection money from local businesses. This provides +1 wealth

Resource – Smugglers 
▶ Maximum: 1. 
▶ Cost: 4,000gp initial cost and -5% to base income per stronghold

removed. 
▶ Description: Smugglers are criminals that specialise in the 
movement of goods across jurisdictions and around boundaries and

Resource – Thieves 
▶ Maximum: 2 per Guild Hall Building Asset. 
▶ Cost: 6,000gp. 
▶ Description: The stronghold employs a host of thieves and

Resource – Thieves’ Honour 
▶ Maximum: 1. 
▶ Cost: 6,000gp. 
▶ Description: The ruler of the guild condemns violence amongst 
guild members and encourages a code of honour and respect 
amongst its thieves. With this asset, the stronghold gains +1

Resource – Violent Methods 
▶ Maximum: 1. 
▶ Cost: 6,000gp. 
▶ Description: The ruler of the guild condones and even 
encourages the use of extreme violence amongst guild members in

asset. 
 


Strongholds and Domains 
91 
 
Domains 
Domains are a significant area of land that some strongholds can 
claim adjacent to themselves. They are, in effect, a very complex type 
of asset. 
 
The 6-Mile Hex 
Domains are made up of 6-mile hexes, like the map below. Each 6-
mile hexes contributes to the power of the stronghold that claims it 
as part of its domain. 
 
 
 
The above shows a citadel stronghold with an attached domain 
(shown by the grey hexes). The hex upon which the citadel sits also 
counts, meaning that this domain is 7 hexes. 
 
Population and Tax 
As a domain expands and more people fall under the control of the 
ruler, the amount of tax made each stronghold round increases.  
▶ Each subject pays 3gp per stronghold in taxes to the ruler’s coffers. 
Simply calculate the population of all the hexes combined and 
multiply the total by 3 to get the tax income. 
 
Domain Actions 
The following options are available as stronghold actions for those 
rulers with strongholds that can claim domains. 
 
Action: Adjust Taxes 
A ruler may adjust the tax threshold for the subjects in their domain. 
Each subject normally pays 3gp per stronghold round.  A character 
may change this to one of the following, with resulting changes to 
LOY tests below: 
▶ Very Low Tax: 1gp per subject per stronghold round. Grants a +6 
bonus to LOY tests and saves. 
▶ Low Tax: 2gp per subject per stronghold round. Grants a +3 
bonus to LOY tests and saves. 
▶ Standard Tax: 3gp per subject per stronghold round. No change 
to LOY tests and saves. 
▶ High Tax: 4gp per subject per stronghold round. Causes a -3 
penalty to LOY tests and saves. 
▶ Very High Tax: 5gp per subject per stronghold round. Causes a -
6 penalty to LOY tests and saves. 
 
 
Action: Claim Hexes 
The ruler chooses a hex or hexes that are adjacent to a hex already 
claimed by the stronghold. If the domain currently contains zero 
hexes (as the ruler has only claimed the stronghold) then they may 
claim the hex in which the stronghold is to begin their domain.  
 
In one Claim Hexes action, a ruler can only claim a certain number 
of hexes based on the stronghold’s Governance proficiency level, 
as seen below: 
 
NUMBER OF HEXES CLAIMABLE PER ACTION 
GOV 
# of Hexes 
Untrained 
1 
Trained 
2 
Journeyman 
3 
Expert 
5 
Master 
10 
Grandmaster 
15 
 
Maximum Domain Size 
The maximum number of claimed hexes a domain can be made of 
is equal to the level of the ruler. 
 
Population of Hexes 
When a hex is first claimed, it contains a number of subjects based 
on the table below. These are immediately added to the domain 
population: 
 
HEX POPULATION TABLE 
Terrain 
Starting Population 
Hills/Plain/Scrub 
3d6 x 100 
Forest/Mountain 
2d6 x 100 
Barren/Jungle/Swamp 
1d6 x 100 
 
Losing a Hex 
When a hex is lost, perhaps due to an Event or invasion, the 
population of domain is reduced. Roll on the Hex population table 
and remove that number of people from the total population. This 
accounts for people fleeing to other parts of a domain, the influx of 
refugees or the sudden loss of life of subjects. 
 
Claiming a Hex Owned by a Different Stronghold 
Claiming a hex owned by another stronghold is nothing short of a 
declaration of war. To do so, the hex must not contain any active 
soldiery of the opposing stronghold. Then, the ruler must test MIG 
to claim the hex as their own. 
 
Action: Prospect 
The ruler chooses two hexes to prospect for valuable stone, metals 
and other mineable. This can only be done to each hex once.  Roll 
on the Mineable Resource Table below to determine to presence, if 
any, of the resource that can be mined, based on the location of the 
mine. 
 
Mine Value 
If a mineable resource is found, then a mine asset must be purchased 
to exploit it. If this is done, then the following resources provide the 
stronghold with the wealth below: 
▶ Granite, Limestone: 2,000gp per Stronghold round. 
▶ Copper, Tin, Iron: 5,000gp per Stronghold round. 
▶ Silver: 10,000gp per Stronghold round. 
▶ Gold: 25,000gp per Stronghold round. 
▶ Platinum: 50,000gp per Stronghold round. 
▶ Gemstones: 100,000gp per Stronghold round.

Strongholds and Domains 
92 
 
Action: Raise Army 
A character may raise an army from the subjects in the domain. The 
percentage number of subjects in a domain that can be converted 
into novice fighters is determined by the stronghold’s Might, as 
seen on the table below: 
 
RAISE ARMY TABLE 
MIG 
% of Subjects Convertible to Novice Fighters 
Untrained 
0 
Trained 
10% 
Journeyman 
20% 
Expert 
30% 
Master 
40% 
Grandmaster 
50% 
 
While these subjects are acting as fighters, they do not pay tax, but 
do not need to be paid for. 
 
Domain Assets 
Control of a domain allows a ruler access to certain domain assets. 
These work exactly like stronghold assets, with one slight change: 
Building Assets must be assigned to a 6-mile hex. This means the 
ruler must track where in the domain their building assets are. 
Agents, Hirelings and Resources are abstracted as usual, and their 
place does not need to be defined in the domain.  
 
Building – Farmland 
▶ Maximum: 1 per hex. 
▶ Cost: 1,500gp. 
▶ Description: A farm to provide food and wealth to owner’s 
coffers. Must be built on a hills, plains or scrub hex. Only one 
Farmland Asset per 500 population may be built and used within 
the domain. Provides +300gp per stronghold round. 
 
Building – Lumber Camp 
▶ Maximum: 1 per hex. 
▶ Cost: 5,000gp. 
▶ Description: A lumber yard that is used to collect and distribute 
wood across the domain. Must be built in a hex with jungle or forest 
terrain. Provides +1,000gp per stronghold round. 
 
Building - Mine 
▶ Maximum: 1 per hex. 
▶ Cost: 10,000gp. 
▶ Description: A mine to extract resources from the ground. The 
area must have already been prospected and there must be a 
mineable resource there (see domain actions). Once this is built, the 
ruler gains the income from the mineable resource each stronghold 
round.  
 
Building - Road 
▶ Maximum: 1 per hex. 
▶ Cost: 250gp. 
▶ Description: A road to connect each of the area of the domain. If 
half or more of the hexes of the domain contain a Road Asset, then 
the stronghold gains +1 proficiency level in Infrastructure. 
 
 
 
 
Building – Shipyard 
▶ Maximum: 1 per hex. 
▶ Cost: 20,000gp. 
▶ Description: A shipyard for building ships. Must be built in a hex 
adjacent to the sea. With this asset, the stronghold gains +1 
proficiency level Might but can only gain this benefit once with 
multiple shipyards not providing more Might.  
 
A shipyard can build the following ships, at the listed cost. Each ship 
has an ongoing cost associated with it. 
 
SHIPYARD TABLE  
Ship 
Initial Cost 
Ongoing Cost/Stronghold Round 
Cog 
14,000gp 
*special (see below) 
Galleon 
50,000gp 
900gp 
Longship 
14,000gp 
75gp 
 
Cogs act are trading ships and can therefore pay for themselves. For 
each cog owned by a stronghold, roll 2d6 during the income step 
and multiply the result by 100. This is the amount of gold produced 
by the cog. However, if the dice come up doubles, then the cog has 
not produced any money and has in fact lost money. The player 
should multiply the total by 100. This is an amount deducted from 
the total income for that round, as stronghold must cover the cost. 
 
Building – Water Mill 
▶ Maximum: 1 per hex. 
▶ Cost: 3,000gp. 
▶ Description: A watermill for grinding corn. Must be built in a hex 
with a river running through it. Provides +600gp per stronghold 
round. 
 
Building - Stone Tower 
▶ Maximum: 1 per hex. 
▶ Cost: 10,000gp. 
▶ Description: A stone tower. The tower has 24 structure points 
and a 2+ armour save if attacked. 
 
Building - Wooden Tower 
▶ Maximum: 1 per hex. 
▶ Cost: 2,500gp. 
▶ Description: A wooden tower. The tower has 16 structure points 
and a 3+ armour save if attacked. Any magical fire damage the wall 
takes counts as having the siege quality.

Strongholds and Domains 
95 
 
Adamantine Vein 
A vein of Adamantine is discovered, rarest of all metals. The ruler 
may now build one Adamantine Mine and one Adamantine 
Workshop. 
 
Building – Adamantine Mine 
▶ Required Depth Points: 10. 
▶ Maximum: Unlimited. 
▶ Cost: 10,000gp. 
▶ Description: A dwarfen mine to extract adamantine from the 
roots of the world. The mine provides 200,000gp per stronghold 
round as income once constructed. 
 
Resource – Adamantine Workshop 
▶ Required Depth Points: 3. 
▶ Maximum: 1. 
▶ Cost: Special (see below) 
▶ Description: The character sets up a workshop for a team of 
highly secretive dwarfs to craft pieces of adamantine armour for 
them. The workshop costs 20,000gp to set up. The stronghold must 
have one Adamantine vein per Adamantine workshop. For each 
Adamantine workshop acquired, one Adamantine mine has its 
income halved. 
 
Alms Request 
Local peasantry request help and aid in the form of alms.  
 
The ruler must pay 1d6 x 100gp, gaining a +3 bonus to LOY tests 
and saves for 4 stronghold rounds. 
 
Apprentice Induction 
The ruler gains an apprentice wizard as a follower. This apprentice 
gains all the experience that the ruler gains – and the controlling 
player may choose to convert the apprentice into a full character at 
any time. The apprentice has no ongoing costs. 
 
Arcane Assembly 
A great assembly of mages gather in order to discuss how to deal 
with ongoing issues relating to the magical world. 
 
The ruler must test INF. On a success, the stronghold gains a +6 
bonus to all GOV tests and saves for 4 stronghold rounds.  
 
Attack from Below 
The dwarf hold is besieged from within, with the attacking force 
originating from tunnels deeper in the hold. 
 
Roll on the invasion table below to see what has attacked the hold. 
Then roll again to see the size of the force. If the ruler wishes, they 
may test MIG with a success allowing them to reroll the size of the 
Attacking Force. They must accept the second result.

Bad Weather 
Endless bad weather such as weeks of rain or long periods of 
drought begin to take their toll on the running of the stronghold. 
 
The ruler should save vs INF. On a success, the income of the next 
stronghold round is reduced by 2d6%. On a failure, it is reduced by 
2d6+12%. 
 
 
 
Bandits 
A group of bandits set up within the area, harassing locals and 
robbing travellers. The bandits enter the domain in a place chosen 
by the game master. They stay in their group, which consists of 1d10 
x 100 bandits and 1 bandit captain. When in a hex, all assets 
attached to that hex such as shipyards and mines cease to function, 
offering no benefit to the ruler. 
 
Business is Booming 
The business experiences an unsustainable but highly welcome 
period of high investment resulting in a windfall. In the next 
stronghold round the dice type used for the establishment’s wealth 
dice is a d20. 
 
Border Reiving 
Whether paid by foreign rulers or simply the cast-off rogues from 
other realms, outlaws and mercenaries raid the domain’s borders. 
 
For each hex adjacent to another realm roll save vs GOV. For each 
failure, the stronghold loses 2% of its total wealth to robberies and 
arsons.  
 
Crime Does Pay 
Avoiding arrests with almost perfect precision, the guild members 
plunder the city, leaving the wealthy bereft.  
 
In the next Income Step, the value of all wealth dice is multiplied by 
200 instead of 100 to calculate the gold piece income. 
 
Crime Doesn’t Pay 
A spate of arrests, a few poorly conceived heists and soon enough 
the coffers of the guild run dry.  
 
In the next Income Step, the value of all wealth dice is multiplied by 
50 instead of 100 to calculate the gold piece income. 
 
Divine Disfavour 
The stronghold has drawn the ire of its patron deity. The ruler 
automatically loses 50,000 experience points. 
 
Divine Intervention 
The stronghold is favoured by its patron deity. The ruler 
automatically gains 50,000 experience points. 
 
Draconic Rage 
An ancient red dragon has become aware of the sheer volume of 
treasure that the dwarfs have collected and intends to seize it for 
itself. The dragon attacks the dwarf hold.

A sudden change in the economy threatens to damage the business. 
For the next four stronghold rounds the dice type used for the 
establishment’s wealth dice is reduced by one die type. The dice 
types are listed here, from lowest to highest: d4, d6, d8, d10, d12, 
d20.

A sudden change in the economy strengthens the income of the 
business. For the next four stronghold rounds the dice type used for 
the establishment’s wealth dice is increased by one die type. The 
dice types are listed here, from lowest to highest: d4, d6, d8, d10, 
d12, d20.

elemental forces of destruction, accidently opening access to the 
elemental planes.

Famine 
Poor harvests combined with variable administrative conditions have 
led to famine large parts of the domain. 
 
The ruler should save vs GOV for each hex. For each failure, the 
population of the domain goes down by a cumulative 1d4%. A hex 
without a road asset automatically fails.  
 
Festival of Brewmastery 
The hold hosts a great beer festival where the dwarfs can sample all 
kinds of dwarfen ale. The ruler pays 2,000gp and all LOY tests are 
made with a +6 bonus for 1d6+6 stronghold rounds. In addition, 
ruler gains 3 bottles of dwarfen ale that count as Tier 3 potions of 
healing, but only when drunk by dwarf characters, otherwise the 
potions are just incredibly potent alcoholic beverages.  
 
Financial Ruin 
A disaster strikes the business such an economic crisis or the sudden 
change in law that makes business almost impossible. The ruler must 
save vs INF for every single resource and building asset, losing the 
asset on a success. In addition, the wealth of the stronghold is 
reduced by 1d100% instantly.  
 
Fire 
A fire breaks out in the stronghold threatening to destroy large 
swathes of the place. 
 
The game master should roll 1d6. This is the number of building 
assets affected by the fire (the game master chooses which). The 
ruler should save vs INF for each building asset, with the asset 
being destroyed on a failure. 
 
Flood 
Heavy flooding threatens to destroy roads and separate communities 
within the domain. 
 
The ruler should save vs INF for each Road asset, with the asset 
being destroyed on a failure. 
 
From the Rich to the Poor 
Guild members steal 10d6 x 100gp from rich marks. They 
immediately give 5d6 x 100 of it to the poor. The guild ma keep any 
remaining gold (if there is any). Regardless of any money all LOY 
tests are made with a +3 bonus for 4 stronghold rounds. 
 
Gang War 
A rival criminal organisation attempts to muscle in on the guilds’ turf 
and after heated exchanges and perhaps a little violence, a large 
gang war breaks out. 
 
A rival gang will attempt to destroy the guild. The guild should 
immediately save vs Subterfuge for each Beggar King, 
Blackmailer, Court Wizard and Enforcer Master hireling it owns 
with a failure meaning they have been killed as part of hit at the 
start of the gang war.

Strongholds and Domains 
98 
 
Poison 
Somehow the ruler’s enemies manage to get poison into the food or 
drink of the ruler. The ruler must immediately save vs SUB or suffer 
1d8 damage that ignores all armour saves. A successful save means 
that the poison was detected before consumed. On a critical success, 
the poisoner is caught in the act. 
 
The details of the poisoner are left to the game master and could 
include anything from a traitorous court wizard to an unruly 
regiment of warriors finally attempting to seize power.  
 
Sabotage 
Someone or something within the stronghold is trying to undermine 
the ruler and wreck the accumulated assets. 
 
The ruler should save vs SUB for one random resource asset, with 
the asset being destroyed on a failure. On a critical success, the 
saboteur is caught in the act. 
 
Snitch 
Someone in the guild blabs to the watch to save their own skin, 
resulting in the arrest of several guild members. 
 
Choose two thieves assets and make a hard (-3) save vs LOY for 
each. On a failure, all the thieves are arrested, and the guild loses 
the asset. 
 
Stave Construction 
The magicians in the stronghold come together to craft a new 
magical staff, which are both augments of their power and symbols 
of magical office. The ruler immediately gains a magic weapon that 
is a staff. Roll on the Magic Item Table F2 to start the generation of 
this magical staff. All tables are rolled with a +10 bonus when 
generating this staff. 
 
Tax Rebate 
The business is made aware through the official channels that the 
local lords have taken too much gold in taxation. In the next 
stronghold round, the stronghold has an additional 5 wealth dice. 
 
Temporal Displacement 
Due to the placement of the tower on a confluence of leylines, 
certain rooms and areas can suddenly disappear of their own 
volition, spirited away from the tower. 
 
The game master chooses one Resource Asset within the tower and 
the ruler should immediately save vs INF. On a success, the ruler 
may prevent the asset from disappearing by paying 1,000gp to place 
runes of entrenchment and grounding on the asset’s thresholds. On 
a failure, or if the ruler does not wish to pay 1,000gp, then the asset 
is gone forever.  
Tourney 
A great tourney is held at the citadel with wandering warriors from 
across the known world coming to the stronghold to compete in the 
melee and the jousting. 
 
The ruler must save vs MIG. On a success, they gain 10,000gp. On 
a failure, they only gain 2,500gp. 
 
Treachery 
Someone within the stronghold attempts a mutiny, with the intention 
of overthrowing the ruler. Roll 1d100. This is the percentage number 
of maintained fighters that immediately join the mutiny. The ruler 
must save vs LOY, halving the percentage of traitors on a success.  
 
The details of the mutiny are left to the game master and could 
include anything from a traitorous court wizard to an unruly 
regiment of warriors finally attempting to seize power.  
 
 
 
 
 
Troubadour 
A wandering performer (or troupe of performers) arrives at the 
stronghold. The ruler may pay 500gp for a performance to test GOV. 
On a success, all LOY tests are made with a +3 bonus for 1d4 
stronghold rounds.
